# Dark Matter and the Arrow of Time: The Dark Towers (regenerated from the store)

**Abstract.** Transport runs in eight parallel towers, the residues of the Clifford clock. At the dimension nearest four (a flagged premise) two towers are sterile, $r = 4$ with no time and $r = 0$ with two (an imported fork): six can hold matter, five dark; the mirror is populated by indifference, the odd four by inference from the observed remainder. The residue is soldered, not algebraic, so the one metric superselects tower charge: exact stability, given the sector decomposition and the wall displacement. The mirror tower is proved a copy of whatever the Standard-Model paper derives: a second Standard Model, one visible share to half a percent, given one orientation. The orientation bit is the chirality of the pure spinor, its two halves in opposite families only at tower dimension a multiple of four, so the odd residues have no bit and asymmetric dark matter is the mirror tower's. The odd towers' symmetric relic needs a coupling rule the series lacks. One geometric rule, a common mass quantum per unit ball of each tower's space, returns the dark matter ratio as $(11\pi + 16)/(3\pi) = 5.364$ against $5.364 \pm 0.065$ observed, found by fitting, at observation level on three premises; the floor $5.255$ holds for every thickness. The mirror's share is the testable claim, already a quarter above the galactic bound on mirror matter. The same bit is argued to be the arrow of time, given a three-way classification of arrows. Tier C: nine premises and a hypothesis are attacked, refutations costed, every identity script-checked.

## What we assume

**Postulate 1 (D: democracy of the total mass).** Every populated tower carries the same total mass $M_{\mathrm{tot}}$ on its de Sitter sphere. This is a democracy of mass, distinct from an equality of the asymmetry per mode (the former Premise P4, retired as a postulate; the asymmetry is equal in this tower and the mirror by Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(v)), which the odd towers do not carry.

\emph{Note (where D belongs; written on 10 and 11 November 2026, when the fitted total was stated under R1 to R3, and to be read with the note of 19 November below).} D is the premise of the de Sitter reading of the count (Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.democracy-de-sitter-radius-unit-ball}, \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}) and is not counted with R1 to R3: no stored statement of the fitted total with the cell radius as unit has D in its past cone, with one exception, the audit \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}, which sets the two readings side by side (its clause (ii) is under D, its clause (i) under R1 to R3) and so has both among its conditions, and Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule}(iv) has the de Sitter reading replace R2 by D. The two readings cannot be held together with more than one quantum per tower (argued): D with R1 (\claimref{dark-matter.r1-equal-mass-per-quantum}) gives every populated tower the same number of quanta on its de Sitter sphere, and R2 (\claimref{dark-matter.r2-one-quantum-per-unit-ball}) gives a tower of dimension $D$ one quantum per ball of the common cell radius $a$, so by the equality of the ball and sphere forms the number is $(\ell/a)^{D}$ for $D = 2, 3, 4$, and $x^{2} = x^{3} = x^{4}$ holds for $x > 0$ only at $x = 1$: the cell radius is the de Sitter radius and each tower holds one quantum per de Sitter ball. D is also the one postulate of the dark-matter subject that names the de Sitter radius, so it is the one whose content depends on that radius being a constant of the laws: it shares the exposure of $w = -1$. If $w$ differed from $-1$ the unit of D would change with time and the two results under D would go with it; the fitted total, which does not use D, would not.

\emph{Note (2026-11-19).} By the decision of 19 November 2026 the fitted total rests on Premise E, equal mass per unit ball (\claimref{dark-matter.e-equal-mass-per-unit-ball}), in place of R1 and R2. D is E at the de Sitter radius, given the stored reading of the sphere form (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(ii)), and E holds in one length only (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(iv)). So the de Sitter reading is the same law with the de Sitter radius as its length, and it can be held with E only at that length; what was said above of D with R1 and R2 stands.

**Postulate 2 (E: equal mass per unit ball).** \emph{Premise (E), equal mass per unit ball.} There is one length $d$, common to all populated towers, such that every populated tower holds the same mass $\mu$ per ball of radius $d$ of its own space. With $\rho_{r}$ the mass per unit volume of the $D_{r}$-dimensional space of the tower of residue $r$, at its representative under Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p3-nearest-dimension-representatives}, and $V_{D}$ the volume of the unit ball $\B^{D}$,
\[
\rho_{r}\,V_{D_{r}}\,d^{D_{r}} \;=\; \mu \qquad \text{for every populated } r .
\]
The unit ball of the unit-ball rule is the ball of radius $d$.

\emph{What it replaces.} Premises R1 (\claimref{dark-matter.r1-equal-mass-per-quantum}, equal mass per quantum) and R2 (\claimref{dark-matter.r2-one-quantum-per-unit-ball}, one quantum per unit ball), taken in the length $d$, give E with $\mu$ the mass of a quantum, and E gives neither: it says nothing about what a tower's quanta weigh or how many there are (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(i)). R1 and R2 are one model of E, the particle model. By the decision of 19 November 2026, E and not R1 and R2 is the premise of the dark-to-baryon total (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}), with the thickness of Premise R3 (\claimref{dark-matter.r3-one-cell-per-unshared-direction}) read in the same length $d$. R1 and R2 remain premises of the stored results that use a quantum's mass or number by itself.

\emph{What it does not fix.} E does not fix $d$ independently of the towers' densities, which are not observed, and the total does not depend on it: once E and R3 hold, $d$ is $\rho_{1}V_{2}/(\rho_{6}V_{3})$, the density of a three-dimensional tower against ours (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(iii)). In particular $d$ is not tied to the spacing of this world's baryons. That tie belongs to the particle model, R1 and R2 together: E at $d$ with R2 in another common length is consistent, the quanta then having unequal masses. The stored results that give the counted thickness a value, today's baryon spacing of about a metre or its value at the deposit, rest on R1 and R2 for that. The democracy premise (\claimref{dark-matter.d-democracy-total-mass}) is E with $d$ the de Sitter radius, given the stored reading of the sphere form, and the particle model is E with $d$ the common cell radius (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(i), (ii)). E can hold in one length only (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(iv)). It presupposes that the towers it names are populated, which for the four odd towers is Premise \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated}. Whether one length serves at more than one epoch is settled as for R2 (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.unit-ball-clauses-hold-at-one-epoch-unless-hidden-direction-co-expands}(v)): only if the five-dimensional towers' hidden direction co-expands with ours, and otherwise E is a statement about the deposit.

\emph{How much it says.} For the mirror, whose space has the dimension of ours, E is equal density, which the population count gives separately on the proton-only branch (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}; on the burning branch the count gives $0.9948$ and E fails for the mirror by about one part in two hundred, Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(iii)). E with R3 holds for some $d$ exactly when the closed form's shares hold tower by tower and the two thicknesses are equal (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(iii)). So E is the fitted relation stated as one law: it adds no support to the fit that is independent of the fitted number, and the series supplies no mechanism for it.

\emph{Refutation condition.} Observation can refute E with R3 only through the closed form: by a dark-to-baryon ratio that excludes $(11\pi + 16)/3\pi$, or by a composition of the dark matter other than the shares $V_{3}/V_{D}$. E with R3 says more than the total does, that the shares hold tower by tower and that the two thicknesses are equal (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(iii)), and that further content is not observed.

**Postulate 3 (Hypothesis (U): equal rank-one eigenvalues at the ceiling).** \emph{Hypothesis (U)}, that the rank-one eigenvalues of the different sectors coincide at the ceiling, so that the neutrino's Dirac eigenvalue $m_{D}$ there is the common leading-order value, which the top retains and the second-order breaking splits for the down-type and charged-lepton sectors.

**Postulate 4 (Pop: the four odd towers are populated).** \emph{Premise (Pop), the population of the odd towers.} The descent deposited matter in each of the four odd towers: the two three-dimensional towers (residues $1$ and $7$) and the two five-dimensional towers (residues $3$ and $5$), at their representatives under Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p3-nearest-dimension-representatives}.

\emph{It is inferred from the number it helps fit.} Nothing in the series reaches the odd towers' population: Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference} does not equate them with this tower, the series gives them algebras and not contents, and what the descent deposited in each belongs to the pre-descent theory. The only support is the record read inside the framework (Observation \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-are-populated}): with the mirror's share exactly one, empty odd towers would give a dark-to-baryon ratio of $1$ against the observed $5.364 \pm 0.065$, and the framework has no other dark candidate, so the observed remainder $4.364$ is the evidence that they hold matter. That remainder is the same number the unit-ball closed form, which sums over these four towers, is then compared with. The premise therefore adds no support to the fit that is independent of the fitted number.

\emph{Scope.} This tower is populated by observation and the mirror by the copy along the reversal orbit (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}); neither is part of this premise. The premise says that the odd towers hold matter, not how much: the amount is Premises \claimref{dark-matter.e-equal-mass-per-unit-ball} and \claimref{dark-matter.r3-one-cell-per-unshared-direction} (until 19 November 2026, R1 to R3). No result outside the fitted total depends on it: the mirror share, the superselection, the parity theorem and the arrow are the same if the odd towers are empty.

\emph{Refutation condition.} The premise is false if the pre-descent theory, once written, deposits nothing in an odd tower, or if the dark matter is shown to be other than the towers' matter; without it the framework's ratio is $1$.

**Postulate 5 (P1: the single frozen point).** The descent was one event freezing one $I$ and making one orientation choice: all towers couple to one metric and are oriented, where they can be, by the one Witt flag of that event (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}). The orientation clause is used only on the reversal orbit, where the identification of the flag across the two towers is canonical. \emph{Support:} \cite[Thm.~4.7]{ButlerT6}, \cite[Obs.~7.4]{ButlerT12}; the orientation clause is this paper's. Structural premise.

\emph{The three clauses and their status.} The premise is three statements, used separately.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(P1a)] \emph{(Metric.)} One frozen point: all towers couple to one metric. Status: a theorem up to a common normalisation wherever the Weyl tensor has no kernel vector, given that every tower's frozen point is parallel for one tractor connection (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.frozen-point-unique-where-weyl-has-no-kernel}); an independent choice on conformally flat regions, the de Sitter vacuum included (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.two-frozen-points-conformally-flat}). What stays postulated is the one tractor connection, the normalisation, and the clause on conformally flat regions. This is the clause read as the sourcing rule (Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones}), and the one most widely used.
\item[(P1b)] \emph{(Orientation.)} Where towers can be oriented they are oriented by the one Witt flag; used only on the reversal orbit. Status: substitutable. The sign of the mirror's asymmetry is fixed by this clause or by Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference} applied to the orientation bit (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.mirror-asymmetry-route-one}; Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(v)), so under P2 as scoped to labels the clause is redundant on the reading that the bit is a label (argued). The mirror's share is even in that sign, so the dark-to-baryon total needs the clause under neither route (argued).
\item[(P1c)] \emph{(Common $N$.)} The number $N$ is common to the towers. Status: postulated, with no derivation found; it is used by the charge reading of the stored asymmetry (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}; Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(v), first route). Whether a second tower can carry its own frozen-point norm is undecided; the conformally flat counter-model of (P1a) suggests it can (argued).
\end{enumerate}
The clauses are kept in one entity; results that assume P1 use the clause their text names.

\emph{Note (which connection, 2026-11-11).} The ``one tractor connection'' of (P1a) is not named above, and the two theorems cited there are about the normal tractor connection, for which a tractor is parallel in the physical scale only on Einstein regions. Under Premise \claimref{synthesis.field-space-premise-parallel-frozen-point} (FS) the frozen point is parallel, with unit pairing with $X$, for the varied connection $A_{\rho}$ on every field, matter included. For that connection the status of (P1a) is given by Theorem \claimref{synthesis.frozen-point-unique-for-the-field-space-connection}: if every tower's frozen point is parallel for the one $A_{\rho}$ on a connected part of the invertible region that contains one point where $-(\mathcal{R} - \tfrac{\Lambda}{3}\mathrm{id})$ has no kernel vector (any point with isotropic matter other than $w = -\tfrac13$; on Einstein regions the Weyl condition above), the frozen points agree up to constants $c_{r}$, and the independent choice is confined to a component that is de Sitter at that $\Lambda$, not to every conformally flat region. What stays postulated is unchanged: the one connection, and the normalisation $c_{r} = \pm 1$, to which the common $N$ of (P1c) is equivalent given one $G$; (FS) normalises one tractor and does not supply it. For the tower of reversed signature the connection can be shared up to the sign of the frozen point or the discrete element $\operatorname{Ad}(I_{4})$ (the Remark of that Theorem); whether that element bears on the orientation clause (P1b) is not examined. The premise is unchanged.

**Postulate 6 (P2: indifference, scoped to sector labels).** The descent cannot instantiate uncaused distinctions \cite[Principle~5.4]{ButlerT8}, \cite[\S2]{ButlerT13}: it equates along symmetry orbits and never equates inequivalent components. The residues are inequivalent as soldered algebras (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.residue-soldered-invariant-not-algebraic-one}) except along the reversal $r \mapsto -r$ (exchanging $2 \leftrightarrow 6$, $1 \leftrightarrow 7$, $3 \leftrightarrow 5$, fixing $0$, $4$), so P2 equates along that orbit only; which of $2$, $6$ is visible is declared indexical. Structural premise.

\emph{Scope: labels, not states.} What P2 equates along the orbit are the sector labels the descent deposits, the superselection labels of the deposited algebra, such as the tower charge (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}); it does not equate thermal states within a selected sector, so it says nothing about the temperatures of the two members of a reversal pair. The deposited mode set is declared finite. In the boundary-mode model of a reversal pair (Hypothesis (B') of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.tower-charge-sector-label-temperature-not}) this scope is well defined: on every finite window the tower charge is a sector label and the temperature ratio $\xi = \beta/\beta'$ is not, so P2 reaches the stored charge and leaves $\xi$ free; a temperature becomes a label, in the disjointness sense, only on an algebra with infinitely many modes in one frequency band, which the finiteness declaration excludes.

**Postulate 7 (P3: nearest-dimension representatives).** Each tower is represented at the dimension nearest four; where two are equally near (the odd residues, three and five), the lower one with a single time, else the higher. \emph{Support:} P1's one metric read as minimality. Structural premise; Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}(iii) records the other windows. \emph{Note.} Premise (W) of \claimref{synthesis.frozen-point-complement-window-lowest-causal}, that every tower's spacetime is, up to sign, a nondegenerate subspace of the frozen point's complement, is recorded as a costed alternative that restates this premise's window (with the single-clock filter it would replace this premise and the residue-$4$ half of the costed single-ideal clause of Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule}(vi) by one hypothesis; at residue $0$ it admits the causal $(1,1)$ where this premise has $(2,2)$); it was found with the fit known and is not adopted. This premise is unchanged. \emph{Note (by residue).} At the odd residues this premise takes the lowest causal member, $(2,1)$, $(1,2)$ at residues $1$, $7$ and $(4,1)$, $(1,4)$ at residues $3$, $5$. At residues $1$ and $7$ that choice follows from Premise U (\claimref{dark-tower-matter.u-transposition-rule}): the constituent field is $\R$ in every causal member and the stored pair count exceeds the real Witt index $2$ above dimension three, so U admits only $(2,1)$ and $(1,2)$. At residues $3$ and $5$ it is independent of the descent: a model with those towers at $(12,1)$ and $(1,12)$ satisfies every premise of the window-sensitive results other than this one, Premise U included, so there the window is an input (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.bott-shifted-model-leaves-the-odd-towers-dimension-free}(ii), (iii)).

**Postulate 8 (P5: boundary modes hold entropy, not global charge).** The ascended boundary mode space stores and returns information \cite[Thm.~11.5, Cor.~11.7]{ButlerT5}, quantified by its deficit theorem \cite[Thm.~9.3]{ButlerT5}, not global charge: Gauss-law charges are returned per tower by discharge (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-schwinger-discharge-charged-hole}), baryon number is returned by no hole in any tower. This is what the imported flux \cite[Assembly~5.4]{ButlerT7} already implies here: a hole emits baryons only once $T_{\mathrm{H}} > m_{p}$, below $M = M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}/8\pi m_{p} \approx 6 \times 10^{36}$ GeV, returning below $10^{-20}$ of a solar-mass progenitor's baryons. Declared premise, decisive for Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}.

**Postulate 9 (CPT covariance of the kinematic structures and of every action).** \emph{CPT covariance.} CPT conjugation is an isomorphism of every kinematic structure and leaves every action functional invariant (T14, Proposition 5.2, its first premise: ``checked in the proof for the metric, the completion, the pairing and the action; premised for the imported gauge sector and for structures later papers add'').

\emph{The conjugation.} CPT conjugation $\phi$ is the composite of grade involution, reversal and the flag conjugate exchanging the two families of maximal isotropic subspaces (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}). Because of the reversal, $\phi$ is an anti-automorphism of the Clifford algebra.

\emph{Convention (field action).} $\phi$ acts on fields as a symmetry: on a vector field by its vector part, and on a connection $A$ through the Lie algebra automorphism it induces, $A \mapsto -\phi(A)$, so that curvature is carried by that automorphism. In this convention $\phi$ acts on fields as $C = \mathrm{Ad}(I_{6}) \circ \kappa$, with $\kappa$ complex conjugation on $\Cl(2,4) \otimes \C$: its vector part $-1$ is the rotor $I_{6}$ of the identity component, and the flag conjugate that preserves the frozen point's action is complex conjugation, a real-linear one reversing that action (\claimref{synthesis.cpt-conjugation-pseudoscalar-rotor-times-complex-conjugation}). So CPT conjugation is antilinear on the complexified ambient and on the Fock module, of Wigner's antiunitary type (argued there, not proved).

\emph{Action clause.} For every action functional $S_{\mu}$ of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-energy.four-ways-number-enters-theory-shape}, the gauge couplings among its parameters $\mu$ included, $S_{\mu} \circ C = S_{\mu}$; in particular $C$ does not move $\mu$. On the family terms (the Yukawa and mass-matrix terms) the clause is premised in the adjoint reading only: $C$ takes each family term to its adjoint, and does not conjugate the family matrices. In this reading the clause is consistent with a non-zero Jarlskog invariant $J$, and $C$ is a symmetry of the laws that also reverses the arrow (argued, in the Reading of \claimref{synthesis.flag-conjugate-antilinear-on-one-chirality-and-conjugation-reverses-jarlskog}). The other reading, entrywise conjugation of the family matrices followed by a change of family basis, sends $J$ to $-J$ (clause (iii) of that result), so with it the clause would require $J = 0$; it is not premised.

\emph{What is checked and what is premised.} Checked: the fibre metric, the completion, the pairing on the Fock module, and the frozen point's action, which with every functional of real tensor fields invariant under constant $SO_{0}(2,4)$ satisfies the action clause in this convention by the cited result's clause (5). Premised: the imported gauge sector, spinor and fermion fields, and structures later papers add. For the gauge sector the standard CPT theorem for local Lorentz-invariant actions concerns the antiunitary CPT operator of quantum field theory, and this conjugation is not that operator (argued): it fixes every bivector of $\Cl(2,4)$ ($I_{6}$ commutes with the even algebra), so every conformal generator and in particular every translation, and it is antilinear, so on a Hilbert space that carries the translations it reverses their generator and with it the energy, whereas the CPT operator of quantum field theory reverses the translations and keeps the energy positive. The gauge sector's invariance under this conjugation is therefore not supplied by the CPT theorem; it ``is imported and carried as a premise''.

**Postulate 10 (The flag as datum: the sign of the Witt flag is configuration data).** \emph{The flag as datum.} The descent's flag $W$ (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}) is the $+I_7$ eigenspace of an orthogonal complex structure $J$ on $\R^{2,4}$, extended to $\R^{2,4} \oplus I_7\R^{2,4}$, and its conjugate $\overline W$ is the $+I_7$ eigenspace of $-J$; here $I_7 = I_6e$, the central pseudoscalar of the odd extension $\Cl(3,4) = \Cl(2,4)_{\C}$, is the scalar unit of the complexification and of the Fock module (\claimref{std-algebra.complexification-is-the-odd-extension}), and $J$ is a map on vectors, not an element identified with it (\claimref{synthesis.cpt-conjugation-pseudoscalar-rotor-times-complex-conjugation}, clause (2)). The premise: the sign of $J$ relative to the unit $I_7$ of the Fock module is configuration data of each solution, planted by the descent, and not part of the kinematic structure. That is, in the terms of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-energy.four-ways-number-enters-theory-shape}, a configuration $x$ and the configuration with the opposite sign of $J$ have isomorphic kinematic structures and lie in the configuration space of the same family of action functionals $S_{\mu}$; the orientation bit $q(x) = \pm 1$ is a function of the configuration, not of the theory.

\emph{What it is for.} With CPT covariance (\claimref{dark-matter.premise-cpt-covariance}), whose conjugation exchanges the two signs, it places the orientation bit in clause (I) of the Definition, taking both values on every nonempty solution space, rather than in (K) or (D). It is used by \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation} and its restatements \claimref{one-relation.imported-flag-arrow-superselection} and \claimref{one-relation.record-chain-votes-measurement-without-collapse}. Without it the conclusion fails in the way those results state: were the sign kinematic, the bit would be constant on every solution space of the theory carrying it, and the two orientations would be two isomorphic theories rather than two solutions of one.

\emph{What would refute it.} A structure of the framework, or a term of one of its action functionals, that distinguishes $J$ from $-J$ relative to the Fock module's $I_7$ and is fixed by the descent's kinematics rather than chosen by a solution: for example a proof that the pairing on the Fock module, the completion or the frozen point's data carry a preferred sign of $J$. Such a result would make the orientation bit kinematic or dynamically fixed. No observation tests the premise directly; it is a modelling choice about where the bit lives.

**Postulate 11 (R1: equal mass per quantum).** The quanta of every populated tower carry the same mass $m$.

**Postulate 12 (R2: one quantum per unit ball).** A populated tower whose space has $D$ dimensions (under P3, \claimref{dark-matter.p3-nearest-dimension-representatives}, $2$, $3$ or $4$) holds one quantum per unit ball $\B^{D}$ of that space, in one unit of length common to all towers: every tower has the same Wigner-Seitz radius. The rule presupposes that all six towers are populated, which for the odd four is the inference of Observation \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-are-populated}.

**Postulate 13 (R3: one cell per unshared direction).** In the sourcing rule, a tower's matter counts through exactly one unit of length of each direction its neighbour lacks, on one side: a five-dimensional tower's matter through one unit of its extra direction, and a three-dimensional tower's sheets stacked one unit apart along the direction it lacks. the section ``The thickness: a contact mechanism'' gives a mechanism that returns this value and costs it.

\emph{The unit.} The unit is the length $d$ of Premise E (\claimref{dark-matter.e-equal-mass-per-unit-ball}): one length common to all towers, the radius of the ball in which E counts mass, and independent of how a tower's mass is divided into quanta. This is the fixed-common-length reading, adopted by the decision of 19 November 2026; it replaces the reading of 10 November 2026, in which the unit was each tower's own cell radius, the Wigner-Seitz radius of its own filling. Under R1 and R2 (\claimref{dark-matter.r1-equal-mass-per-quantum}, \claimref{dark-matter.r2-one-quantum-per-unit-ball}), E holds in R2's common length, which is then $d$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(iv)), every tower's cell radius is $d$ and the two readings coincide; under R2 alone they coincide only if R2 is taken in the length $d$. They differ when R2 fails: with a tower's own cell radius $a_{r}$ as the unit the shares are $(m_{r}/m_{6})(V_{3}/V_{D_{r}})(a_{6}/a_{r})^{3}$, which Premise E does not fix, and with the common length they are $V_{3}/V_{D_{r}}$ under E alone (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(i)). The title keeps the word cell: the cell is the unit ball of Premise E. The premise takes its unit from E, so where E is not assumed the unit has to be supplied in another way: by R1 with R2, whose common cell radius is then E's length, or by the democracy premise (\claimref{dark-matter.d-democracy-total-mass}), whose length is the de Sitter radius.

**Postulate 14 (SI: a single chiral spinor ideal at residues 0 and 4).** \emph{Premise (SI), the single-ideal clause.} At the reversal-fixed residues $0$ and $4$ a constituent lies in one chiral spinor ideal, and its mass is read through the reversion pairing on that ideal (the pairing reading of mass), not through the total momentum of Premise \claimref{dark-tower-matter.h1-momentum} (the momentum reading). The reversion pairing on one ideal vanishes identically exactly at these residues (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.reversion-pairing-vanishes-residues-zero-four}), so under the premise the towers of residues $0$ and $4$ hold no massive matter and do not enter the dark-to-baryon count.

\emph{Where it comes from.} This is clause (vi) of Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule}, a costed clause of the constituent definition, registered here as a premise in that clause's words. It was chosen knowing that the unit-ball fit needs it: the closed form $(11\pi + 16)/(3\pi)$ sums over the mirror and the four odd towers only.

\emph{What it costs.} With residue $4$ populated the ratio is about $6.16$ (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.lowest-causal-residues-zero-four-content}), and with both chiral ideals admitted at residues $0$ and $4$ about $8.254$, against the observed $5.364 \pm 0.065$.

\emph{Support, and its limits.} By Theorem \claimref{synthesis.invariant-forms-on-spinor-ideal-factor-through-reversion} every spin-invariant bilinear form on a single spinor ideal is a linear functional of the reversion pairing, so at these residues no other pairing could carry a mass term: the premise chooses between the pairing reading and the momentum reading, not among pairings. In the lowest-causal window a single ideal is also what anomaly freedom gives at $(1,1)$, and at $(1,5)$ and $(5,1)$ under the reading (G6) that a tower's own gauge fields live on its full six-dimensional space (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.residues-zero-four-real-chirality-anomaly}); (G6) is recorded as a general reading and was itself chosen knowing that the fit needs residue $4$ empty. Neither supplies the premise: the series does not derive the pairing reading at these residues.

\emph{Refutation condition.} The premise is false if the series' own structure gives matter at residue $0$ or $4$ a mass (the momentum reading there, or a second ideal), in which case the closed form's total moves to the values above; it becomes a theorem if the pairing reading is derived.

**Postulate 15 (The rate model's kinematic inputs: (L), (U), (C), (I)).** \leavevmode
\begin{itemize}
\item[(L)] \emph{Localisation, derived in part.} By \cite[Thm.~4.4, Cor.~4.5, Thms 8.7, 8.12]{ButlerT4} and Step 4a, the completion installs q-number degrees of freedom exactly where division fails, which licenses the window mode space $L(t)$ inside the closure of the degenerate locus; the radiation $R(t)$ is a q-number sector on the model's own grounds, its quanta being the emitted modes carried off $E$ by the emission rather than outputs of the localisation theorems; and that these two exhaust the sectors is the model's exhaustiveness postulate. The total state space at each stage is $H_{L(t)} \otimes H_{R(t)}$.
\item[(U)] \emph{Purity.} The ascended description is globally pure, as in Fact \claimref{singularity.assembled-page-curve-up-typicality}(i), each step of the evolution unitary.
\item[(C)] \emph{Capacity.} $\dim H_{L(t)} = 2^{|W(t)|}$ exactly, the number $|W(t)|$ of window modes filling to the mode count $N_{\mathrm{6a}}$ of Step 6a and non-increasing thereafter; the Page reading $C(t) \approx S_{\mathrm{BH}}(M(t))$ is for the full window.
\item[(I)] \emph{Injection.} The $k$-th emitted quantum enters in a thermofield pair $c_k|00\rangle + s_k|11\rangle$ with a fresh window mode, the pair form and the marginals from Theorem \claimref{singularity.modular-descent} and Corollary \claimref{singularity.chains-state-class-stability}; its \emph{freshness}, the clause that the pair arrives uncorrelated with everything already present, comes from neither cited result (Corollary \claimref{singularity.chains-state-class-stability}(3) leaves exactly such correlations free) and is an independent assumption of the rate model, marked as such precisely as (L$_E$)'s never-return clause is. The quantum carries the marginal entropy $h_k := H(c_k^{2}) \leq \ln 2$, with $H(p) := -p\ln p - (1-p)\ln(1-p)$ the binary entropy in nats and equality exactly at the maximally entangled pair $c_k = s_k$; the thermal accumulation is $\Sth(t) := \sum_{k \leq t} h_k$.
\end{itemize}

**Postulate 16 (Premise: the common ceiling coupling of the deposited dynamics).** \emph{Premise (common ceiling coupling).} The two towers of the reversal orbit, this one ($r = 6$) and the mirror ($r = 2$), share one ceiling $M$, and the parameters of their deposited dynamics at that ceiling are equal: the gauge coupling of each factor of the copied gauge algebra, $\alpha_{2}(M) = \alpha_{6}(M)$, and every other coupling of the copied theory at $M$. With the copy of content (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(i)), which gives the two towers the same $\beta$-functions in every scheme, the two theories at $M$ are then isomorphic, so $\Lambda_{2} = \Lambda_{6}$ and $c_{2} = c_{6}$ (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(iii)).

\emph{Scope.} The premise equates parameters of the dynamics, which are neither the sector labels Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference} equates at its version 2 (labels, not states) nor states: P2 so scoped reaches the stored charge and not the ceiling coupling (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.tower-charge-sector-label-temperature-not}, Reading). It equates no state and no deposited energy, so it leaves the mirror temperature ratio $\xi$ free and does not give the $\xi = 1$ of Proposition \claimref{dark-tower-matter.mirrors-temperature-p2}. It acts along the reversal orbit $2 \leftrightarrow 6$ only: it says nothing of the odd towers or of the two-time fork, for which no coupling rule follows (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}).

\emph{What it costs.} One equality between two continuous parameters of two sectors (one per coupling of the copied theory), taken as given and derived by no result of the series; since no coupling value is derived either (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}(b)), it ties two undetermined numbers together without fixing either. On it rest the mass factor $m_{2}/m_{6} = 1$ up to the two sectors' composition, and with it the mirror's share $f_{2} = 1$, $\Omega_{\mathrm{mirror}} = \Omega_{b}$, one of the observed $5.364$ units. Without it the share is a free function of the coupling difference: with identical content a difference $\delta = \alpha_{2}(M)^{-1} - \alpha_{6}(M)^{-1}$ moves $\Lambda_{2}/\Lambda_{6}$ by $e^{-2\pi\delta/b}$, and the share stays within ten percent of one only for $|\delta| < 0.106$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-one-how-fragile}(ii)). It adds one premise to the register: the share of one holds given P1, P2 (for the asymmetry ratio), this premise (for the mass ratio) and the copy.

\emph{Refutation condition.} The premise is false if the series' dynamics, or another premise of the register, gives the two towers different ceilings or different couplings at the ceiling. It becomes a theorem, and is retired as a premise, if the equality is derived, for example from a symmetry of the descent that acts on the parameters of the dynamics along the reversal orbit.

## Introduction



**setting  (Introduction).** The tower paper of this series proves that the (1,1) ascent preserves the signature residue $p - q$ modulo eight, so the transport construction runs in eight parallel towers, one per residue of the real Clifford clock \cite{ButlerT3}. Applied to the matter that gravitates and does not shine, the towers give the mirror's share of it by theorem, prove that the other four shares cannot be derived from the series as it stands, and then, by one geometric rule on three premises of its own, return the whole ratio as a closed form, $(11\pi + 16)/(3\pi) = 5.364$, found by fitting. The paper's level is Tier C: nine premises and a hypothesis, each stated before use, attacked in its own section and carried with its cost, and a falsifiability section that says how each claim would be refuted.

The chain, in outline. A residue fixes no signature, so one must be chosen for each; at the dimension nearest four (Premise P3, with the census of what other choices give) a residue has a single time when the chosen signature has exactly one timelike direction, and two residues fail, $r = 0$ with two times and $r = 4$ with none, the fixed points of the mirror map. Six towers can hold matter, this one and five dark; that they do is a theorem for the mirror (indifference along the reversal orbit) and an inference from the observed remainder for the odd four, since the framework has no other dark candidate (Observation \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-are-populated}). The flag does not refine the sort (Witt's theorem), so the $r = 0$ exclusion rests on a two-time import alone, carried as a fork. The five are dark by superselection: the residue is an invariant of the algebra soldered to the metric, not of the algebra alone ($\Cl(1,3)$ and the sterile tower's $\Cl(0,4)$ are the same algebra, $\Mat(2, \mathbb{H})$), so no operator built from the one metric changes residue, and given the sector decomposition the towers are exactly stable, touching this one only by weight. The descent's one orientation, the flag bit, is the chirality of the pure spinor, and its two halves lie in opposite families only when the tower's dimension is a multiple of four: the odd residues cannot be oriented, the even ones can at their P3 representatives, and the asymmetric dark matter is the mirror tower's alone.

Of the five dark towers this paper derives the contribution of one, the mirror's, by theorem, and gives the other four by a rule:
\begin{gather*}
\frac{\Omega_{\mathrm{dark}}}{\Omega_{b}} \;=\; \underbrace{1}_{\text{mirror, derived, to half a percent}} \;+\; \underbrace{2 \cdot \frac{4}{3} + 2 \cdot \frac{8}{3\pi}}_{\text{four odd towers, the unit-ball rule, fitted}} \;=\; \frac{11\pi + 16}{3\pi} \;=\; 5.364,\\[6pt]
\text{against the survey's}\quad
\frac{\Omega_{c}}{\Omega_{b}} \;=\; 5.364 \pm 0.065.
\end{gather*}
The first term is a theorem (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-assembled}). Everything the Standard-Model paper builds this tower's content from is invariant under the signature reversal that carries $(1,3)$ to the mirror's $(3,1)$, so the mirror tower is a second Standard Model, a massless mirror photon and mirror neutrinos included; with P2 its share is one to half a percent (the half percent is the mean mass per baryon of a helium mirror), at any mirror temperature, because the stored asymmetry is a charge of the descent and not a thermal number. The share is exact and fragile (one flavour's difference at the ceiling would move it by a factor between $4.89$ and $21.4$), and it is already at the edge of the record's bound on dissipative dark matter.

The second term is not derived: the odd towers carry no asymmetry (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}), and a symmetric relic's density needs a coupling rule the series does not supply (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}). What the paper offers instead is the unit-ball rule of the section ``The dark matter ratio: the unit-ball rule'': every populated tower holds one quantum of a common mass per unit ball of its own space, in one common unit, and its matter enters the one metric per unit of our volume through one cell radius of each direction its neighbour lacks. A tower's share is then the ratio of unit-ball volumes $V_{3}/V_{D}$: $\tfrac{4}{3}$ for each three-dimensional tower, $\tfrac{8}{3\pi}$ for each five-dimensional one.

The rule rests on three premises the series does not supply, and it was found by fitting. Its one length, the thickness of the direction we cannot see, is a premise; a contact mechanism returns its value from four clauses on how cells meet a world, each with an alternative the observation excludes, and that mechanism explains the value without deriving it. With the thickness left as a parameter the total has a floor at $5.255$, the family's one prediction independent of it, and meets the observed value at one unit and at $\tfrac{\pi}{2}$ units, the chord and the arc of a quarter turn of the nested spheres. The series' one length, the de Sitter radius, makes the spheres physical, but its reductions give totals of $5$, $5.67$ or $4.33$; the inverse-square law shows the thickness is not a gravitational quantity; and the three-dimensional towers' sheets, which momentum conservation displaces with the matter, respond to gravity in full, so that a Boltzmann computation returns the standard model's linear spectra for the rule's composition. All of this is at observation level with each cost stated (the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress}), and it changes nothing derived.

The rest of the paper: a projection lemma makes every tower's content dilute as this tower's does; the radiation audit closes the three-dimensional towers by theorem plus Polyakov and leaves the mirror (whose cost is a seeded temperature below $0.448$ of ours) and the five-dimensional towers as open channels; the orientation is shown to be the arrow itself (the seventeenth refutation); infall through this tower's horizons is routed through parallel boundary mode spaces at one wall; and the rung scale is valued conditionally at $3.5 \times 10^{11}$ to $6 \times 10^{14}$ GeV. The exposures are five species, gravity-only coupling, composite self-interaction in the three-dimensional towers, which the galaxy-cluster simulations exclude unless their matter is collisionless, dissipative mirror matter against the dark-disk and collisional-fraction bounds, dark neutron-star mergers with our tidal deformability and no counterpart, permanent direct-detection nulls, and a dimmed final burst for evaporating black holes; the section ``The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper'' collects every prediction in one table.

**setting  (Prior art).** The mod-8 periodicity of the real Clifford algebras is Bott's and Cartan's, imported through the taxonomy and tower papers \cite{ButlerT1, ButlerT3}. Hidden-sector and mirror-matter dark matter has a long literature \cite{FootMirror2004}; asymmetric dark matter, with its signature GeV-scale mass from a shared asymmetry, likewise \cite{Zurek2014}; the framework differs in deriving the sector count, the stability and the asymmetry's confinement to the oriented towers from one structure rather than positing them, and its exposure is the mirror share, mirror matter in Foot's sense. Confinement of compact three-dimensional electrodynamics is Polyakov's \cite{Polyakov1977}. The observational inputs (the $\Omega_{c}/\Omega_{b}$ ratio, the $\Neff$ cap, cluster-merger bounds on self-interaction) are the survey record's \cite{Planck2018, Clowe2006}, refreshed at submission. What this paper claims as new is listed under its contributions.

**setting  (Contribution).** (1) Towers as residues, the single-clock filter under the nearest-dimension premise with its census, the Witt-theorem observation that the flag cannot refine the count, the spinor type by residue, and the residue as a soldered invariant (the section ``Towers, residues, and the flag filter''). (2) Dark and exactly stable by superselection, given the sector decomposition; the flag bit at tower dimension $0 \bmod 4$ and which towers can carry the arrow; the mode counts; the mirror tower a copy of this one, its share of one to half a percent the one derived share of the ratio; the asymmetry as a charge (the section ``Towers, residues, and the flag filter'', the section ``Dark, stable, counted''). (3) The asymmetry as the arrow counted, and the orientation as the arrow, both refutations carried as results (the section ``The arrow counted''). (4) The projection lemma, the radiation audit with its two open channels, the infall computation, and the valued ceiling (the section ``The audits''). (5) The unit-ball rule for the remainder: the odd towers' shares as ratios of unit-ball volumes, the closed form $(11\pi + 16)/(3\pi)$, a contact mechanism that returns its one length on four costed clauses, the one-parameter family as sensitivity with the floor it predicts, the de Sitter reading that fixes the unit but not the thickness, and the rule's consistency with locality, the inverse-square law and, by a recorded Boltzmann computation, the linear CMB and matter power spectra, all at observation level with the costs stated (the section ``The dark matter ratio: the unit-ball rule''). (6) The premises attacked, every claim stated with its cost, and every prediction collected with its level and test (the section ``The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper''). (7) Machine verification of every computational identity in exact arithmetic, including the census that could have falsified the sort \cite{ButlerVerifyT14}.

## Setting, premises, and imports



**setting  (Setting, premises, and imports).** Five premises carry the derived content: P1 and P2 are the series' own, P3 and P4 this paper's, P5 decides the infall computation. Each is attacked and costed in the section ``The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper''; the unit-ball rule's premises are stated in the section ``The dark matter ratio: the unit-ball rule''.



**Theorem 17 (Imported: the eight towers and the mod-8 clock).** The (1,1) ascent preserves $p - q$ and $\Cl(p+k, q+k) \cong \Mat(2^{k}, \Cl(p,q))$ (Bott), so the eight residues label eight parallel towers. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[Thms.~3.2, 3.6, Rem.~3.7]{ButlerT3}, \cite[\S7]{ButlerT1}.

*Proof.* The (1,1) ascent preserves $p - q$ and $\Cl(p+k, q+k) \cong \Mat(2^{k}, \Cl(p,q))$ (Bott), so the eight residues label eight parallel towers. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[Thms.~3.2, 3.6, Rem.~3.7]{ButlerT3} (\claimref{tower.rigidity-clock-frozen}, \claimref{tower.commuting-copies}), \cite[\S7]{ButlerT1}.




**Theorem 18 (Gravity-only coupling at first order, restated from the acoustic no-go).** Under the entropy paper's Indifference premise (for the sphere step of the four-dimensional lift) and, wherever the thermal state is used, the singularity paper's premise (Q), and with the other hypotheses the acoustic chain carries: within the s-wave current algebra and its assembled four-dimensional lift, every gauge-charged or charge-conjugation-odd probe has zero linear overlap with the graviton mode, so at first order only the charge-conjugation-even neutral sector couples; in the conformal vacuum its overlap is carried by $T$ alone, and Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.conclusion-does-not-depend-ontology-level} reads it as the one coupling sector, stress-energy (Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.acoustic-no-go-one-way-grip}, Assembly \claimref{dark-energy.lift-inherits-no-go-steps-itemised}). Hence matter that gravitates on this metric and carries no gauge charge shared with us acts on us, through the metric-forming correlations, by its stress-energy alone, at first order. With Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point}, read operationally as the sector decomposition of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.frozen-phase-operators}, which makes these correlations its only contact with us, it does nothing else observable at first order. \emph{Provenance:} the series' acoustic no-go \cite[\S5]{ButlerT10} restated, at its mixed level.

*Proof.* Restatement of the series' own result; no new step. Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.acoustic-no-go-one-way-grip} shows, for any state invariant under the gauge group, charge conjugation and the modular flow, that a gauge-charged or charge-conjugation-odd probe $O$ has $\varphi(T\,O) = 0$ for the graviton mode $T$ and all its modes $T_{n}$; the induced thermal state has the three invariances under premise (Q), and the conformal vacuum has them without it. At first order, therefore, only the charge-conjugation-even neutral subalgebra couples. In the conformal vacuum the orthogonality of quasi-primaries of unequal dimension leaves $T$ as the only quasi-primary with nonzero overlap, so the coupling is carried by $T$ alone; in the thermal state the first-order coupling sector is the whole charge-conjugation-even neutral subalgebra, generated by the current bilinears and strictly larger than the conformal family of $T$, as that proposition states. Assembly \claimref{dark-energy.lift-inherits-no-go-steps-itemised} carries the orthogonality to the four-dimensional lift, its sphere step conditional on the Indifference premise and its near-horizon reduction at assembly level. Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.conclusion-does-not-depend-ontology-level} reads the result as one coupling sector, stress-energy, with no selective coupling, no enhanced source and no enhanced detector, at first order; the statement adopts that reading at the source's mixed level.

Matter of a sector that shares no gauge charge with ours enters our sector's observables through the metric-forming correlations by the linear overlap of its operators with ours on those correlations; by the three results the first-order overlap with the graviton mode is that of the sector just described, so this matter acts on us through these correlations by its stress-energy alone, at first order. Whether the sector touches ours by any other route is not decided by the acoustic chain: "carries no gauge charge shared with us" does not by itself exclude, for example, a gauge-singlet portal. That the metric-forming correlations are its only contact is the sector decomposition of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.frozen-phase-operators}, the operational reading of Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point}, assumed here, and the closing clause of the statement is conditional on it; P1 also enters the dark-matter paper's superselection theorem as an assumption there. Every premise of the three cited results is a premise of this one, and reaches its dependants through them.




**Theorem 19 (Imported: the trichotomy and the descent's data).** \cite[Prop.~7.6]{ButlerT12}: (1) \emph{kinematic}: the rescaling covariance fixes every discrete invariant (proved), and no construction from the conformal class and pair structure alone fixes $N$ (observation level); (2) \emph{non-dynamical}: the norm of the frozen point is not a field, being fixed by a constraint with pure-gauge fluctuations and no equation of motion (imported); (3) \emph{conditional}: if kinematic, dynamical and initial-data are exhaustive (not proved), $N$ is initial data, located at the origin at observation level; (4) \emph{parameter of the laws} (imported): the three are not exhaustive, since under the action as written $N$ is constant on every solution and varies across $\Lambda$, a parameter of the laws \cite[Prop.~5.8]{ButlerT10}, so that (3) holds only if the frozen point's norm is promoted to the value of a Lagrange-multiplier field, a decision that paper records open. Wherever this paper calls a datum initial data of the descent ($\epsilon$, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation}, clause (1) only, as a stance; the orientation bit, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation}, which as corrected cites none of these clauses and in particular not clause (3): it reaches clause (I) of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-energy.four-ways-number-enters-theory-shape} directly from CPT covariance, with no exhaustiveness), these clauses are premises of the sentence, and clause (4) names the alternative for each, a parameter of the laws rather than data of the event: for the bit that would require the laws to distinguish the two orientations, for which the CPT covariance of the structures Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation} lists leaves no room within them, so the bit is data if it is anything; for $\epsilon$ the paper takes no position.

*Proof.* \cite[Prop.~7.6]{ButlerT12} (\claimref{standard-model.trichotomy-what-kind-number}): (1) \emph{kinematic}: the rescaling covariance fixes every discrete invariant (proved), and no construction from the conformal class and pair structure alone fixes $N$ (observation level); (2) \emph{non-dynamical}: the norm of the frozen point is not a field, being fixed by a constraint with pure-gauge fluctuations and no equation of motion (imported); (3) \emph{conditional}: if kinematic, dynamical and initial-data are exhaustive (not proved), $N$ is initial data, located at the origin at observation level; (4) \emph{parameter of the laws} (imported): the three are not exhaustive, since under the action as written $N$ is constant on every solution and varies across $\Lambda$, a parameter of the laws \cite[Prop.~5.8]{ButlerT10} (\claimref{dark-energy.under-action-written-norm-parameter-laws}), so that (3) holds only if the frozen point's norm is promoted to the value of a Lagrange-multiplier field, a decision that paper records open. Wherever this paper calls a datum initial data of the descent ($\epsilon$, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation}, clause (1) only, as a stance; the orientation bit, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation}, which as corrected cites none of these clauses and in particular not clause (3): it reaches clause (I) of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-energy.four-ways-number-enters-theory-shape} directly from CPT covariance, with no exhaustiveness), these clauses are premises of the sentence, and clause (4) names the alternative for each, a parameter of the laws rather than data of the event: for the bit that would require the laws to distinguish the two orientations, for which the CPT covariance of the structures Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation} lists leaves no room within them, so the bit is data if it is anything; for $\epsilon$ the paper takes no position.




**Theorem 20 (Imported: the derived generation and the flag).** The visible matter is the derived Standard Model generation \cite{ButlerT12}; the three pairs of the Fock structure of a conformal spinor (the twistor Fock structure) are a Witt decomposition of the complexified conformal ambient \cite[Thm.~5.3]{ButlerT9}, two realised by real hyperbolic planes of $\R^{2,4}$, the third only in $V_{\C}$ \cite[proof of Thm.~3.1]{ButlerT9}; the one-handed listing choice on it is the particle-antiparticle orientation \cite[Prop.~5.6]{ButlerT12}, under that paper's Hypothesis (S), carried here; the condensate's alignment is \cite[Prop.~4.3]{ButlerT13}. Reading the listing choice as the descent's flag is this paper's (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}).

*Proof.* The visible matter is the derived Standard Model generation \cite{ButlerT12}; the three pairs of the Fock structure of a conformal spinor are a Witt decomposition of the complexified conformal ambient \cite[Thm.~5.3]{ButlerT9} (\claimref{correspondence.twistors-are-states-three-ladder-pairs}), two realised by real hyperbolic planes of $\R^{2,4}$, the third only in $V_{\C}$ \cite[proof of Thm.~3.1]{ButlerT9} (\claimref{correspondence.fock-model-complexified-ascended-algebra}); the one-handed listing choice on it is the particle-antiparticle orientation \cite[Prop.~5.6]{ButlerT12} (\claimref{standard-model.chirality-two-halves-spinor-module-conjugation}), under that paper's Hypothesis (S) (\claimref{standard-model.hyp-s-slot-statistics}), carried here; the condensate's alignment is \cite[Prop.~4.3]{ButlerT13} (\claimref{families.alignment-computed-thirteenth-refutation-democracy-one}). Reading the listing choice as the descent's flag is this paper's (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}).




**Definition 21 (The Witt flag).** On a rung's hyperbolic plane $H_k$ with null pair $(v_k, w_k)$, a \emph{flag} chooses one null line as the annihilated half; $v_k \leftrightarrow w_k$ exchanges the two. The \emph{descent's flag} chooses for every pair at once: a maximal isotropic $W$ of the complexified conformal ambient, the Fock vacuum's annihilator (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.null-blades-fock-ideal-chirality}), on the three-pair structure of Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-derived-generation-flag}. Its conjugate exchanges all three halves, an odd number, which puts it in the other family (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-families-are-two-chiralities}(iii)) and makes the flag a bit (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}). A flag is an orientation datum, not a plane.



**Fact (imported) 22 (Imported: Schwinger discharge of a charged hole).** A charged hole whose horizon field exceeds $E_{c} = m_{e}^{2}c^{3}/e\hbar$ discharges by pair creation, faster than it evaporates, toward $Q_{c} = 4\pi\varepsilon_{0}E_{c}r_{+}^{2}$, into the lightest charged carrier (for the mirror, the mirror electron). $Q_{c}/Q_{\mathrm{ext}} \propto M$ reaches one at about $1.3 \times 10^{5}$ solar masses (at one solar mass $Q_{c} \approx 1.3 \times 10^{15}$ C, $7.5 \times 10^{-6}$ of extremal, with $E_{c} = 1.32 \times 10^{18}$ V/m), so below that mass discharge returns the Gauss-law charge and above it does not limit it. \emph{Provenance:} \cite{Gibbons1975, HiscockWeems1990}, from the record (the charged exterior is outside the Hawking paper's scope).

*Provenance:* G. W. Gibbons, Vacuum polarization and the spontaneous loss of charge by black holes, Commun. Math. Phys. 44 (1975) 245--264.; W. A. Hiscock and L. D. Weems, Evolution of charged evaporating black holes, Phys. Rev. D 41 (1990) 1142--1151..

## Towers, residues, and the flag filter





**Theorem 23 (Towers are residues, and the single-clock filter).** The residue $r = p - q \pmod 8$ is invariant along every tower: the eight parallel towers are the eight residues, their parallelism derived rather than described. Call a signature $(p,q)$ \emph{causal} if $\min(p,q) = 1$: exactly one direction of the minority sign, the timelike one, whichever sign convention carries time. Labelling this world $(1,3)$, residue $6$, fixes the convention time $=$ plus, and the mirror residue $2$ reads $(3,1)$; under the opposite convention the two labels swap and the causal sort is unchanged, but every attribution of a Clifford type to a member of the reversal pair passes to the other member, since $\Cl(1,3) \cong \Mat(2,\mathbb{H})$ and $\Cl(3,1) \cong \Mat(4,\R)$ are not isomorphic. That attribution is fixed by a labelling convention Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference} declares indexical, and is so read below. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(Census; proved.)} Every residue contains causal signatures once the dimension is free: the lowest are $(1,1)$ at $r = 0$, $(2,1)$ at $r = 1$, $(3,1)$ at $r = 2$, $(4,1)$ at $r = 3$, $(1,5)$ or $(5,1)$ at $r = 4$, $(1,4)$ at $r = 5$, $(1,3)$ at $r = 6$, $(1,2)$ at $r = 7$. The residue alone therefore sorts nothing; a representative must be chosen.
\item[(ii)] \emph{(The sort under P3; proved given the premise.)} Under Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p3-nearest-dimension-representatives} the representatives are $(1,3)$ at $r = 6$, this world; $(3,1)$ at $r = 2$, a mirror Minkowski with real (Majorana-type) spinors; $(2,2)$ at $r = 0$, two times; $(4,0)$ or $(0,4)$ at $r = 4$, none; $(2,1)$ and $(1,2)$ at $r = 1, 7$, three-dimensional; $(4,1)$ and $(1,4)$ at $r = 3, 5$, five-dimensional. Exactly two are sterile, $r = 0$ and $r = 4$ (a figure of the original), and they are the fixed points of the mirror map $r \mapsto -r$: under P3 the towers without a single time are the towers that are their own mirrors.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(Sensitivity; proved.)} The sterile set is a function of the window. With any dimension up to five allowed, only $r = 4$ is sterile ($(1,1)$ is a causal signature for $r = 0$); with dimensions three to six, only $r = 0$ ($(1,5)$ is one for $r = 4$); with the upper bound removed, nothing is ($(1,9)$ is one for $r = 0$ at ten); and the literal lowest-dimensional representative of every residue, with no lower bound, is definite for $r = 6$ itself, namely $(0,2)$: without a lower bound the criterion would sterilise this world.
\end{enumerate}
Six admit matter: this one and five dark. Which of the six the descent populated is a further question, answered for the mirror by P2 and for the odd towers only by the record (Observation \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-are-populated}). Structural level given P3; the two-time exclusion at $r = 0$ imports the standard instability of two-time dynamics, flagged, and the flag does not remove it (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.flag-insensitive-residue-no-flag-level}).

*Proof.* Invariance: the ascent adjoins one plus and one minus, fixing $p - q$; periodicity is Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-eight-towers-mod-8-clock}. (i): $(1,s)$ has residue $1 - s$, so every residue has a causal signature; the lowest per residue is the script's exhaustive census over all signatures of dimension at most ten. (ii): P3 (\claimref{dark-matter.p3-nearest-dimension-representatives}) fixes the dimension at four for the even residues, where the signatures are $(2,2)$, $(3,1)$, $(1,3)$, $(4,0)$, $(0,4)$ with residues $0, 2, 6, 4, 4$, of which the first has $\min = 2$ and the last two $\min = 0$; for the odd residues the candidates at dimension three are $(2,1)$, $(1,2)$, $(3,0)$, $(0,3)$ with residues $1, 7, 3, 5$, the first two causal and the last two definite, so P3 sends $r = 3, 5$ to dimension five, where $(4,1)$ and $(1,4)$ have residues $3, 5$ and are causal. The fixed points of $r \mapsto -r \pmod 8$ are $\{0, 4\}$, arithmetic. (iii) is the same census restricted to each window, script-run. The count: eight minus two sterile is six populated; minus this one is five dark.

The labelling convention used throughout, this world as $(1,3)$ at residue $6$, is read as indexical under \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference}, which declares which of $2$, $6$ is visible indexical.




**Theorem 24 (The flag is insensitive to the residue: no flag-level strengthening).** The sterile towers are not the unflaggable ones, and no flag-level strengthening of the count exists: in every nondefinite representative the isometry group acts transitively on hyperbolic planes and on the null lines within them (Witt's theorem), so no representative offers a distinguished plane or half of one, and the descent's flag is as uncaused in $(1,3)$ as in $(2,2)$. The definite representatives of $r = 4$ contain no hyperbolic plane, so no \emph{rung-level} flag can be chosen in them, but the descent's flag, a maximal isotropic of the complexified ambient, exists there as for every even residue (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}); the sterility of $r = 4$ is the single-clock filter restated. So the exclusion of $r = 0$ rests on the two-time instability import alone, and a stabilisation of two-time dynamics is an open fork: six causal towers, the $r = 0$ tower an \emph{oriented} one (Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-can-carry-arrow}), a Kleinian copy of the Standard Model with an asymmetric share of one, costed in the section ``The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper''.

*Proof.* A hyperbolic plane requires one plus and one minus among the representative's directions, and a definite signature has none. Otherwise Witt's theorem (\claimref{std-algebra.witt-extension}) extends any isometry between nondegenerate subspaces to the whole space; two hyperbolic planes are isometric, so the isometry group is transitive on them, and composing with the isometry of a plane that exchanges its null lines gives transitivity on null lines. In $(1,3)$ the rotation $e_{1} \mapsto e_{2}$, $e_{2} \mapsto -e_{1}$ fixes $e_{0}$ and its orientation and carries $\mathrm{span}(e_{0}, e_{1})$ onto $\mathrm{span}(e_{0}, e_{2})$ and $\R(e_{0} + e_{1})$ onto $\R(e_{0} + e_{2})$: the time orientation distinguishes a time direction, not a plane or a null line, the candidate flags through a time axis forming the celestial sphere. In $(2,2)$ the totally isotropic planes form a one-parameter family per component and the hyperbolic planes a four-parameter family, likewise permuted transitively. The script checks the witnesses and the Jacobian rank exactly. The obstruction is the same in every nondefinite residue, so it cannot sterilise $r = 0$ while leaving $r = 6$ causal.




**Definition 25 (Spinor module and type).** For a representative $\Cl(p,q)$ let $S$ be an irreducible real module (a real spinor space), and let $D = \End_{\Cl(p,q)}(S)$ be its commutant, a real division algebra by Schur's lemma. Its \emph{type} is $D \in \{\R, \C, \mathbb{H}\}$ together with the number of inequivalent irreducible modules (one or two). The \emph{intrinsic group} of $S$ is the group of $D$-unitary automorphisms of $S$ commuting with the Clifford action, $U(1, D) = \{d \in D : d\bar{d} = 1\}$: $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$, $U(1)$ or $Sp(1)$.



**Theorem 26 (Type by residue, and what each tower can carry intrinsically).** The type is a function of the residue alone: $D = \R$ with one irreducible module at $r = 0, 2$ and two at $r = 1$; $D = \C$ with one at $r = 3, 7$; $D = \mathbb{H}$ with one at $r = 4, 6$ and two at $r = 5$. Consequently, for the P3 representatives, in the labelling convention of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter} (under the opposite one every attribution to a named member of a pair $\{r, -r\}$ passes to the other):
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] the mirror tower $r = 2$ has real (Majorana) spinors with intrinsic group $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$: no continuous charge commutes with its ground Clifford action, and in particular no $U(1)$ under which its ground spinors are charged; the fork tower $r = 0$, also real with one module, has the same intrinsic group $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$;
\item[(ii)] the residues $r = 3$ (five-dimensional) and $r = 7$ (three-dimensional) have complex spinors with intrinsic group exactly $U(1)$, a distinguished complex structure and hence exactly one intrinsic abelian charge each;
\item[(iii)] this world $r = 6$, and $r = 4, 5$, have quaternionic spinors with intrinsic group $Sp(1)$: a two-sphere of complex structures and no distinguished $U(1)$;
\item[(iv)] the odd residues $r = 1, 5$ have two inequivalent irreducible modules, distinguished by the sign of the central volume element, and $r = 3, 7$ one, the volume element acting as the complex unit.
\end{enumerate}
The intrinsic group is a lower bound on a tower's internal symmetry, not the whole of it: the rungs adjoin ladder pairs whose flags are complex structures (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.where-flag-defined-what-it-carries}), and the derived content of this tower's $U(1)$ is a rung-level object \cite{ButlerT12}. What the theorem fixes is what the ground algebra carries by itself.

*Proof.* $\Cl(p,q)$ is semisimple, so it is $\Mat(m, D)$ or $\Mat(m, D) \oplus \Mat(m, D)$, and the commutant of an irreducible module is $D$. The centre is $\R$ when $n = p + q$ is even and $\R[\omega]$ when $n$ is odd, $\omega = e_{1} \cdots e_{n}$ the volume element, with $\omega^{2} = (-1)^{n(n-1)/2}(-1)^{q}$; so two components ($\omega^{2} = +1$) or a complex centre ($\omega^{2} = -1$) according to $n$ and $q$, which depend on $r$ as stated. For the division algebra, Lounesto's construction gives a primitive idempotent
\[
f \;=\; \prod_{i=1}^{k}\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(1 + e_{T_{i}}\bigr)
\]
from a maximal set of $k$ commuting basis blades squaring to $+1$ generating a group of order $2^{k}$, and then $f\,\Cl(p,q)\,f \cong D$, of real dimension $1$, $2$ or $4$. Bott periodicity in the form $\Cl(p+1, q+1) \cong \Mat(2, \Cl(p,q))$ (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-eight-towers-mod-8-clock}) preserves $D$ and the component count, so both depend on the residue alone; the dependence within one period is the classical table (Cartan, Bott), recomputed here: the script builds every $\Cl(p,q)$ of dimension at most five in \texttt{GA.py}, computes the centre by exact linear algebra, finds a primitive idempotent by exhaustive search over blade sets and computes $\dim_{\R} f\,\Cl(p,q)\,f$ by exact rank, agreeing with the table on all twenty signatures and on the eight P3 representatives. The intrinsic group is the unit group of $D$: $\{\pm 1\}$, $U(1)$, $Sp(1)$. (i) to (iv) read off the table, in the labelling convention of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}; the two-sphere in (iii) is the set of imaginary unit quaternions.




**Theorem 27 (The residue is a soldered invariant, not an algebraic one).** Let $(A, V)$ denote a Clifford algebra together with its generating vector subspace $V \subset A$ (the soldering: $V$ is the image of the metric's tangent space, and $v^{2} = g(v,v)$ for $v \in V$). Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] any algebra isomorphism $(A, V) \to (A', V')$ carrying $V$ onto $V'$ preserves the signature $(p,q)$, hence the residue: the residue is an invariant of the soldered algebra;
\item[(ii)] as abstract algebras, residues $0$ and $2$, $3$ and $7$, and $4$ and $6$ are pairwise isomorphic at equal dimension, $\Cl(1,1) \cong \Cl(2,0)$, $\Cl(1,2) \cong \Cl(3,0)$, $\Cl(1,3) \cong \Cl(0,4) \cong \Mat_2(\HH)$; in particular this world's algebra and the $r = 4$ tower's are the same algebra, and no isomorphism between them carries vectors to vectors.
\end{enumerate}
So the tower charge is an invariant of the operator algebra soldered to the metric, not of the algebra: what forbids a portal in the section ``Dark, stable, counted'' is the one metric, P1.

*Proof.* (i): the quadratic form $v \mapsto v^{2}$ on $V$ is intrinsic to $(A, V)$, an isomorphism carrying $V$ to $V'$ carries it to the quadratic form of $V'$, and signature is an invariant of real quadratic forms (Sylvester, \claimref{synthesis.completion-recovers-the-real-clifford-algebras}(iii)). (ii): the abstract isomorphisms are the classical table (\claimref{primer.classification-real-clifford-algebras}) (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.type-residue-what-each-tower-can} recomputes their invariants: same dimension, centre, division algebra, component count and module dimension for each pair); that none carries $V$ to $V'$ is (i) applied to the differing signatures, and is exhibited directly for the pair $(1,3)$, $(0,4)$: every vector of $\Cl(0,4)$ squares to $-|v|^{2} \leq 0$ while $e_{0} \in \Cl(1,3)$ squares to $+1$, so the image of $e_{0}$ under any isomorphism is not a vector. The script verifies the agreement of the abstract invariants for the three pairs and the sign statements on sampled rational vectors.




**Definition 28 (Frozen-phase operators).** Two things are fixed, and they are different. The one gravitational metric is shared by every tower (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point}); each tower $r$ has its own soldering $V_{r}$, the generating subspace of its own algebra $(A_{r}, V_{r})$, with $V_{6}$ the one metric's tangent space and the others the other towers' (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.residue-soldered-invariant-not-algebraic-one}). The \emph{frozen-phase sector decomposition} is the hypothesis that the frozen phase's state space is the direct sum over residues of sectors, the $r$-th a module over the soldered algebra $(A_{r}, V_{r})$, and that a \emph{frozen-phase local operator} is a section of one tower's soldered Clifford bundle: built, fibrewise, from that tower's soldering by Clifford multiplication and the connection it defines, with gravity entering every sector only through the shared metric's stress-energy coupling. This is the operational reading of Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point}, stated as a hypothesis and carried as one.

**Remark  (Where the flag is defined, and what it carries).** Every tower has rungs, the $(1,1)$ ascent adjoining a hyperbolic plane at each floor (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-eight-towers-mod-8-clock}), so at tower level every residue is flaggable (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}) and flaggability selects nothing. The flag counts no towers; it orients them (the section ``The arrow counted''), and it equips them: a flag on $H_{k}$ is equivalently the complex structure $J_{k}$ on the complexification $H_{k} \oplus I_4H_{k}$ (the plane $H_{k} \otimes \C$, with the field theory's imaginary unit $I_4$, which commutes with every vector of the plane) whose $-I_4$ eigenline is the annihilated half (it lives in the complexification, since no real endomorphism squaring to $-1$ has the two real null lines as eigenlines), and the ladder pair's number operator generates a $U(1)$ commuting with the rest of the rung. So every flagged tower carries rung-level abelian charges, one per rung, whatever its ground type, as this tower's derived content acquires its $U(1)$ from three ladder pairs \cite{ButlerT12}.



**Definition 29 (Null blades, the Fock ideal, and chirality).** Let $V_{\C}$ be the complexified ambient of a representative, of dimension $2m$ or $2m + 1$, and let $V_{\C} = W \oplus W'$ ($\oplus\, \C\ell$ in odd dimension) be a Witt decomposition into a maximal isotropic subspace $W$ and a dual isotropic complement $W'$, with null bases $w_{1}, \ldots, w_{m}$ of $W$ and $v_{1}, \ldots, v_{m}$ of $W'$ normalised so that $v_{i}w_{j} + w_{j}v_{i} = \delta_{ij}$, and, in odd dimension, an extra unit generator $\ell$ orthogonal to both. The \emph{null blade} of $W$ is $f = w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{m}$, and it is annihilated by $W$ from the left: $wf = 0$ for every $w \in W$. The \emph{pseudoscalar} is $I = e_{1}\cdots e_{\dim V}$ for an orthonormal basis, normalised by a fourth root of unity so that $I^{2} = 1$ (the sign of $I$ is a convention; only ratios of its eigenvalues are used below); in even dimension it anticommutes with every vector, in odd dimension it is central. The \emph{Fock ideal} is, in even dimension, the left ideal $S = \Cl(V_{\C})\,f$, spanned by the $2^{m}$ states $v_{i_{1}}\cdots v_{i_{k}}f$ ($i_{1} < \cdots < i_{k}$), the \emph{Fock grade}-$k$ states (counting creation operators, not the Clifford grade); it is the spinor module. In odd dimension the left ideal $\Cl(V_{\C})\,f$ has dimension $2^{m+1}$ and is the sum of the two irreducible modules; the Fock ideal is then either chirality-projected half, $S_{\pm} = \tfrac{1}{2}(1 \pm I)\,\Cl(V_{\C})\,f$, of dimension $2^{m}$, graded by the Fock grade of the projected states $\tfrac{1}{2}(1 \pm I)\,v_{i_{1}}\cdots v_{i_{k}}f$, on which $I$ acts as $\pm 1$ and the extra generator $\ell$ acts as that grade's parity up to a fixed scalar. A state $\psi \in S$ with $I\psi = \pm\psi$ has \emph{chirality} $\pm 1$. For a maximal isotropic subspace $U \subset V_{\C}$, a \emph{pure spinor of $U$} is a nonzero $\psi_{U} \in S$ with $U\psi_{U} = 0$.



**Theorem 30 (The two families are the two chiralities).** In even dimension $2m$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] every maximal isotropic $U$ has a pure spinor, unique up to scale, and it is a chirality eigenstate; the pure spinor of $W$ is the null blade $f$ itself, of Fock grade zero, and the pure spinor of $W'$ is the top state $v_{1}\cdots v_{m}f$, of Fock grade $m$;
\item[(ii)] the chirality of $\psi_{U}$ is preserved by even versors (the spin group) and reversed by odd versors (reflections), so the \emph{family} of $U$, defined as the chirality of its pure spinor, is an invariant of the connected isometry group and not of the full one;
\item[(iii)] there are exactly two families, and a Witt pair $W$, $W'$ lies in opposite families if and only if $m$ is odd.
\end{enumerate}
In odd dimension $2m + 1$ there is one family: the connected isometry group acts transitively on maximal isotropic subspaces, and the spin algebra does not preserve the grade parity of $S$.

*Proof.* (i) Existence for $W$: $w_{i}f = 0$ since $w_{i}$ anticommutes with the other $w_{j}$ and $w_{i}^{2} = 0$. Uniqueness: on the grade-$k$ state $v_{i_{1}}\cdots v_{i_{k}}f$, $w_{j}$ acts as zero if $j \notin \{i_{1}, \ldots, i_{k}\}$ and otherwise, using $w_{j}v_{i} = \delta_{ij} - v_{i}w_{j}$ (\claimref{std-algebra.witt-basis-car}; here the $w_{i}$ annihilate $f$ and the $v_{i}$ create, the reverse of the reading suggested there, and the relations are symmetric in the two roles), removes the factor $v_{j}$ up to sign; so the common kernel of $w_{1}, \ldots, w_{m}$ on $S$ is the grade-zero line $\C f$. For $W'$ the same computation with the roles exchanged gives the top state, which every $v_{i}$ annihilates since $v_{i}^{2} = 0$. For a general $U$, Witt's theorem (\claimref{std-algebra.witt-extension}; as in Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.flag-insensitive-residue-no-flag-level}) supplies an isometry $\rho$ with $\rho(W) = U$, and $\rho$ is the twisted adjoint action of a versor $R$ (Lemma \claimref{primer.twisted-adjoint-action}), $\rho(x) = \pm RxR^{-1}$; then $U(Rf) = \pm (RWR^{-1})(Rf) = \pm R(Wf) = 0$, so $Rf$ is a pure spinor of $U$, and it is the only one up to scale because $R$ is invertible on $S$. Chirality: $I$ commutes with every even element, so $I$ acts on each grade as a scalar; explicitly
\[
I \;\propto\; \prod_{i}(v_{i}w_{i} - w_{i}v_{i}) \;=\; \prod_{i}(2v_{i}w_{i} - 1),
\]
and the $i$-th factor is $-1$ on states without $v_{i}$ and $+1$ on states with it, so the grade-$k$ states have chirality $\epsilon(-1)^{m-k}$ with $\epsilon$ the normalisation; every pure spinor, being a versor image of $f$, is a chirality eigenstate by (ii).

(ii) In even dimension $e_{j}I = -Ie_{j}$ for every basis vector (\claimref{std-algebra.pseudoscalar-commutation}), so $I$ commutes with even versors and anticommutes with odd ones; if $I\psi = \chi\psi$ then $I(R\psi) = \pm R I\psi = \pm\chi(R\psi)$ with the sign that of $R$'s parity.

(iii) Both chiralities occur among pure spinors (reflect $W$ in any non-null vector), chirality is constant on each orbit of the connected group by (ii), and the full isometry group, which is the connected group together with one reflection, is transitive by Witt; so there are exactly two orbits, one per chirality. For the Witt pair, $f$ has grade $0$ and the top state grade $m$, so their chiralities are $\epsilon(-1)^{m}$ and $\epsilon$: opposite exactly when $m$ is odd. Odd dimension: $-1$ is an improper isometry ($\det(-1) = (-1)^{2m+1}$) that fixes every subspace, so every orbit of the full group is an orbit of the connected group, and Witt's transitivity gives one family; and on either half $S_{\pm}$ the extra generator $\ell$ acts as the Fock grade parity up to a scalar (script-checked in dimensions five and seven), while the spin-algebra bivector $e_{1}\ell$ anticommutes with $\ell$, so the spin algebra mixes the Fock grades within each irreducible module.
