\emph{The mechanism's four clauses and the nearest alternative to each. The section weighting counts a cut cell by the measure of its section relative to the maximal section, one normalisation among several: a unit $D$-ball cut at height $h$ has section measure $V_{D-1}(1 - h^{2})^{(D-1)/2}$, and relative to $V_{D-1}$ these weights integrate over both sides to $V_{D}/V_{D-1}$, which for $D = 4$ is $\tfrac{3\pi}{8}$, the $\cos^{4}$ factor of the zero-mode reduction (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}(i)); that reduction is the five-dimensional count with I and S withdrawn. With C withdrawn the sourcing rule reverts to the homogeneous integral over a length $c$, which is Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}. The list is the paper's, not exhaustive; other cell conventions and other normalisations of a partial count are not on it.}

**Remark  (What S uses, and what it does not).** S needs a side for each sourcing quantum or sheet, and the side is postulated. What the lemma supplies is that a side exists: in an odd tower $\omega$ is central, so its sign is a label a quantum carries where the centre splits and a convention for the tower where it does not, and in the even neighbour's frame that sign is the sign of the direction the nesting adds; the even towers have no such sign, their centre being $\R$. S selects no module: where $\omega^{2} = +1$, Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mode-counts-two-modules-which-one}(ii) counts both modules under P2, R2 with S then puts each at half the density on its own side, and the two slabs sum to one cell radius, exactly as if one module held the whole density. Where $\omega^{2} = -1$ the sign is a convention for the tower, every quantum sources the same side, and the count is one cell radius at full density. S uses no orientation off the reversal orbit: reversing the neighbour's $P$ exchanges every side at once and the count is unchanged, so P1's orientation clause stays where Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point} keeps it. And S does not orient the odd tower's family: Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension} concerns the flag bit, and the sign of $\omega$ is a different object. What is postulated is the one-sidedness itself, and for the residue-$1$ sheets the further stipulation that a sheet holds one module; without the first both sides count and the total is $5.729$ (the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3}), and without the second the residue-$1$ sheets are sourced from both sides.

**Remark  (Scope of the mechanism).** Two limits keep the mechanism an explanation of R3's value rather than a derivation of it. First, it does not compose. For the double nesting of the three-dimensional tower's plane in the five-dimensional tower's space, R3 gives $\rho_{5\mathrm{D}}/\rho_{3\mathrm{D}} = V_{2}/V_{4} = 0.637$, one cell radius for each of the two unshared directions; counting the five-dimensional cells that meet the plane directly by C and I gives $V_{2}^{2}/V_{4} = 2$ on the full transverse disc, $1$ on a half-disc and $\tfrac{1}{2}$ on a quadrant, and S is undefined there, $P^{-1}\omega$ across two directions being a bivector. So C with I holds for adjacent nestings only, which is where the rule uses it, and O is not tested there; R3, which composes, is the statement, and the mechanism is a reading of one step. Second, C restricts the homogeneous integral of Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones} to one cell radius, and under it the five-dimensional tower's matter further from this world sources the one metric not at all. With P1 that matter is either absent, so that the extra direction is about one cell deep (the co-expanding direction of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.extra-direction-must-co-expand}, forced rather than allowed) and C adds nothing to the integral over it, which is R3; or present and inert to gravity, against Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.sourcing-rule-forced-not-chosen}(i), which takes the integral over the whole direction as the only covariant choice. On the first branch C is R3; on the second it contradicts a proposition the paper calls forced. On neither is it a foundation.

**setting  (The thickness: a contact mechanism).** \textbf{Sensitivity.} The rest of this subsection records the total's dependence on the thickness taken as a parameter, which is where the observation's constraint on R3 lives.



**Theorem 53 (The total as a function of the thickness).** Let the five-dimensional towers' matter count through a length $c\,d_{b}$ of the extra direction and the three-dimensional towers' sheets be stacked $c\,d_{b}$ apart, with R1, R2 and the sourcing rule otherwise as before, the four odd towers populated (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated}) and residues $0$ and $4$ empty (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.single-ideal-at-residues-zero-and-four}). Here $d_{b}$ is the cell radius, each tower's own and common to all towers under R2, so the thickness is measured in the unit R2 supplies. Then
\[
T(c) \;:=\; \frac{\Omega_{\mathrm{dark}}}{\Omega_{b}} \;=\; 1 + \frac{8}{3c} + \frac{16c}{3\pi},
\]
and:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $T(c) = T\!\left(\dfrac{\pi}{2c}\right)$ for every $c > 0$;
\item[(ii)] $T$ has a single minimum,
\[
c_{\ast} = \sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}} = 1.2533, \qquad T_{\ast} = 1 + 2\sqrt{\frac{128}{9\pi}} = 5.2554,
\]
which is $1.7\sigma$ below the observed value; no thickness gives less;
\item[(iii)] $T(c) = 5.3643$ exactly at $c = 1$ and $c = \tfrac{\pi}{2}$, the two roots of one quadratic, with product $\tfrac{\pi}{2}$;
\item[(iv)] against $5.364 \pm 0.065$ the allowed thicknesses are $c \in [0.943, 1.086]$ or $c \in [1.447, 1.667]$ at one standard deviation, and the single interval $c \in [0.898, 1.750]$ at two;
\item[(v)] the three-dimensional fraction of dark matter, which self-interacts if that matter is at composite strength (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}), is
\[
\frac{2 f_{3\mathrm{D}}}{T(c)} \;=\; \frac{8}{3c\,T(c)} : \qquad 49.7\% \text{ at } c = 1, \quad 40.5\% \text{ at } c_{\ast}, \quad 31.6\% \text{ at } c = \tfrac{\pi}{2}.
\]
\end{enumerate}

*Proof.* Per unit of our three-volume (by Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones}), with the common quantum mass of \claimref{dark-matter.r1-equal-mass-per-quantum} and one quantum per unit ball of \claimref{dark-matter.r2-one-quantum-per-unit-ball}, the three densities are
\[
\rho_{5\mathrm{D}} = \frac{m\,c}{V_{4}\,d_{b}^{3}}, \qquad
\rho_{3\mathrm{D}} = \frac{m}{c\,V_{2}\,d_{b}^{3}}, \qquad
\rho_{\mathrm{us}} = \frac{m}{V_{3}\,d_{b}^{3}},
\]
so the shares are
\[
f_{3\mathrm{D}} = \frac{V_{3}}{c\,V_{2}} = \frac{4}{3c}, \qquad f_{5\mathrm{D}} = \frac{c\,V_{3}}{V_{4}} = \frac{8c}{3\pi},
\]
and the sum, with the mirror's share $1$ from Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent} and two towers of each odd dimension (populated by Premise \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated}, the towers of residues $0$ and $4$ adding nothing by Premise \claimref{dark-matter.single-ideal-at-residues-zero-and-four}), with equal shares within each odd pair (by \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference}), is the relation displayed with \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}. (i) Substitute. (ii) $T$ is of the form $1 + \frac{A}{c} + Bc$ with $A = \frac{8}{3}$ and $B = \frac{16}{3\pi}$; by the arithmetic-geometric inequality
\[
\frac{A}{c} + Bc \;\geq\; 2\sqrt{AB}, \qquad \text{with equality at } c = \sqrt{\frac{A}{B}} = \sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}} .
\]
(iii) $T(c) = T(1)$ is the quadratic
\[
Bc^{2} - \bigl(T(1) - 1\bigr)c + A = 0, \qquad c_{1}c_{2} = \frac{A}{B} = \frac{\pi}{2};
\]
$c = 1$ is a root by construction and $\frac{\pi}{2}$ is the other. (iv) and (v) are evaluation. The script checks (i) as an identity in $\pi$ for rational $c$, brackets $T_{\ast}$ and the window endpoints by rational bounds on $\pi$ and on the square root, and evaluates the fractions.




**Theorem 54 (The two roots are the chord and the arc of a quarter turn).** In the sphere form of the rule, represent the five-dimensional tower's spacetime by a sphere of radius $R$ with our spacetime as its equator. The region between the equator and one pole has extent $R$ along the polar axis (the chord) and $\tfrac{\pi R}{2}$ along the surface (the arc). With $R = d_{b}$: counting the tower's matter per unit chord over that region is the unit-ball rule, $c = 1$; counting it per unit arc is the exchanged assignment, $c = \tfrac{\pi}{2}$; and the two totals agree because Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(i) exchanges them, while their compositions differ (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}). R2 selects the chord: its cells are unit balls of the tower's flat space, whose measure along the added direction is the radius. Between the equator and both poles the values are $c = 2$ and $c = \pi$, giving $5.729$ and $7.182$, both excluded.

*Proof.* The chord from the equatorial plane to a pole is the radius; the arc is a quarter of a great circle. With both poles, in the total of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness},
\[
T(2) = 1 + \frac{4}{3} + \frac{32}{3\pi} = 5.729, \qquad T(\pi) = 1 + \frac{8}{3\pi} + \frac{16}{3} = 7.182 ;
\]
the script brackets both and confirms that each misses by more than four standard deviations. The clock reflection $r \mapsto 4 - r$, which exchanges each three-dimensional tower with a five-dimensional one, is the bookkeeping image of (i) and not a symmetry of the physics, the two odd types differing in gauge content and algebra type.


**Remark  (The common thickness, and the curve if the two differ).** R3 takes the extra length and the sheet spacing equal; in the mechanism that equality is clause O. Let them differ, $c_{5}$ and $c_{3}$. With $c_{5} = 1$ kept, the observation confines $c_{3}$ to $[0.976, 1.025]$ at one standard deviation and nothing else constrains it; with both free it fixes a curve,
\[
\frac{8}{3c_{3}} + \frac{16c_{5}}{3\pi} \;=\; 4.364,
\]
whose two intersections with the diagonal $c_{3} = c_{5}$ are the roots of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(iii), and off the diagonal nothing natural lands on it ($c_{3} = \tfrac{4}{3}$ needs $c_{5} = 1.39$, $c_{3} = 2$ needs $c_{5} = 1.79$).

**setting  (What could fix the unit: the de Sitter reading).** The rule needs one length common to all towers. The series fixes exactly one length, and this subsection follows it.



**Theorem 55 (Imported: the frozen point and the de Sitter radius).** On the Einstein region with $\Lambda > 0$ of a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}), the scale tractor $I$, parallel for the tractor gauge field of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gtg-tractor-gauge-field-reproduces-tractor-connection}, has squared norm $Q(I) = \tfrac{\Lambda}{3}$, its stabiliser is the de Sitter group $SO(1,4)$, its orthogonal complement $I^{\perp}$ in the vectors of $\Cl(2,4)$ carries the induced form of signature $(1,4)$, and the flat-model section is the de Sitter hyperboloid of radius $\ell$, $\ell^{2} = \tfrac{3}{\Lambda}$. Under the action as written, $\Lambda$ is a parameter of the laws, not initial data. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[Thm.~4.7, Prop.~4.10, Prop.~4.4]{ButlerT6}; \cite[Fact~2.1, Thm.~3.1, Prop.~5.8]{ButlerT10}.

*Proof.* Read in Gauge Theory Gravity: the metric $g$ below is the derived metric of the gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime, the manifold is the invertible region, and the tractor connection is the flat-space $\Cl(2,4)$ gauge field of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gtg-tractor-gauge-field-reproduces-tractor-connection}; the index computation below is its reading in the splitting of $g$ (Fact \claimref{synthesis.imported-gtg-dictionary}).

On the Einstein region with $\Lambda > 0$ the parallel scale tractor $I$ has squared norm $Q(I) = \tfrac{\Lambda}{3}$, its stabiliser is the de Sitter group $SO(1,4)$, its orthogonal complement $I^{\perp}$ carries the induced form of signature $(1,4)$, and the flat-model section is the de Sitter hyperboloid of radius $\ell$, $\ell^{2} = \tfrac{3}{\Lambda}$. Under the action as written, $\Lambda$ is a parameter of the laws, not initial data. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[Thm.~4.7, Prop.~4.10, Prop.~4.4]{ButlerT6} (\claimref{interior.scale-tractor-leaves-cone}, \claimref{interior.non-null-stabilisers-section-they-act}, \claimref{interior.stabiliser-rotates-parabolic-reductive}); \cite[Fact~2.1, Thm.~3.1, Prop.~5.8]{ButlerT10} (\claimref{dark-energy.frozen-point-makes-stelle-west-vector}, \claimref{dark-energy.under-action-written-norm-parameter-laws}, \claimref{dark-energy.imported-frozen-point-geometry}).


**setting  (What could fix the unit: the de Sitter reading).** By Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-frozen-point-de-sitter-radius}, our world with $\Lambda > 0$ is de Sitter space of radius $\ell = \sqrt{\tfrac{3}{\Lambda}}$ at the level of the frozen point, and Euclidean de Sitter space $\dS_{4}$ is $\Sph^{4}$ of radius $\ell$, the boundary of $\B^{5}$ of radius $\ell$. The towers share one gravity, and $\Lambda$ is a parameter of the laws, so every tower has the same $\Lambda$ and its own Euclidean de Sitter sphere $\Sph^{n(r)}$ of the same radius $\ell$; the nesting of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.nesting}(iii) is the nesting of these spheres by equators. So the objects of the rule's sphere form (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}), and the common unit R2 needs, are already in the series, with the unit the de Sitter radius rather than a microscopic spacing.



**Theorem 56 (Democracy at the de Sitter radius is the unit-ball rule).** Under D, with the thickness set to $\ell$ (R3 with $\ell$ as the unit), the shares are $\tfrac{|\Sph^{4}|}{|\Sph^{n}|}$, the sphere form of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}, and the total is that of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}, summed as there over the mirror and the four odd towers (populated by Premise \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated}, residues $0$ and $4$ empty by Premise \claimref{dark-matter.single-ideal-at-residues-zero-and-four}). \emph{Note (the unit; restated 19 November 2026).} Premise R3 takes as its unit the length of Premise E (\claimref{dark-matter.e-equal-mass-per-unit-ball}). D is E at the de Sitter radius, given the stored reading of the sphere form (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(ii)), so under D the unit of R3 is $\ell$ and the thickness used here is R3's own; E holds in one length only, so this reading and the fitted total with E at another length are alternatives. Held with R1 and R2, D gives each tower one quantum per de Sitter ball (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.d-democracy-total-mass}, note). D is not among the premises of the fitted total, which are E and R3.

*Proof.* The total is the sum of these shares over the mirror and the four odd towers, as in Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}: that the odd towers hold matter is Premise \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated}, and that residues $0$ and $4$ add nothing is Premise \claimref{dark-matter.single-ideal-at-residues-zero-and-four}.

A tower of dimension $n$ has density $\frac{M_{\mathrm{tot}}}{|\Sph^{n}|\,\ell^{n}}$ on its sphere (by \claimref{dark-matter.d-democracy-total-mass}); through a thickness $\ell$ of the direction we cannot see, or with sheets $\ell$ apart (by \claimref{dark-matter.r3-one-cell-per-unshared-direction}), that becomes a density per unit of our four-measure, and the share is
\[
f \;=\; \frac{M_{\mathrm{tot}}}{|\Sph^{n}|\,\ell^{4}} \Bigg/ \frac{M_{\mathrm{tot}}}{|\Sph^{4}|\,\ell^{4}} \;=\; \frac{|\Sph^{4}|}{|\Sph^{n}|} . \qedhere
\]


**setting  (What could fix the unit: the de Sitter reading).** This reading removes the two costs Remarks \claimref{note.dark-matter.what-rule-has-has-not} and \claimref{note.dark-matter.extra-direction-must-co-expand} record: no conserved count is needed to fill the odd towers at one microscopic spacing, and there is no metre-scale expanding extra dimension. Two things stand against it. First, under the sourcing rule a five-dimensional tower's fifth direction is a direction of its own space, not of spacetime, so the residue-$5$ tower cannot be identified with our own tractor ambient $I^{\perp}$; the reading survives only as ``each tower has its own $\dS_{n}$ at the shared $\Lambda$'', which the series permits and does not make. Second, the thickness is still R3's, now at one de Sitter radius: within the series as written there is no $\dS_{5}$ to slice, because there is no five-dimensional Einstein equation for it to solve, and $c$ is exactly the freedom P1 leaves, which R3 fixes, the contact mechanism of the section ``The thickness: a contact mechanism'' explains, and the geometry does not reach. To compute the thickness at all one must promote the fifth direction to a metric direction, against the sourcing rule. Doing so: $\dS_{5}$ of radius $\ell$ is foliated by $\dS_{4}$ slices,
\begin{equation}
ds_{5}^{2} = \ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\; ds_{4}^{2}, \qquad \theta \in (-\pi/2, \pi/2),
\end{equation}
with our world the equator $\theta = 0$. Let the five-dimensional tower's dust fill the bulk at density $\rho_{5}$, comoving with the slices, and let ours sit on the equator with four-dimensional density $\rho_{\mathrm{us}}$. Two standard ways of extracting a four-dimensional Friedmann equation exist, and both are computed here.



**Fact (imported) 57 (Imported: the induced equations on a hypersurface).** Let a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat $\R^{4,1}$, in the signature $(-,+,+,+,+)$ of the source (the $d$-dimensional clause of Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}, $d = 5$, with the product of that signature) obey the five-dimensional field equations, which in the source's sign read $a \cdot G(b) = \kappa_{5}^{2}\, a \cdot T(b)$ for physical vectors $a, b$, with $G$ the Einstein map of the derived metric and $T$ the stress-energy map, and let $\Sigma$ be a timelike hypersurface of the flat background with unit physical normal $n$ (a physical vector, $Q(n) = +1$, orthogonal to $\hb^{-1}$ of the tangent directions of $\Sigma$). Then the Einstein map of the metric that $\hb^{-1}$ induces on $\Sigma$, as a bilinear form on the physical tangent vectors $a, b$ of $\Sigma$, contains, from the bulk stress-energy, the term
\[
\frac{2\kappa_{5}^{2}}{3}\Bigl[\,a \cdot T(b) + \bigl(n \cdot T(n) - \tfrac{1}{4}T\bigr)\, a \cdot b\Bigr],
\]
with $T = \partial_a \cdot T(a)$ the trace (in components: $\tfrac{2\kappa_{5}^{2}}{3}[T_{\mu\nu} + (T_{ab}n^{a}n^{b} - \tfrac14 T)g_{\mu\nu}]$), together with terms quadratic in the hypersurface's own extrinsic curvature, the projected $D$-derivative of $n$, and the projected bulk Weyl curvature $\mathcal{W}$, the trace-free part of the five-dimensional field strength. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[eq.~(15)]{SMS2000}, read in five-dimensional Gauge Theory Gravity through the frame formalism, which holds in every dimension.

*Provenance:* T. Shiromizu, K. Maeda and M. Sasaki, The Einstein equations on the 3-brane world, Phys. Rev. D 62, 024012 (2000)., eq.~(15).



**Theorem 58 (The two reductions).** In the setting of $ds_{5}^{2} = \ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\; ds_{4}^{2}, \qquad \theta \in (-\pi/2, \pi/2)$, read in five-dimensional Gauge Theory Gravity (the $d$-dimensional clause of Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}): the warped product $\ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\,ds_{4}^{2}$ is the derived metric of the gravitational field on flat $\R^{4,1}$, in the signature $(-,+,+,+,+)$ of $ds_{5}^{2} = \ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\; ds_{4}^{2}, \qquad \theta \in (-\pi/2, \pi/2)$ (in the store's sign the derived metric is its negative), with $\hb^{-1}(e_\theta) = \ell\,e_\theta$ and $\hb^{-1}(a) = \cos\theta\,\hb_{4}^{-1}(a)$ on the four-dimensional directions, and the five-dimensional Einstein-Hilbert action is $\int \det(\hb)^{-1}\,\mathcal{R}\,d^{5}x$ (sign as in $ds_{5}^{2} = \ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\; ds_{4}^{2}, \qquad \theta \in (-\pi/2, \pi/2)$):
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] (Zero mode.) Integrating the five-dimensional Einstein-Hilbert and matter actions over $\theta$, the gravitational term, the bulk dust and the equatorial sheet acquire the factors
\[
\ell\int_{-\pi/2}^{\pi/2}\cos^{2}\theta\,d\theta = \frac{\pi\ell}{2}, \qquad
\ell\int_{-\pi/2}^{\pi/2}\cos^{4}\theta\,d\theta = \frac{3\pi\ell}{8}, \qquad 1,
\]
so $G_{4} = \frac{2G_{5}}{\pi\ell}$. Newton's constant cancels in the ratio, and the five-dimensional tower's effective thickness is
\[
L_{\mathrm{eff}} \;=\; \frac{3\pi}{8}\,\ell \;=\; \frac{V_{4}}{V_{3}}\,\ell .
\]
\item[(ii)] (Local projection.) For dust comoving with the slice, $T_{ab}n^{a}n^{b} = 0$ and $T = -\rho_{5}$, so the bulk term of Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-induced-equations-hypersurface} contributes on the slice the energy density
\[
\frac{2\kappa_{5}^{2}}{3}\left(\rho_{5} - \frac{\rho_{5}}{4}\right) \;=\; \frac{\kappa_{5}^{2}\,\rho_{5}}{2} .
\]
With the $G_{4}$ of (i) this is an effective thickness $L_{\mathrm{eff}} = \frac{\pi}{4}\,\ell$.
\item[(iii)] (The sheets.) A comoving family of three-dimensional sheets carrying total mass $M_{\mathrm{tot}}$ over our $\Sph^{4}$ of radius $\ell$, each at the three-dimensional tower's own density $\frac{M_{\mathrm{tot}}}{|\Sph^{3}|\,\ell^{3}}$, has spacing
\[
s \;=\; \frac{|\Sph^{4}|}{|\Sph^{3}|}\,\ell \;=\; \frac{4}{3}\,\ell
\]
along the polar arc.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, under D: (i) with (iii) gives every share $1$ and the total $5.000$; (ii) with (iii) gives $4.333$; (i) with sheets one radius apart gives $5.667$; (ii) with sheets one radius apart gives $5.000$ again (the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}). The rule's $L_{\mathrm{eff}} = \ell$ is produced by neither reduction. \emph{Beyond one flat chart.} The closed four-sphere of radius $\ell$ over which the sheet family of (iii) is counted is global and is not carried by a single gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime; the statements above that hold on the invertible region are in Gauge Theory Gravity, and that four-sphere is read on the manifold assembled from the derived metric's charts.

*Proof.* Read in Gauge Theory Gravity: the metric below is the derived metric of a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat $\R^{4,1}$, in the signature $(-,+,+,+,+)$ of $ds_{5}^{2} = \ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\; ds_{4}^{2}, \qquad \theta \in (-\pi/2, \pi/2)$, and its index expressions are readings of $D$ and of the field strength through Fact \claimref{synthesis.imported-gtg-dictionary}.

The integrals are Wallis's. Foliating $\Sph^{n+1}$ by latitude $\phi$, the latitude sphere is an $\Sph^{n}$ of radius $\cos\phi$, so
\[
|\Sph^{n+1}| \;=\; |\Sph^{n}|\int_{-\pi/2}^{\pi/2}\cos^{n}\phi\,d\phi, \qquad \int\cos^{n} \;=\; \frac{n-1}{n}\int\cos^{n-2}, \qquad \int\cos^{0} = \pi, \quad \int\cos^{1} = 2,
\]
which give
\[
\int\cos^{2} \;=\; \frac{\pi}{2}, \qquad \int\cos^{3} \;=\; \frac{4}{3} \;=\; \frac{|\Sph^{4}|}{|\Sph^{3}|}, \qquad \int\cos^{4} \;=\; \frac{3\pi}{8} \;=\; \frac{V_{4}}{V_{3}}
\]
(script: the recursion and the three values as identities in $\pi$). (i) For the warped product $ds_{5}^{2} = \ell^{2}d\theta^{2} + \cos^{2}\theta\; ds_{4}^{2}, \qquad \theta \in (-\pi/2, \pi/2)$, $\sqrt{g_{5}} = \ell\cos^{4}\theta\sqrt{g_{4}}$ and the scalar curvature contains $\cos^{-2}\theta\,R_{4}$ plus terms independent of $g_{4}$, so the coefficient of $\sqrt{g_{4}}R_{4}$ is $\ell\int\cos^{2}$; a bulk density uniform in $\theta$ integrates with $\sqrt{g_{5}}$ to $\ell\int\cos^{4}$; a density $\rho_{\mathrm{us}}\delta(\ell\theta)$ on the equator integrates to $\rho_{\mathrm{us}}$. Both matter terms feed the same four-dimensional equation with the same $G_{4}$, so their ratio is
\[
\frac{3\pi\,\ell\,\rho_{5}}{8\,\rho_{\mathrm{us}}}.
\]
(ii) Contract the bracket of Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-induced-equations-hypersurface} with $u^{\mu}u^{\nu}$, $u^{2} = -1$: the energy density is $\rho_{5} - \rho_{5}/4$. (iii) With $s$ the spacing, the family's mass is
\[
\int_{-\pi/2}^{\pi/2}\frac{M_{\mathrm{tot}}}{|\Sph^{3}|\,\ell^{3}}\;|\Sph^{3}|\,\ell^{3}\cos^{3}\phi\;\frac{\ell\,d\phi}{s}
\;=\; \frac{4M_{\mathrm{tot}}\ell}{3s},
\]
which equals $M_{\mathrm{tot}}$ when $s = \tfrac{4}{3}\ell$. The consequences: under D (\claimref{dark-matter.d-democracy-total-mass}),
\[
\frac{\rho_{5}\,\ell}{\rho_{\mathrm{us}}} \;=\; \frac{|\Sph^{4}|}{|\Sph^{5}|} \;=\; \frac{8}{3\pi}, \qquad
f_{5\mathrm{D}}^{\mathrm{(i)}} \;=\; \frac{3\pi}{8}\cdot\frac{8}{3\pi} \;=\; 1, \qquad
f_{5\mathrm{D}}^{\mathrm{(ii)}} \;=\; \frac{\pi}{4}\cdot\frac{8}{3\pi} \;=\; \frac{2}{3};
\]
(iii) gives $f_{3\mathrm{D}} = 1$, and sheets one radius apart give $\tfrac{4}{3}$. The totals follow from the relation displayed with \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}; the script brackets each and its distance from the observation.

\emph{The odd towers' shares under democracy (Premise D) for each way of letting the five-dimensional geometry decide how much of a tower's mass sources the equator (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}). **Remark  (The one pole and the flat count).** Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-roots-are-chord-arc-quarter} reads the rule as ``our world sources from the five-dimensional tower's matter between the equator and one pole, counted flat,'' and two contrasts are in play. Chord against the sphere's own measure, whose $\cos^{4}$ weighting gives $\tfrac{3\pi}{8}$ in Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}(i), is I with S: one pole rather than two is S (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.what-s-uses-what-it-does}), counting flat is I. Chord against arc, the two roots of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(iii), R2 decides: its cells are unit balls of the tower's flat space, whose measure along the added direction is the chord.

**setting  (Consistency with other laws).** The sourcing rule says that another tower's matter enters the one metric as its energy per unit of the shared three-volume, not as a total. Against laws the series does not own, that is forced rather than chosen, and it fixes the form of R3 without fixing its value. The same laws say how the three-dimensional towers' sheets must move, and a Boltzmann computation then checks the rule's composition against the linear spectra.



**Theorem 59 (The sourcing rule is forced, not chosen).** (i) The field equations of a gravitational field on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}), Einstein's equations for its derived metric, $\mathcal{G}(a) = -8\pi G\, T(a)$ in the store's sign (in components $G_{\mu\nu} = 8\pi G\,T_{\mu\nu}$), are sourced by a local energy density and never by a total; the only covariant four-dimensional stress-energy map obtainable from matter homogeneous along a direction the metric does not see is its integral over that direction, $\rho_{4} = \rho_{5}L$, which is Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones} read from the field equations. (ii) It passes the three standard tests. Continuity for dust in a product metric $a(t)^{2}dx^{2} + b(t)^{2}dy^{2}$ (the derived metric of the diagonal field $\hb^{-1}(e_x) = a\,e_x$, $\hb^{-1}(e_y) = b\,e_y$ on flat $\R^{1,4}$, the $d$-dimensional clause of the definition) gives $\rho_{5} \propto 1/(a^{3}b)$, so with $L \propto b$ the projected density $\rho_{5}L$ dilutes as $a^{-3}$ for a co-expanding and a static fifth direction alike, as the lemma states. Kaluza-Klein reduction over a metric fifth direction of size $L$ gives $G_{4} = G_{5}/L$ with $\rho_{4} = \rho_{5}L$, so $G_{4}\rho_{4} = G_{5}\rho_{5}$ is independent of $L$ for the bulk tower and the share $\rho_{5}L/\rho_{\mathrm{us}}$ is the same however Newton's constant is defined. And the projected stress-energy of five-dimensional dust comoving with our slice is four-dimensional dust, a quantum with momentum along the fifth direction appearing as a heavier particle at rest, by $Q(p) = m^{2} + p_{5}^{2}$ for its four-momentum $p$.

*Proof.* Read in Gauge Theory Gravity: the line elements are the derived metrics of gravitational fields $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime, and the action densities are written with the volume factor $\det(\hb)^{-1}$ and the scalar field strength $\mathcal{R}$ through Fact \claimref{synthesis.imported-gtg-dictionary}.

(i) is the statement of the field equations. (ii): the conservation law and the reduction,
\[
\dot\rho + \rho\,\partial_{t}\ln(a^{3}b) \;=\; 0, \qquad \int\det(\hb_{5})^{-1}\,\mathcal{R}_{5}\,d^{5}x \;\supset\; L\int\det(\hb_{4})^{-1}\,\mathcal{R}_{4}\,d^{4}x,
\]
the latter against the reduction of the matter term; and the decomposition of the five-momentum, $Q(p + p_{5}e_{5}) = Q(p) - p_{5}^{2} = m^{2}$ with $Q(e_5) = -1$. The script checks the dilution exponents for $b \propto a$ and $b$ constant, the cancellation of $L$ in $G_{4}\rho_{4}$, and the momentum identity on rational samples.




**Fact (imported) 60 (Imported: the inverse-square law at short range).** Newton's inverse-square law holds down to separations of about $50$ micrometres, and a single extra spatial dimension of size larger than about $40$ micrometres is excluded. \emph{Provenance:} \cite{Lee2020}.

*Provenance:* J. G. Lee, E. G. Adelberger, T. S. Cook, S. M. Fleischer and B. R. Heckel, New test of the gravitational $1/r^2$ law at separations down to 52 $mu$m, Phys. Rev. Lett. 124, 101101 (2020)..



**Theorem 61 (The thickness is not a gravitational quantity).** If the fifth direction carried the metric, gravity would fall as the inverse cube on scales below its extent $L$. The two extents the series has are the baryon spacing and the de Sitter radius,
\[
d_{b} \;=\; \left(\frac{3}{4\pi n_{b}}\right)^{1/3} \;\approx\; 0.98\ \mathrm{m}, \qquad \ell \;=\; \sqrt{\frac{3}{\Lambda}} \;\approx\; 10^{26}\ \mathrm{m},
\]
with $n_{b} = 0.25\ \mathrm{m}^{-3}$ from $\Omega_{b}h^{2} = 0.02237$ (script: $d_{b}$ bracketed in $(0.95, 1.02)$ m). Both exceed the bound of Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inverse-square-law-short-range} by more than four orders of magnitude. So the four-dimensional reading of P1 is forced by experiment, not chosen; the reductions of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}, which needed a metric fifth direction to be well posed, describe a setting that is itself excluded; and the thickness in R3 can never be measured or computed gravitationally, since gravity does not see the direction it lives in. Only the tower's own dynamics, its cell size, can set it, so R3 is correctly a statement counted in the tower's own cells; and for the projected density $\rho_{5}L$ to be finite the fifth direction must be compact or the matter localised within $L$ by those dynamics, which the series does not supply. That the count is one radius is R3; the contact mechanism of the section ``The thickness: a contact mechanism'' returns it, and gravity cannot. The comparison with $d_{b}$ takes today's baryon spacing as the extent, which assumes that the hidden direction co-expands with the shared ones. With R2 and R3 read as deposit clauses and a static hidden direction, the counted thickness today is the deposit-epoch cell radius, below $2.4 \times 10^{-10}$ m for any descent above $1$ MeV, more than $10^{5}$ times under the bound (Proposition \claimref{synthesis.unit-ball-clauses-hold-at-one-epoch-unless-hidden-direction-co-expands}(iv)); for that length the comparison above gives no exclusion, and the experimental argument rests on the co-expanding reading and on $\ell$.

\emph{Note (evaporation, argued).} Black holes reach where gravity does not. Under the interface premise a populated five-dimensional tower radiates through its Kaluza-Klein levels, whose number below a hole's temperature grows linearly with the extent $L_{f}$ of the fifth direction (\claimref{synthesis.five-dimensional-tower-kaluza-klein-channels-steepen-burst}(i)). If a massless five-dimensional species exists, an open fifth direction therefore makes every hole's emission infinite, so observed black-hole lifetimes exclude the branch above in which the direction is open and the matter is localised within $L$ by its dynamics, for any species free to move along the whole direction; the compact branch remains. And under the static deposit reading the extent is finite and small, and the break $E_{b} \propto 1/L_{f}$ of the burst's primary high-energy tail (\claimref{synthesis.five-dimensional-tower-kaluza-klein-channels-steepen-burst}(iii), (iv)) measures it, so the thickness, which gravity cannot see, is in principle observable through evaporation ($L_{f}$ is at least the counted thickness, and equal to it when the matter fills the fifth direction).

*Proof.* Follows from \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inverse-square-law-short-range} and \claimref{dark-matter.sourcing-rule-forced-not-chosen}, as the source states.




**Fact (imported) 62 (Imported: the linear momentum equation and the traceless field equation).** For the perturbed FLRW gravitational field on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}) in conformal Newtonian gauge, $\hb^{-1}(e_\tau) = a(1 + \psi)\,e_\tau$, $\hb^{-1}(e_i) = a(1 - \phi)\,e_i$ at linear order, whose derived metric in the source's signature $(-,+,+,+)$ is $ds^{2} = a^{2}[-(1 + 2\psi)d\tau^{2} + (1 - 2\phi)dx^{2}]$, the covariant conservation of the stress-energy map under $D$, $\dot T(\dot D) = 0$ (in components $\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu\nu} = 0$), gives a pressureless component of velocity divergence $\theta$ and anisotropic stress $\sigma$ the momentum equation $\theta' = -\mathcal{H}\theta + k^{2}\psi - k^{2}\sigma$, and the traceless part of the field equations of the gravitational field gives $k^{2}(\phi - \psi) = 12\pi G a^{2}\sum_{i}(\rho_{i} + p_{i})\sigma_{i}$. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[eqs.~(30), (23d)]{MaBertschinger1995}.

*Provenance:* C.-P. Ma and E. Bertschinger, Cosmological perturbation theory in the synchronous and conformal Newtonian gauges, Astrophys. J. 455, 7 (1995)., eqs.~(30), (23d).



**Theorem 63 (A reduced response to gravity needs a supporting stress; the sheets move).** Let a pressureless component respond to the gravitational field on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}; the perturbed FLRW field in the conformal Newtonian gauge of Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-linear-momentum-equation-traceless-field}, potentials $\psi$, $\phi$) at a fraction $g$ of the full strength, $\theta' = -\mathcal{H}\theta + g\,k^{2}\psi$. Then (i) it carries the anisotropic stress $\sigma = (1 - g)\psi$, and no other value; (ii) that stress enters the field equations, so that with the other components' stresses $\Sigma$,
\[
\psi \;=\; \frac{\phi - 12\pi G a^{2}\Sigma/k^{2}}{1 + 12\pi G a^{2}\rho\,(1 - g)/k^{2}} .
\]
For a three-dimensional tower whose sheets are held fixed, $g = \sin^{2}\theta$ for a wavevector at angle $\theta$ to the missing direction (script: $k_{\perp}^{2}/k^{2}$), $\tfrac{2}{3}$ averaged over orientations, and the stress of (i) is that of a medium holding the sheets in place. The series supplies no such medium: nothing in Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones} or in the towers' content gives the sheet family a rigidity along its normal, and the sourcing rule couples the sheets' matter to the one gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ like any other. Hence, on the series as written, the sheets are displaced along their normal by the potential gradient, $g = 1$, and the three-dimensional towers respond to gravity in full at linear order.

*Proof.* (i) and (ii) are Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-linear-momentum-equation-traceless-field} read with $\theta' = -\mathcal{H}\theta + gk^{2}\psi$: subtracting the two momentum equations gives $k^{2}\sigma = (1 - g)k^{2}\psi$, and substituting $\rho\sigma = \rho(1 - g)\psi$ into the traceless equation and solving for $\psi$ gives the display (script: both identities on rational samples). The source factor is the projection of the potential gradient onto the sheet, $|\mathbf{k}_{\perp}|^{2}/|\mathbf{k}|^{2}$, whose average over the sphere of directions is $\tfrac{2}{3}$. The last two sentences are a statement about the series' content, at the level of Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones}: the lemma's sheet family is comoving in the background and carries no stress, so the only consistent reading of it is the one in which the sheets move.


**Remark  (The sheet family and the linear spectra).** Two readings of the sheet family are excluded, one by symmetry and one by the record. A fixed missing direction gives no clustering along it for $42\%$ of all matter, against the statistical isotropy of the matter power spectrum and the microwave background at the level of a few percent, and it is the uncaused selection P2 refuses. Orientations varying below observed scales give the isotropic average $g = \tfrac{2}{3}$, and that the spectra exclude directly. Computed with a Boltzmann code (a figure of the original; CLASS \cite{CLASS2011}, version 3.4, Planck 2018 parameters otherwise, the three-dimensional towers as a separate pressureless species responding at $g$ with the stress of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress} carried so that the field equations stay consistent; the patch, inputs and outputs are deposited with the verification materials as a record), $49.7\%$ of the dark matter at $g = \tfrac{2}{3}$ gives $\sigma_{8} = 0.36$ against $0.81$, the lensing-potential spectrum at $28\%$ of the standard model's, and the temperature spectrum $17\%$ high at $\ell = 100$, against measurements at the percent level; the floor's $40.5\%$ and the exchanged assignment's $31.6\%$ give $\sigma_{8} = 0.42$ and $0.49$; and with half the dark matter in the towers a deficit $1 - g$ above about half a percent is excluded by the lensing amplitude alone. Both readings need the stress the series does not have. The reading it does have, $g = 1$, returns in the same code the standard model's temperature, polarisation, lensing and matter power spectra to four decimal places for the rule's composition (the towers as a separate species at full response), so at linear order the rule's dark sector is the standard model's. Within collapsed structures the three-dimensional towers differ from cold dark matter only by what the sheets add beyond collisionless gravity (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.halo-built-collisionless-sheets-cold-dark}): the self-interaction of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}, which is the fraction test of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(v), with the cross-section read per sheet (at composite strength a fluid, the section ``The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy''), and, on the deposit branch only, an in-plane free-streaming cutoff. None of this bears on the value $5.364$. The Boltzmann integration is floating point and is recorded, not checked by the script.

**Remark  (A halo built from collisionless sheets is a cold-dark-matter halo).** Beyond linear order the question is what the sheets do to a halo, and the answer is an argument, not a simulation. A particle of a three-dimensional tower has two velocities: its motion within its sheet, the tower's own dynamics, and the motion of the sheet, which by Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress} follows the normal component of gravity pointwise. The one constraint the sheets impose is that at a point of a sheet the normal velocity is single-valued, the sheet being a surface; in-plane velocities are whatever the tower's dynamics allows, and sheets may cross, nothing forbidding it and the sourcing rule already stacking them. Cold dark matter is the same object in all three directions: a single-valued velocity field on a three-dimensional Lagrangian sheet in phase space, multi-streaming only where Lagrangian patches cross. So the sheet family's phase space contains cold dark matter's, one direction treated exactly as cold dark matter treats all three and two directions treated more permissively; with cold initial conditions, which the seeding floor gives (the section ``Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows''), the in-plane motion is itself a cold single-valued flow and the two phase spaces coincide. The Lagrangian map of the sheet matter is then cold dark matter's at every order in collisionless dynamics, not only at the linear order a figure of the original checks, and a simulation of sheet-labelled particles against plain ones would integrate the same equations. Halo shapes, velocity structure and the pancake, filament and halo sequence are cold dark matter's. What can differ is what the sheets add. First, in-sheet self-interaction: a quantum collides only with quanta of its own sheet, at composite strength on the reading of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}, so the collision rate is set by the surface density within a sheet and the galaxy-cluster bounds on $\sigma/m$ must be re-expressed for a cross-section per sheet before the fraction test of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(v) is read against them. The companion note T14b does this: per unit of the mass gravity sees, the cross-section is $10^{8}$ to $10^{13}\ \mathrm{cm^{2}\,g^{-1}}$, so at composite strength the sheet matter is a fluid within its sheets, not collisionless, and in simulated galaxy clusters it forms a cored component rounder than cold dark matter (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-composition-simulated}). The conclusion of this remark therefore holds only if the three-dimensional towers' matter is collisionless. Second, on the deposit branch, an in-plane thermal velocity $\sigma_{v}$: free streaming then damps small scales within the plane only, and averaged over orientations the damping is $\langle\exp(-k^{2}\sigma_{v}^{2}\tau^{2}\sin^{2}\theta)\rangle$, milder than isotropic warm dark matter at the same dispersion by the missing normal direction; at the seeding floor $\sigma_{v}$ is of order $10^{-4}$ of our matter's thermal velocity and the cutoff lies far below any observed scale. Structural level: the argument rests on cold initial conditions, on the sheets carrying no stress and on the sheet matter being collisionless.

**Conjecture 64 (The status of the rule).** \begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] The relation was found by solving the relation displayed with \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall} for the observed value, not predicted.
\item[(ii)] Among all expressions $1 + 2a + 2b$ with $a$ and $b$ ratios of the eight measures $V_{2}$ to $V_{5}$ and $|\Sph^{1}|$ to $|\Sph^{4}|$ ($3136$ expressions), $54$ lie within one standard deviation of $5.364$, taking $14$ distinct values, and $85$ within two; neighbouring targets give comparable counts ($43$ at $5.322$, $33$ at $5.24$). The closed form is one of fourteen equally simple candidates in the error bar (script). The mechanism of the section ``The thickness: a contact mechanism'' adds a discrete look-elsewhere of the same kind: of the clause combinations the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3} lists, only the chosen one reaches the observed value, and the clauses were chosen with it known.
\item[(iii)] The ball and sphere forms of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not} are one relation. The exchanged assignment and the clock reflection give the same total as the other root of $T(c) = 5.364$, with the three- and five-dimensional shares exchanged; none of them is independent evidence for the total, and the exchanged assignment is not physically consistent and differs from the rule in composition (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}).
\item[(iv)] The relation rests on R1, R2 and R3 (since 19 November 2026 on Premise E and R3, see (viii)), none supplied by the series; the contact mechanism explains R3's value and does not derive it, and the de Sitter reading, which replaces R2 by democracy and fixes the unit, gives thickness $\tfrac{3\pi}{8}$ or $\tfrac{\pi}{4}$ and totals of $5.000$, $5.667$ or $4.333$, none within four standard deviations.
\item[(v)] The comparison is with one Planck column; the others give $5.322$ to $5.376$, and the ACT DR6 combination gives about $5.22$, below the floor of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(ii). If that lower value holds, no thickness in the family fits.
\item[(vi)] The relation needs residues $0$ and $4$ empty: with residue $4$ populated the ratio is about $6.16$ (\claimref{synthesis.lowest-causal-residues-zero-four-content}), and with both chiral ideals admitted at residues $0$ and $4$ the total is about $8.254$. With one spinor ideal the emptiness of residues $0$ and $4$ is equivalent to the pairing reading of mass in dimensions $n \equiv 2 \pmod 4$, where the reversion pairing on one ideal vanishes identically (\claimref{synthesis.reversion-pairing-vanishes-residues-zero-four}); in the lowest-causal window it is also what anomaly freedom of a single ideal gives at $(1,1)$, and at $(1,5)$, $(5,1)$ under the reading (G6) that a tower's own gauge fields live on its full six-dimensional space and act on its constituents (\claimref{synthesis.residues-zero-four-real-chirality-anomaly}). The series adopts the single chiral ideal at residues $0$ and $4$ with the pairing reading of mass as a costed clause of the constituent definition, chosen knowing that the unit-ball fit needs it, and records (G6) as the general reading of where a tower's own gauge fields live, not as a postulate. The clause is part of what the relation rests on, beside R1 to R3. \emph{Note.} By Theorem \claimref{synthesis.invariant-forms-on-spinor-ideal-factor-through-reversion}, every spin-invariant bilinear form on a single spinor ideal is a linear functional of the reversion pairing, so at residues $0$ and $4$ no other pairing exists that could carry a mass term: the clause chooses between the pairing reading and the momentum reading of mass, not among pairings. It remains a clause chosen with the fit known. \emph{Note (register, 2026-11-10).} The clause is now a registered premise, \claimref{dark-matter.single-ideal-at-residues-zero-and-four}, and so is the population of the odd towers, \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated}, which is inferred from the number the relation fits; the stored statements of the total assume both.
\item[(vii)] \emph{(Note, 2026-11-10: the two readings are alternatives.)} The de Sitter reading of (iv) and the unit-ball reading cannot be held together: Premise D with R1 and R2 forces one quantum per de Sitter ball (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.d-democracy-total-mass}, note; argued). D is the premise of the de Sitter reading only and is not counted with R1 to R3. R3's unit is each tower's own cell radius, so R2 also supplies the one length of the relation. \emph{(Superseded on 19 November 2026 by (viii).)}
\item[(viii)] \emph{(Note, 2026-11-19: the premises of the relation.)} By the decision of 19 November 2026 the relation rests on Premise E, equal mass per unit ball (\claimref{dark-matter.e-equal-mass-per-unit-ball}), and on R3 with E's one common length as its unit, in place of R1, R2 and R3; R1 and R2 are one model of E and are not needed (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}(ii)). Three things follow, each stated there. E with R3, for some length, holds exactly when the shares hold tower by tower, $\tfrac{4}{3}$ for each three-dimensional tower, $\tfrac{8}{3\pi}$ for each five-dimensional one and $1$ for the mirror, with the two thicknesses equal; so the premises restate the closed form's shares and add only that equality, which the total does not see, and a three-dimensional tower's share holds by the definition of the length (the same, (iii)). E holds in one length only (the same, (iv)); the democracy premise D is E at the de Sitter radius, given the stored reading of the sphere form (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dark-to-baryon-total-uses-r1-r2-and-d-only-as-mass-per-unit-ball}(ii)), so D with the thickness one de Sitter radius is the same law at that length and not a second law, and it cannot be held with E at another length. And E does not say what its length is: it is tied to this world's baryon spacing only in the particle model, through R1 and R2 together.
