The relation is fitted: a closed form, a consistent mechanism inside the sourcing rule, and one length, that of Premise E, which R3 takes as the thickness and a contact mechanism on four clauses of its own returns. Its tier is observation level on Premise E and R3 (on R1 to R3 until 19 November 2026; (viii)) and on the costed clause (vi), and it supersedes nothing in Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}: the mirror's share stays derived and the odd towers' shares stay underived, now with one candidate value each and its price stated.

**setting  (The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy).** The rule fixes how much matter each tower holds, and with it how much of the dark matter collides: the mirror's $18.6$ percent, a plasma once ionised inside a halo (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}), and the three-dimensional towers' $49.7$ percent, at composite strength within their sheets. Counted as a bound on what a galaxy cluster loses in a merger, the two add, to $68$ percent; Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(v) counts the three-dimensional towers alone. The companion note T14b \cite{ButlerT14b} tests these amounts where dark matter's collisions show, with a multi-species simulation code, a Bullet-like merger of two galaxy clusters, the assembly of a galaxy cluster from eight progenitors and a semi-analytic model of the Milky Way, deposited with its code, run outputs and films. It takes the amounts as given and runs them in two readings: as this paper writes them, and with the odd towers' matter collisionless, so that only the mirror collides, and it runs the exchanged assignment of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not} in the same way, since the two assignments differ in composition and not in the total. Its results are imported here.



**Conjecture 65 (Imported: the composition simulated).** \begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Quanta confined to a sheet scatter in two dimensions. Per unit of the mass gravity sees, the three-dimensional towers' matter at composite strength has a cross-section of $10^{8}$ to $10^{13}\ \mathrm{cm^{2}\,g^{-1}}$ in galaxy clusters, eight to thirteen orders of magnitude above the galaxy-cluster bounds, whatever its confinement scale: it is a fluid whose pressure acts within its sheets.
\item[(ii)] In the merger, with the three-dimensional towers' matter colliding, the subcluster's sheet fluid and mirror gas are held back with its gas, and with randomly oriented sheets the subcluster has no separate lensing peak. With the odd towers' matter collisionless, the subcluster keeps a lensing peak on its galaxies with its gas $355$ kpc behind, and its mass-to-light change, $0.82$, is consistent with the observed ratio $0.84 \pm 0.07$ \cite{Randall2008}, as cold dark matter's is; the cost is a puffier subcluster, with lensing contrast $4.7$ against cold dark matter's $7.5$.
\item[(ii$'$)] The exchanged assignment of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}, with the three-dimensional towers' matter colliding, fails in the same way: lensing contrast $1.1$, the subcluster's own mass $0.27$ of cold dark matter's, and galaxy-cluster ellipticity $0.49$ of cold dark matter's. With the mirror settled it is marginal, contrast $2.7$ and mass $0.44$.
\item[(iii)] In the assembled galaxy clusters, two seeds each, the projected ellipticity is $0.43$ of cold dark matter's with the three-dimensional towers colliding, both seeds below cold dark matter's, the sheet fluid forming a cored component rounder than the collisionless dark matter; with the odd towers' matter collisionless it is $0.77$, rounder, with one seed inside the range of the cold-dark-matter seeds, so not excluded.
\item[(iv)] In the Milky Way, the proton-only branch is excluded at the Sun. On the burning branch a helium mirror, cooling slowly and with helium-star supernovae giving $3.6$ to $7.3$ times our feedback per unit mass formed, fits the $2\sigma$ room for extra disk-like mass at the Sun under some normalisations of its gas, and fits the bulge only if the bulge holds over twice the mirror's proportional share of its dark mass. With the consistent yield, extra disk-like mass at the Sun below $5\ M_{\odot}\,\mathrm{pc^{-2}}$, or disk-like dark mass in the bulge volume below $1.7 \times 10^{9}\ M_{\odot}$, would exclude it. Neither has yet been made (the dark-matter paper's own refutation condition 6, the section ``The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper'', not the series' sixth refutation).
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Provenance:} \cite[Prop.~3.1, Cor.~3.4, Obs.~7.1, 7.2, 9.1, Tables~3, 4]{ButlerT14b}; the observations compared are those of \cite{Clowe2006, Randall2008}.



**Fact (imported) 66 (Imported: the composition simulated (external ingredients)).** The results from outside the series on which \claimref{dark-matter.imported-composition-simulated} draws, as cited there: S. W. Randall, M. Markevitch, D. Clowe, A. H. Gonzalez, and M. Bradac, Constraints on the self-interaction cross section of dark matter from numerical simulations of the merging galaxy cluster 1E 0657-56, Astrophys. J. 679 (2008), 1173--1180.; D. Clowe et al., A direct empirical proof of the existence of dark matter, Astrophys. J. Lett. 648 (2006), L109--L113..

*Provenance:* S. W. Randall, M. Markevitch, D. Clowe, A. H. Gonzalez, and M. Bradac, Constraints on the self-interaction cross section of dark matter from numerical simulations of the merging galaxy cluster 1E 0657-56, Astrophys. J. 679 (2008), 1173--1180.; D. Clowe et al., A direct empirical proof of the existence of dark matter, Astrophys. J. Lett. 648 (2006), L109--L113..

**setting  (The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy).** What follows for this paper is that the amounts survive. With the odd towers' matter collisionless the composition passes the Bullet Cluster, under either assignment, and is not excluded by the shapes of galaxy clusters. Read at composite strength, as Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what} reads it, the composition fails both, and it fails them because of that self-interaction, not because of the amounts. That reading is a premise of T14b, not derived here or there: it is a statement about the three-dimensional towers' couplings and the strength with which their lightest stable state interacts, which the series does not fix (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}). The mirror's exposure is the same in both readings and is refutation item 6's: (iv) turns the dark-disk test into two measurements not yet made. The simulations are idealised, with one merger geometry, $80{,}000$ particles per merger and two seeds per galaxy cluster, and they compare compositions; their absolute numbers are not predictions.

\emph{The density the rule assigns excludes composite strength as well.} On a sheet the number per unit area and the expansion rate both fall as $a^{-2}$ in radiation domination, so for two-dimensional s-wave annihilation, $\sigma_{1}v = 2P/m$ with $P$ the absorption probability per encounter, the ratio of the annihilation rate to the expansion rate does not fall, and the depletion runs on logarithmically to the present. A symmetric relic annihilating at full strength, $P = 1$, into lighter states of its own tower, which a confining tower has (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}, the Goldstone clause), is left at about $3 \times 10^{-14}$ of the density R2 assigns; a relic whose number is fixed at the descent, interacting too weakly to annihilate, keeps it. This is an order-of-magnitude estimate at the mean density on R1 and R2, script-checked in floating point (the section ``Machine verification'', Part N). It does not say why the odd towers' matter is free; it says that the density the rule assigns, like the galaxy-cluster record, needs it to be nearly so.

## The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper



**setting  (The premises attacked, and what would refute this paper).** Each premise gets its own attack, run in the series' manner: state the strongest case against, and report what survives.

**setting  (P1 attacked: could there have been more than one descent, or more than one orientation?).** The case against the orientation clause: a flag is defined per rung (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}) and every tower has rungs, so nothing in the algebra forbids each tower its own choice; the clause is a statement about the event, not the algebra. What survives: the choice is initial data of the descent (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation}), a per-tower choice would be a second uncaused datum of one event, which P2 refuses, and the clause is used in exactly two places, neither of which fails without it: the internal statement of the fifteenth refutation (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation}), and the second route to Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(v), whose first route is P2's audit. Withdraw the clause and (v) stands by the audit, P4 reverts to a premise for the mirror, and the count is unchanged. Off the reversal orbit the clause cannot reach: over $\C$ there is no signature, an identification of two towers' ambients is fixed only up to a group whose two components exchange the two families, and ``the one flag'' would be defined only up to the bit at issue; hence the scoping in Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point}.

The case against the rest: nothing in the transport principle forbids multiple division events, and a multi-descent history would give each tower its own $\Lambda$, expansion and asymmetry, dissolving the count, the ratio and the universality at once. What survives: the vacuum theorem derives one frozen point per metric, and there is observationally one metric, a second gravity being excluded by the equivalence-principle tests \cite{ButlerT6}; and the dating Observation locates the descent at the origin, a later descent being a second big bang inside this one's classical era, for which the record shows nothing \cite[Obs.~7.4]{ButlerT12}. P1 is supported, not proved; a pre-descent theory of the freezing (the capstone's declared open problem) would settle it. Its reading as a sourcing rule (Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones}) is a further choice inside P1, the one that makes the count and the audit consistent; a different rule would have to be stated and both re-run.

**setting  (P2 attacked: is indifference a law?).** The case against: indifference is a selection rule on initial data, exactly where the trichotomy locates the framework's freedom, and might forbid the very contingency initial data is. The response is the instrument's audit, inherited from the families paper: in every use in a proof indifference equates along exact symmetry orbits and refuses selections with no distinguished member, and never legislates the \emph{values} of genuine moduli ($N$, $\epsilon$, the bit), which it classifies as data; it constrains the \emph{shape} of the pre-descent state, not its \emph{coordinates}. Here P2 counts both irreducible modules where the centre splits (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mode-counts-two-modules-which-one}(ii)); it does not equate the asymmetries of towers off the reversal orbit, and it does not sterilise $r = 0$ by refusing a flag there, which would refuse every residue (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.flag-insensitive-residue-no-flag-level}). P2 is a principle with a record of successful use (the families' democracy, the fuzzy sphere's principality), not a theorem; its failure mode is refutation item 1.

**setting  (P3 attacked: why the nearest dimension?).** The case against: every residue has a causal member once the dimension is free (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}(i)), so the count five belongs to one window, and P3 picks it. What survives: P3 reads P1 (one metric) as a minimality condition, each tower as close to the visible four as its residue allows, and it is the only natural window returning the two-sterile answer; clause (iii) records what every other window gives, so the dependence is exposed. What does not survive: any claim that the fixed-point coincidence $\{0, 4\} = \{r : r \equiv -r\}$ is structural; it belongs to the window. A derivation of tower dimension from the descent (the wall's dimension in the sheaf paper is where it would come from \cite{ButlerT4}) would retire P3.

**setting  (P4 attacked: is the asymmetry democratic?).** The case against was that the asymmetry per mode depends on the tower's charge structure, which differs by residue, and nothing equates functions of inequivalent structures. The attack succeeds, and P4 is scoped to what survives it: it is a theorem for the mirror (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(v), by the audit along the orbit or by the copy with P1's one orientation, the paper carrying both routes), void for the odd residues (Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-can-carry-arrow}: nothing there to be democratic about), and a premise only on the two-time fork. The declared remainder: what remains of P4 is not used in any proof the paper relies on.

**setting  (Hypothesis (U) attacked: are the sectors' eigenvalues equal at the ceiling?).** The case against: the families paper supplies a direction and states that the magnitude was never its physical content, each sector carrying its own normalisation; and this paper needs the second-order breaking to depress the down-type and lepton eigenvalues relative to the up sector by $m_{t}/m_{b}$, where the families paper describes that breaking as what lifts the two light families. What survives: (U) is used only to value a scale, carried as a range whose ends are the two readings of (U) itself; nothing proved here depends on where in the range the ceiling lies, and the fragility bound of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-one-how-fragile}(ii) is quoted across the whole range. If (U) fails the ceiling is unvalued, not wrong: the rung scale becomes a free datum of the pre-descent theory, successor problem (2).

**setting  (P5 attacked: must the boundary modes return every conserved quantum number?).** The case against: the singularity paper's purity premise, written (U$_{\mathrm{T5}}$) here to keep it apart from Hypothesis (U) \cite[Premise~11.3(U)]{ButlerT5}, extends to every conserved quantum number of the infalling state, so the boundary mode space must return baryon number and Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}(iv) is false. What survives: (U$_{\mathrm{T5}}$) is purity of the state on $H_{L} \otimes H_{R}$, which says nothing about a global $\mathrm{U}(1)$ the Hamiltonian need not commute with at the wall, where the algebra degenerates and the rung $\mathrm{U}(1)$ of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.where-flag-defined-what-it-carries} (argued, not proved) has no reason to survive the ascent; and, decisively, the alternative makes every stellar hole in our tower a remnant or a non-Hawking emitter, against the imported flux. Read the other way, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall} is a constraint on the series: exact return of global charge would force the Hawking paper's interface premise or the singularity paper's exhaustiveness to fail in the saturated regime. If the series ever promotes exact return to a theorem, P5 reverses and Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall} with it; the count and the shares are insensitive to holes and do not move.

**setting  (R1 to R3 and D attacked: the unit-ball rule's premises).** The case against R1 (equal mass per quantum): the odd towers' quanta are composites of sectors whose confinement scales the series does not derive (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}), so nothing fixes their mass to ours; a factor two either way in a quantum's mass is a factor two in that tower's share, and no closed form survives it. What survives: Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.r1-against-series-mass-formulas} shows the series' mass formulas, compared in one normalisation, do not contradict R1, and the closed form's agreement is evidence for R1 at exactly the strength of Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule}(ii), one candidate in fourteen.

The case against R2 (one quantum per unit ball): this tower's filling is set by baryon number, a conserved charge the odd towers cannot carry (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}), so R2 asks a symmetric sector to be filled at a spacing a charge sets elsewhere; and its co-expanding reading (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.extra-direction-must-co-expand}) adds a comoving metre-scale direction to what must exist. What survives: the de Sitter reading replaces R2 by D at the one length the series has (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.democracy-de-sitter-radius-unit-ball}), at the price of a thickness the geometry then gets wrong.

The case against R3 (one cell radius per unshared direction, one side): it is the one number the whole relation turns on, the geometry's two computations of it give $\tfrac{3\pi}{8}$ and $\tfrac{\pi}{4}$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}), and no gravitational measurement can reach it (Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.thickness-not-gravitational-quantity}). The contact mechanism that returns it (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3}) is open to the same attack one clause at a time: contact and indivisibility are a hard-ball picture that nothing in the series gives an odd tower's quanta, and under contact the tower's matter beyond one cell radius sources nothing (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.scope-mechanism}); the side is postulated, the sign of $\omega$ being a label with no reason to become a direction of sourcing (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.what-s-uses-what-it-does}); the contact count does not compose across two nestings; and reciprocity is the equal-thickness assumption restated. What survives: each clause has a computed alternative that the observation excludes (the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3}), so the mechanism's choices are exposed rather than hidden; and the family's floor, $5.255$, holds whatever becomes of R3.

The case against D: it is P2 applied to inequivalent towers, exactly the equation P2's audit refuses (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}(c)). What survives: nothing in the section ``The dark matter ratio: the unit-ball rule'' rests on D; it is named as the one route by which the series' own length could enter, and it does not reach the observed value.

**setting  (What would refute this paper).** Seven ways, each with its cost. Item 6 is the standing test, where the derivation meets the record and the record is already pressing; item 7 is its gravitational-wave form, unconditional in class and model-dependent in rate; item 2 is an unconditional running experiment; item 4 is bounded by the deposit interval; item 5 is conditional on an unsolved successor problem; items 1 and 3 are theory-internal risks. That the odd towers are empty, or that no consistent value of their relic densities supplies the remainder, does not refute this paper, which does not derive the remainder (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}(iii)); the capstone carries both as conditions on the programme \cite{ButlerT17}. The unit-ball rule's own exposures follow the six and refute the rule, not the paper.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Exhibit caused, tower-distinguishing structure in the pre-descent state, and P2 fails where most depends on it: the mirror's equivalence to this tower and the family ordering fall together (this paper and the families paper share the weakness).
\item Detect dark matter directly or indirectly at any strength above the gravitational floor, and the superselection is breached: Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable} is refuted outright, and with it the exact stability, the gravity-only seeding, and both conditional null stances.
\item Break the superselection argument itself: find a frozen-phase local operator that changes signature. The stability guarantee goes, though not the population count.
\item Exhibit one exactly massless dark species at a temperature above the thresholds of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.open-channels-their-costs}, and the radiation audit fails against the measured $\Neff$. Within the interval of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number} an excess reads first as a deposit hotter than the paper's estimate, the deposit energy being a datum the framework does not fix; it refutes item 2 only above the cap's own limit on the deposit, $T'/T_{\gamma} = 0.448$ for the mirror, where a species must have been heated by something other than gravity.
\item Derive the tower dimensions from the descent and find a window other than P3's, and the count of causal residues moves with the census of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}(iii); the asymmetric share moves only if a new even causal residue appears at a dimension divisible by four (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}): the windows that make $r = 0$ and $r = 4$ causal do so at $(1,1)$ and $(1,5)$, which carry no bit, so on those windows the asymmetric share is unchanged.
\item \emph{The standing test.} Find the mirror's share other than one. Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one} makes it a theorem, so this refutes P2 for the mirror, the descent's single ceiling, or the charge reading of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}. With the remainder of the ratio underived, this is the one place the paper's derived content meets the record, and the record is already pressing on it.

\emph{Already in tension:} the share is $18.6$ percent of the dark matter in cold, dissipative mirror baryons, and the record's analysis of mirror dark matter's galactic structure allows at most $0.8\,\Omega_{b}$ at low mirror temperature \cite{RouxCline2020}, a fifth below the prediction, which is a quarter above it. That analysis rescales the mirror's baryon-to-photon ratio as $\eta' = \eta\,\beta/x^{3}$ and includes a helium mirror down to $x = T'/T = 10^{-3}$, which covers the hot end of the framework's interval; it does not include the interval's floor, where the framework's mirror, whose $\eta'$ runs from about $0.008$ to $26$, reaches forty billion times ours, and is helium throughout on the expected branch (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}), nor the supernova energy of helium stars, which the companion note T14b finds to be $3.6$ to $7.3$ times ours per unit mass formed \cite{ButlerT14b}.

The dark-disk test runs in two phases. In the \emph{diffuse phase} mirror gas accreted into a halo is shock-heated to the virial temperature and ionised, a fluid with a mean free path of order ten parsecs ($\sigma/m \sim 10^{8}\ \mathrm{cm^{2}\,g^{-1}}$); this is what the Bullet Cluster's bound on the subcluster's mass loss, about $23$ percent and inferred from the mass-to-light ratios, tests \cite{Randall2008}; read as a bound on the dark matter, $18.6$ percent passes, and simulated, a merger in which only the mirror collides gives a mass-to-light change of $0.82$, consistent with the observed $0.84 \pm 0.07$, as cold dark matter's is (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-composition-simulated}(ii)). Its radiative cooling time is of order ten gigayears at the virial radius and a gigayear or less inside the cooling radius, where $n \gtrsim 10^{-3}\ \mathrm{cm^{-3}}$, so, as with our baryons, the inner gas radiates its support within a Hubble time and the outer gas does not. In the \emph{settled phase} what cools forms a mirror disk or compact objects of total mass up to $\Omega_{b'} \approx \Omega_{b}$; a thin disk of the visible disk's mass, about $50\ M_{\odot}\,\mathrm{pc^{-2}}$ locally, exceeds the Gaia thin-dark-disk bounds of a few $M_{\odot}\,\mathrm{pc^{-2}}$ \cite{Schutz2018, Widmark2021} by an order of magnitude, so the settled phase must not be a thin disk. A thick or fragmented component, or mirror feedback holding gas in the diffuse phase, avoids the bound; fragmentation into compact objects is closed by the microlensing limits (compact objects at most $1$ percent of the halo from $1.8 \times 10^{-4}$ to $6.3\ M_{\odot}$ and $10$ percent up to $860\ M_{\odot}$ \cite{Mroz2024}) unless the objects form outside that window. The phase split, the star-formation efficiency, the settled gas's fate and whether helium composition delays settling are the model; the record's mirror-galaxy analysis \cite{RouxCline2020} models them with a rescaled $\eta'$ and a helium mirror at the hot end of the interval, not at its floor; Randall et al.'s mass-to-light ratios \cite{Randall2008} are the observation behind the mass-loss bound, and Clowe et al.'s offset \cite{Clowe2006} behind the lensing test. Model-dependent, so on the burning branch a tension and not an exclusion: this is the framework's one theorem-level dark-matter prediction, and it is at the edge of the record already. The companion note T14b models the settled phase in the Milky Way (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-composition-simulated}(iv)): the proton-only branch is excluded at the Sun, and the burning branch's helium mirror, cooling slowly and with helium-star feedback, is open at the Sun under some normalisations of its gas and in the bulge only if the bulge holds over twice the mirror's proportional share of its dark mass, so that either of two measurements not yet made would decide it. Neither measurement can yet be made from published results. The tightest Gaia bound on a thin dark disk, about $5\ M_{\odot}\,\mathrm{pc^{-2}}$ at $95$ percent \cite{Widmark2021}, holds only for scale heights of $50$ pc or less, and at the $100$ to $500$ pc the test needs the bound is weaker; the dynamical models of the bulge \cite{Portail2017} measure its dark mass, about $3.1 \times 10^{9}\ M_{\odot}$, as halo-like, and do not separate a disk-like part from the stars' mass-to-light ratio. The test therefore needs two analyses not yet made: a vertical-dynamics or phase-space-spiral analysis of Gaia data with a dark disk of that thickness, and a bulge model with a disk-like dark component. Until then the Galaxy test is registered, not run.
\item \emph{Dark neutron-star mergers.} The copy theorem makes the mirror sector baryonic, dissipative and at the proton mass, so it forms stars, and its neutron stars have our equation of state: the same maximum mass and the same tidal deformability $\Lambda(M)$. The prediction is a class of compact-binary mergers in the ground-based gravitational-wave band with component masses of one to two solar masses, $\Lambda$ in the range of ordinary neutron stars, and no electromagnetic counterpart at any depth or distance. Nothing in the standard model produces that class, and primordial black holes in the mass range have $\Lambda = 0$. The rate is model-dependent: the mirror sector is helium-dominated on the expected branch (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}) and helium star formation has no calibrated efficiency, so the natural expectation is within an order of magnitude of the visible binary-neutron-star rate, tens to hundreds per cubic gigaparsec per year, and the framework fixes the class and the deformability, not the number. GW190425, with no counterpart and its deformability unconstrained, is the class's example event. Refutation: a large sample of neutron-star-mass mergers whose counterpart fraction is consistent with an all-visible population bounds $\Omega_{b'}$ below $\Omega_{b}$ and refutes Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-assembled} as item 6 does; confirmation is a counterpart fraction well below the visible population's with $\Lambda$ consistent with our equation of state, which third-generation detectors will measure for hundreds of events.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{The unit-ball rule's exposures} (the section ``The dark matter ratio: the unit-ball rule''; these cost the rule and Premises R1 to R3, not the paper). (a) A combined measurement of $\Omega_{\mathrm{dark}}/\Omega_{b}$ settling below the floor $5.255$ of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(ii) by more than its error excludes every thickness at once; the ACT DR6 combination's $5.22$ is already there if it holds. (b) A sharper measurement leaving $c = 1$ outside the window of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(iv) excludes R3 and the mechanism behind it; one leaving both windows excludes the exchanged assignment too and leaves a fitted $c$ with no geometric name. (c) The self-interacting fraction (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(v)): at composite strength it is about a half at $c = 1$, and simulated, that composition fails the Bullet Cluster and the shapes of galaxy clusters (the section ``The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy''); with the odd towers' matter collisionless the colliding fraction is the mirror's $18.6$ percent, the composition passes the merger and is not excluded by the shapes, and a colliding fraction measured well away from it excludes that reading whatever the total does. Under that reading the fraction no longer separates the rule from the exchanged assignment (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}). (d) The sheets' displacement (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress}): if the three-dimensional towers' sheets are held fixed by a stress the series does not name, the linear spectra exclude them (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.sheet-family-linear-spectra}) and no thickness reaches $4.364$ from the five-dimensional towers alone; if they move, their halos are cold dark matter's when their matter is collisionless (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.halo-built-collisionless-sheets-cold-dark}) and a cored fluid at composite strength (the section ``The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy''), and their exposure is the fraction test of (c) and, on the deposit branch, an in-plane free-streaming cutoff. Against these stands the caveat of Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule}(ii): the closed form is one of fourteen equally simple candidates in the error bar, found by solving for the target, so the value alone is not evidence; the floor is, and the fraction is evidence about the three-dimensional towers' couplings.

Stabilising two-time dynamics opens a fork onto an oriented Kleinian Standard Model (Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-can-carry-arrow}) with an asymmetric share of one: on the fork the asymmetric ratio is two on the two equalities Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-can-carry-arrow} names as premises there. The fork is a theoretical one (two-time dynamics has no stable formulation on the record) and is carried as such.

**setting  (The predictions collected).** Everything the paper predicts, in one place, with its level and its test; this paper's refutation conditions 6, 7, 2, 3 and 4 are the sharp ends of rows 1, 2, 7, 8 and 9, and the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress} carries the rule's costs. Rows marked \emph{rule} rest on R1 to R3 at observation level; rows marked \emph{theorem} on P1, P2 and the copy; \emph{stance} rows are the conditional stances of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}.

Inherited and not this paper's: $w = -1$ exactly (the frozen point, \cite[Prop.~4.2]{ButlerT10}), and the partner sector below the ceiling (the Standard-Model paper's unification argument \cite{ButlerT12}). Not predicted: any primordial gravitational-wave background from the descent, whose scale puts the signal above $10^{5}$ Hz; any accelerator cutoff, the only high-energy scale being the ceiling; and the ladder of states a micro-electronvolt apart that momentum along a five-dimensional tower's extra direction produces, which is real on the sourcing rule and gravity-only.

**setting  (The levels of the results).** \emph{Proved in this paper, at the standard of \cite{ButlerT1}, given the imported Facts:}
\begin{itemize} 
\item the residue census, its window sensitivity and the sort given P3 (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}); the flag's insensitivity (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.flag-insensitive-residue-no-flag-level}); type by residue and the intrinsic groups (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.type-residue-what-each-tower-can}); the residue as a soldered invariant (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.residue-soldered-invariant-not-algebraic-one});
\item exact superselection given Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.frozen-phase-operators} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}(1));
\item the mirror tower as a copy of this one, and its share of one given P1, P2 and the copy, to half a percent on the burning branch (Theorems \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}, \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-assembled}); the mode counts and the convention-dependent weightings (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mode-counts-two-modules-which-one}); the one-loop fragility bound under its stated assumptions (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-one-how-fragile});
\item the two families as the two chiralities, the flag bit exactly in the even residues at P3, and which towers can carry the arrow (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-families-are-two-chiralities}, Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}, Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-can-carry-arrow});
\item the asymmetry as a charge (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}); the $\theta$ no-go's degree count (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation}); the CPT-covariance classification of the orientation, given the covariance of the listed structures, Hypothesis (S) and, for the final step, the exhaustiveness of the trichotomy, which its clause (4) makes conditional on the promotion of the frozen norm (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-trichotomy-descents-data}, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation}; status now, 7 November 2026: the stored proposition, since its amendment, uses the premise that the flag is a datum in place of the exhaustiveness);
\item the projection lemma given the sourcing rule, the $\Delta\Neff$ arithmetic and thresholds, and the ceiling range's arithmetic (Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones}, Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}, \claimref{dark-matter.open-channels-their-costs}, \claimref{dark-matter.ceiling-valued-inherited-success});
\item the infall routing, given P1, P5, the imported flux and Facts \claimref{dark-matter.imported-schwinger-discharge-charged-hole} and \claimref{singularity.rate-models-kinematic-inputs-l-u}, its Gauss-law clause limited to holes below about $1.3 \times 10^{5}$ solar masses (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall});
\item the seeding floor given the imported rate, the coefficients as species counts, and the yield's shortfall against the baryon number (Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.seeding-gravity-floor-dark-towers-temperature}, \claimref{dark-matter.coefficients-floors-they-give-what-gravity});
\item for the unit-ball rule, given R1 to R3 or D where stated: Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.nesting}, \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}, Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.direction-nesting-adds-algebra}, Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3} (given its four clauses), \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}, \claimref{dark-matter.two-roots-are-chord-arc-quarter}, \claimref{dark-matter.democracy-de-sitter-radius-unit-ball}, \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}, \claimref{dark-matter.sourcing-rule-forced-not-chosen}, Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.thickness-not-gravitational-quantity}, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress}(i), (ii) given Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-linear-momentum-equation-traceless-field}.
\end{itemize}

\emph{Argued at structural level, not proved:} that the series supplies no stress holding the three-dimensional towers' sheets, so that they move (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress}, last clause), and that a halo built from them is cold dark matter's given cold initial conditions and collisionless matter (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.halo-built-collisionless-sheets-cold-dark}); that no coupling rule for the odd towers follows from the series (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}); the mirror's nucleosynthesis beyond its arithmetic, with the burning branch and the $0.995$ that rests on it, and the settling of mirror gas in refutation item 6, both at assembly level; steps (2) and (3) of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}, imports and their conjunction; the rung-level abelian charges of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.where-flag-defined-what-it-carries}, on which Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation} and \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what} depend; the audit's confinement clauses.

\emph{Hypotheses stated and carried:} the frozen phase's sector decomposition (Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.frozen-phase-operators}); the reading of the Standard-Model paper's inputs (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inputs-standard-model-chain}); that paper's Hypothesis (S), behind Facts \claimref{dark-matter.imported-derived-generation-flag} and \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inputs-standard-model-chain}, so that Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(i) holds under it; the eigenvalue universality behind the ceiling (Hypothesis (U), Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.ceiling-valued-inherited-success}); the labelling convention of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}, on which the named-member type attributions and the weightings of Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mode-counts-two-modules-which-one} depend.

\emph{Imported with provenance:} Facts \claimref{dark-matter.imported-eight-towers-mod-8-clock} to \claimref{dark-matter.imported-derived-generation-flag}, \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inputs-standard-model-chain}, \claimref{dark-matter.imported-schwinger-discharge-charged-hole}, \claimref{singularity.rate-models-kinematic-inputs-l-u}, \claimref{dark-matter.imported-frozen-point-de-sitter-radius}, \claimref{dark-matter.imported-induced-equations-hypersurface}, \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inverse-square-law-short-range}, \claimref{dark-matter.imported-linear-momentum-equation-traceless-field} and \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate}, the last four and Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-schwinger-discharge-charged-hole} from outside the series; the foundations paper's connectedness criterion and one-bit corollary, the latter under Hypothesis (S) (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.forks-cost-restated-causal-criterion-theorem}); Witt's and Polyakov's theorems; the survey record, refreshed at submission.

\emph{Premises, stances and observations, flagged:}
\begin{itemize} 
\item P1 to P5, each attacked above, the fifth deciding the infall trichotomy against exact return of global charge; R1 to R3 and D, attacked above and costed in the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress};
\item the population count, its one share and its symmetric remainder (observation level on the premises); that the odd towers are populated at all, inferred from the observed remainder within the framework and not derived (Observation \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-are-populated});
\item $\epsilon$ as initial data of the descent (a stance, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation});
\item the remainder, underived (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}(iii)) and read by the unit-ball rule at observation level on R1 to R3, found by fitting, with the one length fixed by R3 and returned by a contact mechanism on four clauses, and the look-elsewhere counts stated (Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule});
\item the temperature floor (the section ``Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows'', proved given the imported graviton rate, with the coefficients as species counts) and the deposit energy above it (a datum, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number});
\item the three conditional stances (direct-detection nulls, no indirect signals, Standard-Model $\Neff$, the last conditional on Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.open-channels-their-costs} and on the deposit energy); the ceiling's conditionality and range; the dimmed-burst observable, unconditional on the charge regime; the two-time instability import, carried as a fork.
\end{itemize}

**setting  (Successor problems).** Six open and one closed, numbered as in the capstone's inventory; its (0), the charge-saturated hole, is discharged by P5 and Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}.

(1) \emph{The odd towers' content:} the odd-dimensional Fock ideals, their mass formulas and gauge algebras, not used in any proof here, are to be established at their proved levels; the parity theorem and the seeding floor are this paper's (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}, the section ``Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows'').

(2) \emph{The pre-descent theory:} P1's residue, with two more entries the count depends on, the descent's reheating temperature, which the temperature floor is a function of, and the energy with which the descent deposits each tower's charge, which sets the temperature above the floor (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}).

(3) \emph{The coupling rule:} the relation between an odd tower's dimensionful coupling and this tower's at the ceiling, which nothing derived in the series supplies; it alone stands between Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}(iii) and a computed contribution from the odd towers, and the unit-ball rule constrains it (the odd towers' densities per unit of our volume) without supplying it. Two questions travel with it as conditions on the programme: whether a tower can be populated at all without the flag bit, and whether any consistent value of the odd towers' relic densities supplies the remainder. One signpost, conditional on two antecedents this paper does not assert: if the remainder is the odd towers' and they are relics of the gravitational seeding of the section ``Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows'' rather than of the deposit, the yield's $T_{\mathrm{desc}}^{3}$ scaling and the requirement that the produced quanta be no heavier than the bath put $T_{\mathrm{desc}}$ above about $2 \times 10^{12}$ GeV, excluding the lower end of the ceiling range, and the odd towers' lightest stable state near $10^{5}$ GeV at its top.

(4) \emph{The burst dimming factor:} promote the late-evaporation counting of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}, unconditional on the charge regime, from a remark to a spectrum prediction for burst searches, once (1) supplies the degrees of freedom.

(5) \emph{The partner question, closed in the negative} in the Standard-Model paper: under the sector decomposition no field of another tower carries a component of this tower's hierarchy bundle, so every other tower contributes zero to this tower's one-loop coefficients at every scale, and the mirror's running, a copy of ours, carries a copy of whatever resolves the desert here \cite[Prop.~8.5, Cor.~8.6]{ButlerT12}. The zero rests on the sector decomposition and on that paper's theorem that this tower's gauge fields are connections on its hierarchy bundle \cite[Prop.~5.7]{ButlerT12}, and holds for any content in the other sectors without Hypothesis (S); the mirror coefficients hold under (S), as Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(i) does. What remains is this tower's pair structure, which that paper takes up.

(6) \emph{The window:} derive the tower dimension per residue from the descent and retire P3; note that the odd residues' exclusion from the asymmetric share is window-free (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}(i)), while the even residues' inclusion is P3's, so a derived window moves the asymmetric share only by admitting an even causal residue at a dimension divisible by four.

(7) \emph{The thickness:} everything in the unit-ball rule turns on R3, one cell radius of the direction we cannot see, on one side. The contact mechanism of the section ``The thickness: a contact mechanism'' returns that value on four clauses chosen with it known, its contact count does not compose across two nestings, and it makes the tower's matter beyond one cell inert to gravity (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.scope-mechanism}); the two standard reductions give $\tfrac{3\pi}{8}$ and $\tfrac{\pi}{4}$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}); and gravity cannot measure the quantity (Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.thickness-not-gravitational-quantity}). What would settle it is a rule for the descent's deposit: the one such rule P2 licenses, equal filling per rung on the shared wall, gives every share one and $5.000$, so the rule needs democracy in each tower's own cells, which is R2 and R3 together. With it travel a reason for equal filling (a conserved number a symmetric sector can carry, fixed at the descent in every tower alike, or a democratic principle at the level of the descent measure) and, for the three-dimensional towers, a derivation of the sheets' displacement from the towers' own dynamics rather than from momentum conservation alone (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress}), and the galaxy-cluster bounds on self-interaction re-expressed for a cross-section per sheet (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.halo-built-collisionless-sheets-cold-dark}), which the companion note T14b has done. At composite strength the self-interacting fraction, a half at $c = 1$, a third at $\tfrac{\pi}{2}$, forty percent at the floor (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}(v)), fails the Bullet Cluster and the shapes of galaxy clusters whatever becomes of the total (the section ``The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy''); what remains open is a derivation of the odd towers' couplings that would make their matter collisionless, or not.

## Position in the series



**setting  (Position in the series).** This paper is T14 of the series. \emph{It relies on twelve predecessors, and on two later papers:}
\begin{itemize} 
\item the taxonomy paper T1 \cite{ButlerT1} for the clock and the standard of proof;
\item the tower paper T3 \cite{ButlerT3} for the eight residues and the rungs (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-eight-towers-mod-8-clock});
\item the sheaf paper T4 \cite{ButlerT4} for the wall displacement (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}(2), under its hypotheses);
\item the singularity paper T5 \cite{ButlerT5} for the ascend-then-descend structure and its exhaustiveness clause (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}, Fact \claimref{singularity.rate-models-kinematic-inputs-l-u});
\item the geometry paper T6 \cite{ButlerT6} for the single $\Lambda$ (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p1-single-frozen-point}) and, with T10, the de Sitter radius (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-frozen-point-de-sitter-radius});
\item the entropy paper T8 \cite{ButlerT8} for the indifference instrument (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference});
\item the twistor paper T9 \cite{ButlerT9} for the three-pair Witt decomposition (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-derived-generation-flag}), whose reading as the descent's flag is this paper's;
\item the dark-energy paper T10 \cite{ButlerT10} for the acoustic no-go and the frozen norm (Facts \claimref{dark-matter.imported-gravity-only-coupling}, \claimref{dark-energy.vacuum-region-registration-condition});
\item the mass paper T11 \cite{ButlerT11} for the two-constituent mass formula whose normalisation Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.r1-against-series-mass-formulas} compares;
\item the Standard-Model paper T12 \cite{ButlerT12} for the derived content whose chain Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one} reruns (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inputs-standard-model-chain}), for the trichotomy (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-trichotomy-descents-data}), for the listing choice under Hypothesis (S), and for the closure of the partner question (successor problem (5));
\item the Hawking paper T7 \cite{ButlerT7} for the greybody assembly and the imported flux behind P5 and Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall};
\item the companion note T14b \cite{ButlerT14b} for the simulations of the composition in galaxy clusters and in the Milky Way (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-composition-simulated});
\item the foundations paper T16 \cite{ButlerT16} for the connectedness criterion and the one-bit corollary that restate the fork's cost (Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.forks-cost-restated-causal-criterion-theorem});
\item the families paper T13 \cite{ButlerT13} for the audit of indifference and the within-sector alignment (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.ceiling-valued-inherited-success});
\item and, outside the series, the record's graviton-production rate (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate}), Schwinger discharge (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-schwinger-discharge-charged-hole}), the induced equations on a hypersurface (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-induced-equations-hypersurface}), the linear perturbation equations (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-linear-momentum-equation-traceless-field}) and the short-range test of the inverse-square law (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-inverse-square-law-short-range}).
\end{itemize}

\item the foundations paper T16 \cite{ButlerT16} imports the superselection of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}, the flag and arrow of the section ``The arrow counted'', and $\epsilon$'s status as a declared stance;
