## Machine verification



**setting  (Machine verification).** Every computational identity asserted in this paper at its own proof level, with the exceptions listed at the end, has been verified in exact arithmetic by the script \texttt{verify\_T14.py} \cite{ButlerVerifyT14}, which imports the series' shared library \texttt{GA.py}: exact rationals and arbitrary-precision integers, no machine floating point outside the two checks of Part N, an order-of-magnitude estimate labelled as floating point in its printed messages, the irrational constants entering only through rational brackets proved by integer inequalities. Two hundred checks, all passing, in fourteen parts keyed to statement tags; five are labelled as records rather than computations in their own printed messages (the $\theta$ no-go's degrees and homotopy table, the parity identity of Part J, the one-loop coefficient formula of Part H, and the coupling-departure illustration), and every witness the script relies on internally is counted in an aggregate check. Parts H, J and K use forty-digit decimal arithmetic for logarithms, exponentials and fixed points, the inequality chain itself being exact rational algebra. The script's printed labels give every bracket; the parts, with the statements they cover:

\emph{Part A} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.towers-are-residues-single-clock-filter}): the census over every signature of dimension at most ten, the reversal $r \mapsto -r$, the P3 representatives and the sterile set, and the window sensitivity (four windows, four answers).

\emph{Part B} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.flag-insensitive-residue-no-flag-level}): the isometry witnesses in $(1,3)$ and $(2,2)$, entry by entry, and the Jacobian of the isotropy equations.

\emph{Part C} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}): the survey ratio, its uncertainty and the remainder $4.364$.

\emph{Part D} (Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.asymmetry-arrow-counted-fifteenth-refutation}, \claimref{dark-matter.orientation-arrow-seventeenth-refutation}): the $\theta$ no-go's inputs as recorded data, and on $\Cl(1,1)$ the reflection that exchanges the null pair and preserves the form.

\emph{Part E} (Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.projection-every-towers-content-dilutes-ones}, Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}, \claimref{dark-matter.open-channels-their-costs}): the dilution exponents as a function of the sourcing rule for $D = 2, 3, 4$ and the single-sheet reading; the $\Neff$ coefficients, the thresholds $0.51$, $0.46$ and $0.465$, the entropy-release factor and the joint five-dimensional channels.

\emph{Part F} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.ceiling-valued-inherited-success}): the two valuations and the ratio of the ends.

\emph{Part G} (Theorems \claimref{dark-matter.type-residue-what-each-tower-can}, \claimref{dark-matter.residue-soldered-invariant-not-algebraic-one}, \claimref{dark-matter.mode-counts-two-modules-which-one}): for every $\Cl(p,q)$ of dimension at most five, the centre, a primitive idempotent by exhaustive search, the division algebra by exact rank, the component count and module dimension, agreeing with the classical table on all twenty signatures; the abstract agreements across residues; the module dimensions, the Witt indices and the weightings.

\emph{Part H} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-share-one-how-fragile}): the one-loop chain as exact rational algebra on a grid, the one-flavour factors at both ends of the ceiling range, and the coupling sensitivity with its ten-percent window.

\emph{Part I} (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}): the even-subalgebra isomorphisms for $(2,4)$, $(4,2)$, $(1,3)$, $(3,1)$, the matching types, the pair-count witnesses and the mirror threshold $0.448$.

\emph{Part J} (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.two-families-are-two-chiralities}, Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.flag-bit-exists-exactly-tower-dimension}, Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.population-count-one-share-half-percent}, \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}): the Fock ideals on the split forms of dimensions four, six and eight (the null-pair relations, the blade and top state, the chirality eigenvalues, the exchanging versors and their determinants), $I$ central in dimensions five and seven, the parity identity to dimension ten, the odd-dimensional Fock ideal at $m = 1, 2, 3$, the reversion form on the twistor ideal; and the mirror's cosmology at the paper's inputs: $\eta$ and $\eta^{1/3}$, the species count and the floor, the gravitational yield's shortfall, the hot deposit, the weak freeze-out, the bottleneck, the reheating, the mean-mass deficit, recombination, the cross-sections, cooling times and mean free path.

\emph{Part K} (the section ``Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows''): the floor prefactor and its scalings, the Boltzmann integral between Riemann sums, the nine-vector coefficient, the $\Neff$ shares, and the mirror share $1/5.364$.

\emph{Part L} (Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p5-boundary-modes-hold-entropy-not}, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}, Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-schwinger-discharge-charged-hole}): the baryon-emission mass, the burst and entropy ratios, $T_{\mathrm{H}}$ at one solar mass, and the Schwinger charge, its ratio to extremal and the crossover mass.

\emph{Part M: the unit-ball rule} (the section ``The dark matter ratio: the unit-ball rule''; Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.nesting}, \claimref{dark-matter.unit-ball-rule}, Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.equivalent-forms-assignment-not}, Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.direction-nesting-adds-algebra}, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3}, the table accompanying \claimref{dark-matter.contact-mechanism-returns-r3}, Remarks \claimref{note.dark-matter.scope-mechanism}, \claimref{note.dark-matter.common-thickness-curve-if-two-differ}, Propositions \claimref{dark-matter.total-function-thickness}, \claimref{dark-matter.two-roots-are-chord-arc-quarter}, \claimref{dark-matter.two-reductions}, Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.r1-against-series-mass-formulas}, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.sourcing-rule-forced-not-chosen}, Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.thickness-not-gravitational-quantity}, Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.sheet-family-linear-spectra}, Observation \claimref{dark-matter.status-rule}): one hundred and four checks, every measure an exact monomial $q\,\pi^{k}$ with $q$ rational; the measures, the hat-box identity and the Wallis integrals; the nesting relations and the four identities of Lemma \claimref{dark-matter.direction-nesting-adds-algebra} in $\Cl(1,4)$, $\Cl(1,3)$, $\Cl(4,1)$, $\Cl(3,1)$, with the reflection that exchanges the components; the closed form against the record; the equivalent forms; the arithmetic of the mechanism's stated counts (the slab as an exact Riemann sum, the downward count, O solved for the spacing, the alternatives, the section weight as a bracketed integral, the non-composition across the double nesting; the clauses themselves are postulated and not checked); $T(c)$ with its symmetry, floor, roots, windows and fractions; the de Sitter reductions; the clock reflection; the mass bound over the Gaussian rationals; the look-elsewhere count; the consistency numbers, with the stress identity, the metric feedback and the orientation average $\tfrac{2}{3}$ of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.reduced-response-gravity-needs-supporting-stress}.

\emph{Part N: the density R2 assigns} (the section ``The composition in galaxy clusters and in the Galaxy''): on a sheet the number per unit area and the expansion rate fall together in radiation domination ($a^{2}H$ constant at fixed $g_{*}$), and a symmetric relic annihilating at full strength is left at about $3 \times 10^{-14}$ of R2's density; floating point, order of magnitude.

What the script does not check, imported at the cited levels: the Boltzmann computation of Remark \claimref{note.dark-matter.sheet-family-linear-spectra} (CLASS, floating point, deposited as a record with its patch and inputs); Witt's theorem (witnesses only) and Polyakov's; the wall-displacement and acoustic no-go theorems; the two-time instability import; the CPT covariance of structures later papers add; the Goldstone and deposit-energy estimates (dimensional analysis in-proof); the homotopy groups (recorded); the Fock-module identification of the tower module (T3); the Standard-Model paper's chain (its inputs' reversal invariance is checked, not the chain); Chevalley's general criterion (not used); the graviton production rate and the standard thermal history the seeding formula assumes (the integral and the coefficients are checked); the thermalisation of a pure-glue tower's produced quanta; the dimensional-analysis step of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}; the dissipative-dark-matter bounds; the mirror's nucleosynthesis network and the settling of its gas beyond their arithmetic; the two spin-group isomorphisms of Corollary \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-can-carry-arrow}; the Schwinger field and the discharge mechanism (the scales built from them are checked); the exhaustiveness clause; and the survey values \cite{Planck2018}, refreshed at submission.

## Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows



**setting  (Seeding by gravity, and why no coupling rule follows).** Two results the body uses are collected here so that nothing used in a proof rests on an uncited document. The first is a computation, and what it computes is a floor; the second is a statement about what the series contains.



**Fact (imported) 67 (Imported: the massless-graviton production rate).** Matter that couples to the Standard-Model plasma through gravity alone is produced from it, at tree level, at a rate per volume
\[
R(T) \;=\; \frac{\alpha\,T^{8}}{\bar{M}_{P}^{4}}, \qquad \bar{M}_{P} = 2.435 \times 10^{18}\ \mathrm{GeV},
\]
with $\bar{M}_{P} = M_{P}/\sqrt{8\pi}$ the reduced Planck mass, the sum over the Standard-Model bath being inside the coefficient, weighted as $4|\mathcal{M}^{0}|^{2} + 45|\mathcal{M}^{1/2}|^{2} + 12|\mathcal{M}^{1}|^{2}$ over its scalar, fermion and vector degrees. Two published values of the coefficient for a produced real scalar are imported, with what each source states.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] \cite{Bernal2018}, equation (31) and Table I: $\alpha = 3997\pi^{3}/663552000 \approx 1.9 \times 10^{-4}$ for a scalar, and $1.1 \times 10^{-3}$, $2.3 \times 10^{-3}$ for a Dirac fermion and a vector, as the massless limit of that paper's massive spin-2 mediator. Its coupling is $h_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}/(2\bar{M}_{P})$ (its equation (3); the source writes $M_{P}$ for the reduced mass), its propagator that of a canonically normalised field (appendix, equation (17)), and its squared amplitudes (appendix, equations (25) to (27)) are one sixteenth of the canonically normalised ones (both without symmetry factors).
\item[(b)] \cite{CleryEtAl2022}, equations (86) and (90): $\alpha = 3997\pi^{3}/41472000 \approx 3.0 \times 10^{-3}$ for a scalar and $11351\pi^{3}/20736000 \approx 1.7 \times 10^{-2}$ for a Dirac fermion, with the coupling $-h_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}/\bar{M}_{P}$ of a canonically normalised field (its equation (2)) and the same rate integral. This is sixteen times (a).
\item[(c)] Both sources sum the fermion amplitude over both helicities and multiply by $45$, which weights each chiral fermion of the Standard Model as a Dirac fermion.
\end{enumerate}
The normalisation of (b) is the one that reproduces Newton's law, and the coefficients the store uses are those of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v), which derives the rate with the chiral fermions weighted once: $5789\pi^{3}/82944000 \approx 2.16 \times 10^{-3}$ for a real scalar, $16087\pi^{3}/82944000 \approx 6.01 \times 10^{-3}$ for a chiral fermion, $16087\pi^{3}/20736000 \approx 2.41 \times 10^{-2}$ for a real massless vector, counting creation events of two quanta each. The value (a), which the store used until 18 November 2026, is low by $46312/3997 \approx 11.6$ for the scalar. In its sources the rate is computed from the exchange of a quantum of a spin-2 field; the cited theorem obtains the same rate from the operator source with no state of the gauge field, and the two accounts agree at this order.

*Provenance:* N. Bernal, M. Dutra, Y. Mambrini, K. Olive, M. Peloso, and M. Pierre, Spin-2 portal dark matter, Phys. Rev. D 97 (2018), 115020.; S. Clery, Y. Mambrini, K. A. Olive, and S. Verner, Gravitational portals in the early Universe, arXiv:2112.15214..



**Theorem 68 (Seeding by gravity: the floor on a dark tower's temperature).** By exact superselection the towers interact only through the shared metric (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}), so after the descent a dark tower can be heated only gravitationally; what the descent itself deposited in it (its charge, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}, with whatever energy the deposit carried) is a datum, not a computation. Let the descent reheat this tower to $T_{\mathrm{desc}}$ and let tower $r$, with $g_{d}$ light degrees of freedom that thermalise among themselves, be heated by gravitational pair production from the visible plasma at the rate per volume $\Gamma = c\,T^{8}/M_{P}^{4}$ of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v) (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate} for the published values), under that theorem's hypothesis (OS), or on the standard account of the Fact, with $c$ the sum of the theorem's per-species coefficients over the tower's light content and $\Gamma$ counting creation events, each of two quanta. Then the produced comoving yield of events is
\[
Y \;=\; \frac{c\,T_{\mathrm{desc}}^{3}}{3\,\kappa_{s}M_{P}^{3}}, \qquad \kappa_{s} = \sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}g_{*}}{90}}\;\frac{2\pi^{2}}{45}\,g_{s} \;=\; 145.3 \ \text{at } g_{*} = g_{s} = 100,
\]
and the number of quanta per unit entropy is $2Y$.

\emph{(The mean energy per event.)} A creation event puts into the tower the energy $E_{1} + E_{2}$ of the incoming pair. In the rate integral of clause (v) of that theorem the angular weight of every channel is a constant times $s^{2} = 4E_{1}^{2}E_{2}^{2}(1 - \cos\theta_{12})^{2}$, so each incoming leg is weighted by $E^{3}$ times its occupation, independently of the other leg, and the mean energy of a leg is
\[
\frac{\Gamma(5)\,\zeta(5)}{\Gamma(4)\,\zeta(4)}\,T \;=\; \frac{4\,\zeta(5)}{\zeta(4)}\,T \;\approx\; 3.832\,T \ \ \text{(Bose)}, \qquad
\frac{\tfrac{15}{16}\cdot 24\,\zeta(5)}{\tfrac{7}{8}\cdot 6\,\zeta(4)}\,T \;=\; \frac{30\,\zeta(5)}{7\,\zeta(4)}\,T \;\approx\; 4.106\,T \ \ \text{(Fermi)},
\]
with $\zeta(4) = \pi^{4}/90$ and $1.0369277 < \zeta(5) < 1.0369278$. An event from a Bose pair therefore carries $8\,\zeta(5)/\zeta(4)\,T \approx 7.664\,T$ on average and an event from a Fermi pair $(60/7)\,\zeta(5)/\zeta(4)\,T \approx 8.212\,T$. For a produced species $j$ the mean energy per event $\bar{E}$ weights these two by the Bose and Fermi parts of $\alpha_{j}$ in clause (v), which are proportional to $\bigl(c_{j}(N_{s} + 12N_{v}) + 5\tau_{j}\sum_{i}\tau_{i}\bigr)/225$ and to $49\,c_{j}N_{w}/4800$; for a tower the parts are summed over its light content. So $7.66 < \bar{E}/T < 8.22$ for every bath and every content, and for a bath of the Standard Model's content $(N_{s}, N_{w}, N_{v}) = (4, 45, 12)$ with zero curvature couplings
\[
\bar{E} \;=\; 7.873\,T \ \text{(a real scalar)}, \qquad 7.890\,T \ \text{(a chiral fermion, or a real massless vector, or any number of them)}, \qquad 7.889\,T \ \text{(a copy of that content)}.
\]

\emph{(The floor.)} With $s/\rho = 4/(3T)$ at $g_{*} = g_{s}$ the energy fraction at production is $\tfrac43(\bar{E}/T)\,Y$, between $10.49\,Y$ and $10.52\,Y$ for those contents, and the temperature ratio it alone would give is
\[
\xi_{\mathrm{floor}} \;=\; \Big(\tfrac43\,\frac{\bar{E}}{T}\,Y\,\frac{g_{*}}{g_{d}}\Big)^{1/4} \;=\; 1.23 \times 10^{-3}\; c^{1/4}\Big(\frac{16}{g_{d}}\Big)^{1/4}\Big(\frac{T_{\mathrm{desc}}}{6 \times 10^{14}\ \mathrm{GeV}}\Big)^{3/4}
\]
for $g_{*} = g_{s} = 100$ at the reheating. The prefactor depends on the produced content only through $\bar{E}^{1/4}$: it lies between $1.225$ and $1.226 \times 10^{-3}$ for each of the contents above, and between $1.21$ and $1.24 \times 10^{-3}$ for any bath and content. This is a floor: the tower's bath cannot be colder, and it is hotter by whatever energy the deposit carried. The ratio thereafter evolves through the two sectors' separate entropy conservation, by
\[
\left(\frac{g_{d}^{\mathrm{seed}}}{g_{d}}\right)^{1/3}\left(\frac{g_{s}}{g_{s}^{\mathrm{seed}}}\right)^{1/3}
\]
at any later epoch. Two inputs are not fixed by the formula: $T_{\mathrm{desc}}$, the ceiling of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.ceiling-valued-inherited-success} or below it, so that across that range the floor spans a factor $(3.5 \times 10^{11}/6 \times 10^{14})^{3/4}$, about $4 \times 10^{-3}$ ($3.75 \times 10^{-3}$), the body quoting the top and saying so; and the deposit's energy, which sets the actual ratio above the floor.

*Proof.* With $dt = -dT/(HT)$,
\[
H \;=\; \sqrt{\frac{\pi^{2}g_{*}}{90}}\;\frac{T^{2}}{M_{P}}, \qquad s \;=\; \frac{2\pi^{2}}{45}\,g_{s}T^{3},
\]
in the reduced-mass convention the rate is quoted in (the familiar $1.66\sqrt{g_{*}}\,T^{2}/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}$ with the non-reduced $M_{\mathrm{Pl}} = 1.22 \times 10^{19}$ GeV is the same rate, $1.66 = \sqrt{8\pi}\sqrt{\pi^{2}/90}$), the Boltzmann equation $\dot{n} + 3Hn = \Gamma$ for the density $n$ of creation events (the rate of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v) (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate} for the published values) counts events, each of two quanta, so the density of quanta is $2n$), gravitational production being the only source (by \claimref{dark-matter.superselected-hence-dark-exactly-stable}), for the comoving yield of events $Y = n/s$ reads
\[
\frac{dY}{dT} \;=\; -\frac{\Gamma}{HTs} \;=\; -\frac{c\,T^{2}}{\kappa_{s}M_{P}^{3}},
\]
which integrates from $T_{\mathrm{desc}}$ downward to the stated $Y$, dominated by the top of the range (the script brackets the integral between Riemann sums of that integrand). \emph{The mean energy per event.} By the proof of clause (v) of that theorem the rate of a channel is $\int d\Pi_{1}\,d\Pi_{2}\,f_{1}f_{2}$ of $W_{ij}s^{2}/M_{P}^{4}$, with $d\Pi_{1}\,d\Pi_{2}$ proportional to $E_{1}E_{2}\,dE_{1}\,dE_{2}\,d\cos\theta_{12}$, $s = 2E_{1}E_{2}(1 - \cos\theta_{12})$ and $W_{ij}$ a constant for each pair of species (its clause (iv)); the integral over the angle gives $\tfrac{32}{3}E_{1}^{2}E_{2}^{2}$, so the integrand is a constant times $E_{1}^{3}f_{1}(E_{1})\,E_{2}^{3}f_{2}(E_{2})$ in every channel. An event removes the pair's energy $E_{1} + E_{2}$ from the bath and puts it into the produced pair, and $E_{1} + E_{2}$ does not depend on the angle, so its mean over events of a channel is the sum of the two legs' means with the weight $E^{3}f(E)$. With $\int_{0}^{\infty}E^{k}(e^{E/T} - 1)^{-1}dE = \Gamma(k+1)\zeta(k+1)T^{k+1}$ and $(1 - 2^{-k})$ times that for the Fermi occupation, the mean is $\Gamma(5)\zeta(5)/(\Gamma(4)\zeta(4))\,T = 4\zeta(5)/\zeta(4)\,T$ for a Bose leg and $\tfrac{15}{16}\cdot 24\,\zeta(5)/(\tfrac78 \cdot 6\,\zeta(4))\,T = (30/7)\zeta(5)/\zeta(4)\,T$ for a Fermi leg. In every channel the two incoming quanta are of one species, so an event carries $8\zeta(5)/\zeta(4)\,T$ or $(60/7)\zeta(5)/\zeta(4)\,T$ on average. The rate of species $j$ is the sum over the bath's channels, and in the bracket of $\alpha_{j}$ the term $(c_{j}(N_{s} + 12N_{v}) + 5\tau_{j}\sum_{i}\tau_{i})/225$ is the sum over the bath's bosons and $49c_{j}N_{w}/4800$ the sum over its fermions ($49/4800 = 3\,(7/8)^{2}/225$, the fermion's weight times the square of the ratio of the two $E^{3}$ moments); the mean energy per event is the mean of the two values with these weights, and for a tower with the weights summed over its light content. It lies between the two values, so between $7.66\,T$ and $8.22\,T$. With $\zeta(4) = \pi^{4}/90$ and $\zeta(5)$ bracketed by its partial sum to $60$ terms and the integral bounds on the tail ($1.0369277 < \zeta(5) < 1.0369278$), at the bath $(4, 45, 12)$ with zero curvature couplings the Bose and Fermi parts are $168/225$ and $2205/4800$ for a real scalar and $148c_{j}/225$ and $2205c_{j}/4800$ for a chiral fermion ($c_{j} = 3$) or a vector ($c_{j} = 12$), which give $\bar{E}/T = 7.873$ and $7.890$, and $7.889$ for the sum over four scalars, $45$ fermions and twelve vectors; a content of fermions and vectors alone has the fermion's value whatever their numbers. A scalar with a curvature coupling has $\tau_{j} \neq 1$ and a value between $7.66$ and $7.89$.

\emph{The floor.} The energy density $\rho_{d}$ put into the tower obeys $\dot{\rho}_{d} + 4H\rho_{d} = \bar{E}\,\Gamma$ while it redshifts as radiation, so with $\rho = (\pi^{2}/30)g_{*}T^{4}$ the fraction $\rho_{d}/\rho$ obeys the equation of $Y$ with the source multiplied by $\bar{E}/T$ and $s$ replaced by $\rho/T$: it is $(\bar{E}/T)\,(sT/\rho)\,Y = \tfrac43(\bar{E}/T)\,Y$ at $g_{*} = g_{s}$, the same as the energy $\bar{E}$ per event at $T_{\mathrm{desc}}$ times the yield. Then $\xi_{\mathrm{floor}}$ follows from $\rho_{d} = (\pi^{2}/30)g_{d}\xi^{4}T^{4}$, which presupposes that the produced quanta thermalise within the tower (true for the mirror's content; assumed for a pure-glue tower); the change of $g_{*}$ during production is neglected, as in the yield. The prefactor, referred to the top of the ceiling range of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.ceiling-valued-inherited-success}, is bracketed by the check at $c = 1$, $g_{d} = 16$: between $1.225$ and $1.226 \times 10^{-3}$ at each of the three values of $\bar{E}$, and between $1.21$ and $1.24 \times 10^{-3}$ at the Bose and Fermi ends. The deposited paper and the series script took energy $3T$ per event, which gives the prefactor $9.6 \times 10^{-4}$; the floor stated here is $(\bar{E}/3T)^{1/4}$ times that, a factor between $1.272$ and $1.274$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.coefficients-floors-they-give-what-gravity} evaluates the stated floor at the derived coefficients), with the two Planck-mass conventions confirmed to give one rate. Level: proved given the rate (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v) (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate} for the published values), under that theorem's hypothesis (OS), or on the standard account of the Fact) and the standard thermal history (the expansion law, the entropy density and the degree-of-freedom values, listed in the section ``Machine verification'' as assumed; the mean energy per event is computed above and no longer assumed).




**Theorem 69 (The coefficients, the floors they give, and what gravity cannot supply).** The production coefficients are those of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v) (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate} for the published values), under that theorem's hypothesis (OS), or on the standard account of the Fact: per creation event of two quanta, from a bath of the Standard Model's content with zero curvature couplings, $5789\pi^{3}/82944000 = 2.164 \times 10^{-3}$ for a real scalar, $16087\pi^{3}/82944000 = 6.014 \times 10^{-3}$ for a chiral fermion and $16087\pi^{3}/20736000 = 2.4055 \times 10^{-2}$ for a real massless vector. The floors are those of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.seeding-gravity-floor-dark-towers-temperature}, $\xi_{\mathrm{floor}} = (\tfrac43(\bar{E}/T)\,Y g_{*}/g_{d})^{1/4}$ with the mean energy per creation event $\bar{E}$ computed there from the rate integral: $7.890\,T$ for vectors and $7.889\,T$ for a copy of the Standard Model's content.

(i) For a five-dimensional tower whose light content is the nine vectors of its $\mathfrak{u}(3)$, eight gluons and the abelian photon, $c = 9 \times 2.4055 \times 10^{-2} = 0.2165$, $g_{d} = 18$, and $\xi_{\mathrm{floor}} = 8.1 \times 10^{-4}$ at the ceiling reheating; whether those are its light species is part of the coupling question of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.no-coupling-rule-odd-towers-follows}, and the value is the floor for that content, not for the tower.

(ii) For the mirror tower, whose content is the Standard Model's (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}), the species sum over four real scalars, forty-five Weyl fermions and twelve vectors gives $c = 1519247\pi^{3}/82944000 = 0.568$, each of the forty-five being chiral and weighted once (the earlier bracket between counting the fermions per Dirac and per Weyl species is closed by the derived weights), with $g_{d} = 4 + 45 \times \tfrac{7}{4} + 24 = 106.75$, hence $\xi_{\mathrm{floor}} = 6.6 \times 10^{-4}$ and, with the charge reading of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}, $\eta' = 2.1$ at the floor; $\eta'$ would fall below one at the floor only for a coefficient above about $1.5$ ($1.54$), which is $2.7$ times the mirror's.

(iii) The produced yield at the ceiling, $Y = 1.95 \times 10^{-14}$ creation events per unit entropy for the mirror, falls short of the $8.7 \times 10^{-11}$ baryons per unit entropy our baryon number requires by a factor of $4.4 \times 10^{3}$ (of $2.2 \times 10^{3}$ against the $2Y$ quanta); closing the gap would need $T_{\mathrm{desc}} = 9.9 \times 10^{15}$ GeV, $16.4$ times the ceiling, where the floor itself would be $5.4 \times 10^{-3}$, above the charge-domination crossover. That is $1.27$ times the hot-deposit value $4.2 \times 10^{-3}$ of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}, and no longer equal to it: at that temperature gravity supplies one creation event per unit of baryon number, and an event carries $7.9\,T$ where the hot deposit takes $3T$ per unit of baryon number. The hot deposit's $3T$ is a stipulated figure of order the reheating temperature, not a computed mean, so the ratio $1.27$ records the two counts and carries no further weight. Gravity heats the dark towers; it does not populate them with charge, which is the descent's.

(iv) The mirror's contribution to $\Neff$, $7.447\,\xi^{4}$, is $1.4 \times 10^{-12}$ at the floor, below $2 \times 10^{-12}$, and below $10^{-8}$ at the hot deposit of Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.stored-asymmetry-charge-not-number}; a nine-vector tower's, $2.202 \times 18\,\xi^{4}$ for eighteen light polarisations, the coefficient being half the two-polarisation $\Delta\Neff$ bracketed in Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}, is $1.7 \times 10^{-11}$ at its floor, below $2 \times 10^{-11}$. Both are far below any measurement.

*Proof.* Arithmetic on Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.seeding-gravity-floor-dark-towers-temperature} and the coefficients of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v) (Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate} for the published values), under that theorem's hypothesis (OS), or on the standard account of the Fact, script-bracketed, with $g_{d}$ counting two polarisations per vector, one per real scalar and $\tfrac{7}{8} \times 2$ per Weyl fermion. The coefficients are the theorem's closed form at the bath $(N_{s}, N_{w}, N_{v}) = (4, 45, 12)$ with zero curvature couplings, $\alpha_{j} = (\pi^{3}/17280)\,[(c_{j}(N_{s} + 12N_{v}) + 5\tau_{j}N_{s})/225 + 49c_{j}N_{w}/4800]$ with $c_{j} = 1, 3, 12$ and $\tau_{j} = 1, 0, 0$ for a real scalar, a chiral fermion and a real massless vector, which gives $5789\pi^{3}/82944000$, $16087\pi^{3}/82944000$ and $16087\pi^{3}/20736000$; $\pi$ is bracketed, not approximated. The floors are $\xi_{\mathrm{floor}} = (\tfrac43(\bar{E}/T)\,Y g_{*}/g_{d})^{1/4}$ of the seeding proposition, with its mean energy per creation event: the Bose and Fermi parts of the rate that weight it, $(c_{j}(N_{s} + 12N_{v}) + 5\tau_{j}N_{s})/225$ and $49c_{j}N_{w}/4800$, are the two terms of the bracket above, so $\bar{E}/T$ is between $7.889$ and $7.890$ for vectors and for the sum over the mirror's content ($7.8896$ and $7.8893$). (i): $c = 9 \times 16087\pi^{3}/20736000$, between $0.2164$ and $0.2165$, and the floor between $8.1$ and $8.2 \times 10^{-4}$ ($8.118 \times 10^{-4}$). The species summed in (ii) are the mirror's Standard Model content (by \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}): $c = (4 \times 5789 + 45 \times 16087 + 48 \times 16087)\pi^{3}/82944000 = 1519247\pi^{3}/82944000$, between $0.5679$ and $0.5680$, each chiral fermion weighted once, so the earlier bracket between a fermion coefficient per Weyl and per Dirac species is closed; the floor lies between $6.6$ and $6.7 \times 10^{-4}$ ($6.621 \times 10^{-4}$), $\eta' = \eta/\xi^{3}$ lies between $2.1$ and $2.2$ ($2.112$), and the crossover $\eta' = 1$, at the mirror's $g_{d}$ and energy per event, lies between $c = 1.539$ and $1.54$, between $2.7$ and $2.8$ times the mirror's $c$. The yield $Y$ of the seeding proposition counts creation events, each of two quanta. (iii): $Y$ lies between $1.94$ and $1.96 \times 10^{-14}$; $\eta$ times the photon-to-entropy ratio $n_{\gamma}/s = 0.1413$ against $Y$; the shortfall lies between $4.4$ and $4.5 \times 10^{3}$, and the temperature that would make it up inverts $Y \propto T_{\mathrm{desc}}^{3}$, a factor between $16.4$ and $16.5$; these count events and quanta and do not depend on the energy per event. The floor at that temperature has $\xi_{\mathrm{floor}}^{4} = \tfrac43(\bar{E}/T)\,(n_{B}/s)\,g_{*}/g_{d}$, since $Y = n_{B}/s$ there, which gives $\xi$ between $5.40$ and $5.41 \times 10^{-3}$; the hot-deposit value is $\xi^{4} = 4\,(n_{B}/s)\,g_{*}/g_{d}$, energy $3T$ per unit of baryon number against $\rho/s = \tfrac34 T$, $\xi$ between $4.2$ and $4.3 \times 10^{-3}$; their ratio is $(\bar{E}/3T)^{1/4}$, between $1.273$ and $1.274$. With energy $3T$ per event in the floor the two expressions were the same, which is why the earlier statement had them equal. (iv): $g_{r}$ light polarisations at $2.2018$ each, Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.open-channels-their-costs}; the mirror's value at its floor lies between $1.4$ and $1.5 \times 10^{-12}$ ($1.43 \times 10^{-12}$) and the nine vectors' between $1.7$ and $1.8 \times 10^{-11}$ ($1.72 \times 10^{-11}$), so the bounds $10^{-12}$ and $10^{-11}$ that held with energy $3T$ per event do not hold at the mean energy per event, and $2 \times 10^{-12}$ and $2 \times 10^{-11}$ do.




**Theorem 70 (Three obstructions to a coupling rule for the odd towers).** Three facts bear on a rule relating an odd tower's gauge coupling to this tower's. (a) In a tower of dimension $d \neq 4$ the gauge coupling is dimensionful, $[g^{2}] = \mathrm{mass}^{4-d}$, so a comparison with this tower's dimensionless coupling needs a length, and in five dimensions that length is the size of the fifth direction, which the sheaf paper's wall construction does not fix \cite{ButlerT4}. (b) No gauge coupling value is derived anywhere in the series: the Standard-Model paper takes $\alpha_{\mathrm{em}}$ and $\alpha_{s}$ from the record \cite[Fact 2.8, Prop.~6.1]{ButlerT12}. (c) The odd towers are inequivalent to this one as soldered algebras (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.residue-soldered-invariant-not-algebraic-one}) and lie off the reversal orbit, and neither premise that equates across towers reaches them: Premise \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference} equates sector labels, not couplings, and only along that orbit, and Premise \claimref{synthesis.common-ceiling-coupling-premise} equates the ceiling couplings of the two members of that orbit only (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(iv)).

*Proof.* (a): in Gauge Theory Gravity in $d$ dimensions the Yang-Mills density is $\det(\hb)^{-1}\langle\mathbf{F}^{2}\rangle_{0}$, with $\mathbf{F} = \hb^{T}(F)$ the physical field strength of the background bivector field $F = \tfrac12 F_{\mu\nu}\,e^{\mu}\wedge e^{\nu}$ (in components, a labelled reading: $\sqrt{|g|}\,g^{\mu\alpha}g^{\nu\beta}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\alpha\beta}$, the integrand of $F \wedge \star F$). Under $g \mapsto \lambda^{2}g$, that is $\hb^{-1} \mapsto \lambda\,\hb^{-1}$ and $\hb^{T} \mapsto \lambda^{-1}\hb^{T}$, the volume factor $\det(\hb)^{-1}$ scales as $\lambda^{d}$ and the outermorphism $\hb^{T}$ on bivectors as $\lambda^{-2}$, so $\langle\mathbf{F}^{2}\rangle_{0}$ scales as $\lambda^{-4}$, the action $\int\det(\hb)^{-1}\langle\mathbf{F}^{2}\rangle_{0}\,d^{d}x$ as $\lambda^{d-4}$, and $1/g^{2}$ carries the compensating dimension. (b) is a statement about the record the series takes its couplings from. (c) rests on Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.residue-soldered-invariant-not-algebraic-one} for the inequivalence, on \claimref{dark-matter.p2-indifference}, which equates sector labels along the reversal orbit only, on \claimref{synthesis.common-ceiling-coupling-premise}, which equates the ceiling couplings along that orbit only, and on Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}(iv) for the reversal orbit. The remark at the technical level, that any rule would be a further premise, is a reading of (a) to (c) and is not proved here.


## Dark matter and the dark towers





**Prediction 71 (Direct detection stays null).** Permanent direct-detection nulls: dark matter couples through gravity alone, at first order.



**Prediction 72 (Dimmed final evaporation bursts).** Dimmed final evaporation bursts: the hole radiates into every populated tower, so it loses mass faster than the standard expectation (this tower plus gravitons) by the factor $D = \Psi_{\mathrm{tot}}/\Psi_{0}$ of total to standard emission, and the visible burst of its last $\tau$ is the standard burst of its last $D\tau$ divided by $D$ (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.dimmed-burst-time-rescaled-copy}). Dimming by the full ratio holds for the primary high-energy tail and for the visible fraction of the power at fixed mass; the visible energy in a fixed final window is dimmed by $D^{2/3}$, and the temperature at a fixed time before the end is lowered by $D^{1/3}$. The populated towers are this one and, under P2, the mirror, whose light content copies ours (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}), so $D = (2 + g + o)/(1 + g)$, with $g$ and $o$ the gravitational and odd-tower emission relative to this tower's: $D \ge 2$ if and only if $o \ge g$, and $D = 2 - g/(1 + g)$ if the odd towers emit nothing. If the four odd towers hold matter, which Premise \claimref{dark-matter.odd-towers-populated} now postulates on the inference of Observation \claimref{dark-matter.which-towers-are-populated} from the observed dark remainder, then $o > 0$ and the dimming is larger. \emph{Scope note.} The time-rescaled copy assumes constant emission coefficients; if a five-dimensional tower is populated, its Kaluza-Klein levels are emission channels under the interface premise and the copy holds only for holes well above the crossover mass $M_{\times}$ of \claimref{synthesis.five-dimensional-tower-kaluza-klein-channels-steepen-burst}(ii): below it $M^{4} = 4B\tau$ and the primary high-energy tail steepens from $E^{-3}$ to $E^{-4}$, so a search for the final burst then tests that law, not the rescaled copy. The baseline ``this tower plus gravitons'' counts emission into the helicity-2 channels of the gravitational field as standard physics does, through the term $g$; the series holds no quantum of that field (conventions, section ``Gravity and the graviton''), and whether $g$ is kept is part of open problem op.graviton-rate-as-classical-field-process ($g = 0$ gives $D = 2 + o$), the prediction being unchanged until that is settled.



**Prediction 73 (No dark radiation).** No dark radiation: $\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} \approx 0$, conditionally on the dark towers\' open channels closing, which holds if the descent\'s deposit is no hotter than the dark-matter paper\'s estimate, and only with the mirror\'s seeded temperature taken as a free datum: applied with the reach of the copy theorem, P2 gives the mirror $\xi = 1$ and $\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} = 7.45$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-tower-matter.mirrors-temperature-p2}), so the stance rests on an exception to P2 for the mirror. This is an exception to P2 at its version-1 reach; P2 as scoped leaves $\xi$ free (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.tower-charge-sector-label-temperature-not}), and the cold mirror then needs a non-equilibrium descent (Proposition \claimref{synthesis.cold-mirror-needs-non-equilibrium-descent}). The seeding floor behind the radiation audit uses the rate of gravitational pair production, which is now derived in the store, with corrected coefficients and with no quantum of the gravitational field (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(v), under its hypothesis (OS), or on the standard account of Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate}); with the corrected coefficients and the mean energy per creation event computed from the same rate integral the mirror's floor is $6.6 \times 10^{-4}$ and its channel closes by eleven orders there, where twelve stood (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.radiation-audit-what-theorem-closes-what}), and the prediction is unchanged.



**Prediction 74 (No indirect dark-sector signals).** No indirect dark-sector signals: no decay products, since the tower charge is superselected, and annihilation products only at the rate of graviton exchange, suppressed by powers of $E/M_{P}$, and for the mirror, a charge-asymmetric relic, further by its negligible anti-mirror density. The words ``graviton exchange'' name the process as its sources compute it; the series holds no quantum of the gravitational field (conventions, section ``Gravity and the graviton''). The store now derives the transition of a pair of one species into a pair of another through the gravitational field from the operator source, with no such quantum, at a rate carrying $\bar{M}_{P}^{-4}$ and with corrected coefficients for the thermal rate (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order}(ii) and (v), under its hypothesis (OS); the standard account gives the same tree-level rate), so the annihilation channel is the gravitationally suppressed one this stance assumes, and the prediction is unchanged.

## Quantum fields in one algebra

On 10 November 2026 the word occurred in 51 entities of the store and in five top-level files. ## Results cited, not shown

**Fact (imported) 75 (The Fock model of the complexified ascended algebra).** Here $V_\C = V \oplus I_7V$ is the complexification of $V = \R^{2,4}$ by the central pseudoscalar $I_7 = I_6e$ of the odd extension $\Cl(3,4) = \Cl(2,4)_\C$, whose scalars are $\C = \mathrm{span}\{1, I_7\}$ (\claimref{std-algebra.complexification-is-the-odd-extension}). Let $W \subset V_\C$ be a maximal totally isotropic subspace ($\dim_\C W = 3$) and $W'$ a complementary maximal isotropic subspace, dual to $W$ under the $\C$-bilinear form (such a pair exists, and any two such pairs are conjugate under $O(V_\C)$). On the exterior algebra $\Lambda^\bullet W$ ($\dim_\C = 2^3 = 8$) define
\[
c(w) := \sqrt2\; w \wedge (\cdot) \quad (w \in W),
\qquad
c(w') := \sqrt2\; \iota_{w'} \quad (w' \in W'),
\]
with $\iota$ contraction through the pairing. Then $c(u)^2 = Q(u)\,\id$ for every $u \in W \oplus W' = V_\C$, so $c$ extends to an algebra map $\Cl(V_\C) \to \End(\Lambda^\bullet W)$; this map is an isomorphism, and $\Lambda^\bullet W$ is the unique irreducible module of $\Cl(2,4)_\C$: the Dirac spinors of the completion. Vectors act oddly, so the even and odd halves
\[
\CS := \Lambda^{\mathrm{even}} W, \qquad \CSm := \Lambda^{\mathrm{odd}} W,
\]
each of complex dimension 4, are modules of the even subalgebra $\Cl^0(2,4)_\C \cong \End(\CS) \oplus \End(\CSm)$: the two chiralities, the conformal spinor space $\CS$ and its opposite $\CSm$ (twistor and dual twistor space).

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result correspondence.fock-model-complexified-ascended-algebra, version 9 (T09 Thm 3.1).

**Fact (imported) 76 (Conformal spinors are the states of three ladder pairs).** Let $u \in \CS\setminus\{0\}$ and define its \emph{annihilator space} $W_u := \{v \in V_\C : c(v)u = 0\}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item $W_u$ is a maximal totally isotropic ($3$-dimensional) subspace of $V_\C$: \emph{every} conformal spinor (twistor) is a pure spinor. Moreover $W_u$ determines $u$ up to scale (the joint kernel of $c(W_u)$ is the line $\C u$), so $[u] \mapsto W_u$ is an injection of $\PCS$ into the maximal isotropic subspaces, equivariant for the spin action.
\item For any maximal isotropic complement $U'$ dual to $W_u$, the map $\Lambda^\bullet U' \to \Lambda^\bullet W$, $\xi \mapsto c(\xi)u$, is a linear isomorphism: $u$ is the Fock vacuum of the three-ladder-pair system built on $(W_u, U')$, every state of the module is generated from $u$ by its own pair operators, and $\CS$ (respectively $\CSm$) is what the even (respectively odd) products generate. The pair system is canonical given $u$ up to the choice of dual complement.
\item Ambient vectors act on conformal spinor states as sums of three fermionic creation and annihilation operators; conformal spinors are therefore not \emph{operators} of the pair algebra but its \emph{states}. The annihilator spaces of $\CS$-spinors and of $\CSm$-spinors are disjoint families (no maximal isotropic annihilates both a nonzero even and a nonzero odd spinor).
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result correspondence.twistors-are-states-three-ladder-pairs, version 6 (T09 Thm 5.3).

**Fact (imported) 77 (The acoustic no-go: one way to grip).** In the current-algebra description of the boundary modes (Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-s-wave-current-algebra}) let $\varphi$ be a state invariant under the $U(1)$ gauge group of the currents, under charge conjugation $C$ ($J \mapsto -J$, $T \mapsto T$), and under the modular flow generated by the zero mode $L_0$ of the Sugawara stress tensor $T$: the conformal vacuum is such a state, and so is the induced state of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-s-wave-current-algebra} under premise (Q), with charge conjugation implemented on the mode array $\psi_k$ (the fermion modes of the singularity paper's array, Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-s-wave-current-algebra}) as the particle-hole reflection about the sea level, $C\psi_kC^{-1} = \psi^\dagger_{-k}$, so that $J \mapsto -J$ and $T \mapsto T$; the proof shows that this state has the three invariances, and the proposition is conditional on (Q) wherever it is applied to it. The graviton mode is $T$. Let $O$ be a probe that is gauge-charged (a fermion field, a condensate order parameter, any operator of nonzero $U(1)$ charge) or neutral and $C$-odd (the charge and spin currents), or, more generally, that lies in a nontrivial irreducible representation of any symmetry of the algebra that $\varphi$ respects and $T$ is invariant under. Then the linear susceptibility of the graviton mode to $O$ vanishes identically: $\varphi(T\,O) = 0$, and likewise for every mode $T_n$. At first order, nothing couples to the pair correlations except the $C$-even neutral subalgebra; in the conformal vacuum that subalgebra's overlap with $T$ is carried by $T$ alone, while in the thermal state it is the whole $C$-even neutral subalgebra, generated by the current bilinears and strictly larger than the conformal family of $T$, that can couple. In the conformal vacuum the statement is strengthened by Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-conformal-orthogonality-sugawara-construction}: every quasi-primary of dimension other than two is orthogonal to $T$ as well, so the only quasi-primary with nonzero overlap is $T$. The equivalence principle thereby acquires its converse at this level: fields differ in how strongly they couple, and the reduced theory proves there is no channel outside the $C$-even neutral sector through which to couple. \emph{Level:} the orthogonality is proved below for any state with the stated invariances; that the induced state of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-s-wave-current-algebra} has them is proved in the proof from premise (Q), and is conditional on (Q); that the thermal $C$-even neutral subalgebra is strictly larger than the conformal family of $T$ is a remark, not used.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.acoustic-no-go-one-way-grip, version 6 (T10 Prop 5.2).

**Fact (imported) 78 (The conclusion does not depend on the ontology, at its level).** The gravitational-engineering conclusion, that the metric-forming structure admits exactly one coupling sector (stress-energy), with no selective coupling, no enhanced source and no enhanced detector at first order, holds on either reading of the graviton's ontology, with a different level on each. If the graviton is the framework's collective mode: by derivation, Propositions \claimref{dark-energy.acoustic-no-go-one-way-grip} and \claimref{dark-energy.point-infinity-has-no-physical-fluctuation} at proof level within the s-wave sector and Assembly \claimref{dark-energy.lift-inherits-no-go-steps-itemised} for the lift. If the graviton is fundamental: by Weinberg's soft theorem \cite{Weinberg1964}, the leading low-energy coupling of any massless spin-two particle is universal, which is the equivalence principle and rules out a selective coupling at leading order; non-minimal couplings, subleading in energy, are not excluded by the theorem, so the fundamental branch delivers the conclusion at leading order only. The conclusion is therefore common ground between the ontologies at leading order; what is distinctive to the composite reading is the ultraviolet behaviour (quantisation as output; no graviton loops to renormalise), the derivation of weakness as dimensional suppression (Remark \claimref{note.dark-energy.weakness-dimension-nonlinear-bound-open-items}), and the $\ell_P^2/A$ corrections carried by the spectrum paper; the discriminating experiments, if any, lie in those corrections, and the asymmetry (hard to refute, hard to confirm below the Planck scale) is stated rather than hidden. The steps: the composite branch is the cited chain with its declared levels; the fundamental branch is the imported theorem read within its scope; the structural consequence recorded is that were the composite reading wrong, the leading-order conclusion would survive it.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.conclusion-does-not-depend-ontology-level, version 1 (T10 Asm 6.1).

**Fact (imported) 79 (The four alternatives are exhaustive; the three are not).** Every conserved $q$ is (D), (P) or (I), and exactly one of them; (K) can hold together with any of them and is excluded by none; (D) implies (K), while (P) and (I) are compatible with (K) and with its negation. A conserved $q$ that is neither (K), (D) nor (I) is (P). Hence the inference ``not kinematic, not dynamical, therefore initial data'' is valid only if (P) is excluded.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.four-alternatives-are-exhaustive-three-are, version 2 (T10 Prop 5.7).

**Fact (imported) 80 (The frozen point makes the Stelle-West vector).** On the Einstein region with $\Lambda \neq 0$ of a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}), whose tractor connection is the $\Cl(2,4)$ gauge field $\Omega_{T}$ of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gtg-tractor-gauge-field-reproduces-tractor-connection}, in the notation of Lemma \claimref{dark-energy.stelle-west-vector-frozen-point}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(Reduction; proved.)} Parallelism of $I$ reduces the ambient $SO(2,4)$-structure to the stabiliser of $I$, the de Sitter group $SO(1,4)$ for $\Lambda > 0$ and the anti-de Sitter group $SO(2,3)$ for $\Lambda < 0$, and the tractor connection to a metric connection on the rank-five bundle $I^\perp$, of signature $(1,4)$ or $(2,3)$ respectively; the ambient curvature takes values in $\Lambda^2 I^\perp$. (The de Sitter names are used below; the anti-de Sitter branch reads identically with these substitutions, and every statement that follows uses only $\{I,V\}^\perp$, whose signature is $(1,3)$ on both branches.)
\item[(ii)] \emph{(The Stelle-West datum; proved.)} $V$ is a section of $I^\perp$ of fixed squared norm $-3/\Lambda = -\ell^2$ ($\ell^{2} = 3/\Lambda$, negative for $\Lambda < 0$), its stabiliser in $SO(1,4)$ (or $SO(2,3)$) is the Lorentz group of $\{I, V\}^\perp$, of signature $(1,3)$, its covariant derivative is the soldering form, the physical frame $\hb^{-1}(a)$, and the reduced connection is $D + e\wedge V/Q(V)$, on flat spacetime the gauge field $\Omega(a) - \hb^{-1}(a) \wedge V/\ell^{2}$, with Lorentz curvature block $R + \ell^{-2}\,e\wedge e$. The pair (reduced connection, $V$) is exactly the datum of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-macdowell-mansouri-theorem-stelle-west}. The Stelle-West vector is therefore not the frozen point, which is parallel; it is made from the frozen point and the canonical tractor, $V = X - (3/\Lambda)\,I$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(The action; proved, with the Stelle-West contraction of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-macdowell-mansouri-theorem-stelle-west} as the imported input.)} The MacDowell-Mansouri functional of that datum is
\[
\begin{aligned}
S[\nabla, I, V] \;&\sim\; \frac{1}{Q(I)} \int \epsilon(I, V, F, F), \\
S[\nabla, I, X - \tfrac{3}{\Lambda}I] \;&\sim\; \frac{3}{\Lambda}\int \epsilon(I, X, F, F) \quad\text{(value at the canonical solution),}
\end{aligned}
\]
where $\epsilon(I, V, F, F)$ denotes the fibre volume form of $\mathcal{T}$ contracted with $I$, with $V$, and with the two bivector legs of $F\wedge F$, the first line being the functional and the second its value at the canonical Stelle-West vector of Lemma \claimref{dark-energy.stelle-west-vector-frozen-point}, written in the scale the frozen point determines (the canonical tractor $X$ carries a conformal weight): \emph{extremise the square of the curvature as seen from the point at infinity}, the frozen point supplying the reduction and the prefactor, the canonical tractor the Stelle-West slot. In the variational principle $\nabla$ ranges over the metric connections of the ambient bundle that preserve $I$, $\nabla I = 0$, equivalently those that reduce to $I^\perp$, which is the field space of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-macdowell-mansouri-theorem-stelle-west} (the twenty further components per point of an unconstrained ambient connection are exactly $\nabla I$, which enter the integrand quadratically through $[I\wedge u, I\wedge v] = -Q(I)\,u\wedge v$ and would spoil the identification with the Einstein-Cartan equations); this space is carried to itself by the ambient gauge group acting on $\nabla$, $I$ and $V$ together, since $(g\nabla g^{-1})(gI) = g(\nabla I)$, and the tractor connection \claimref{dark-energy.imported-frozen-point-geometry} is the point of it occupied by the canonical solution of Lemma \claimref{dark-energy.stelle-west-vector-frozen-point}, generic gauge transformations moving it within the space without preserving the tractor shape. $I$ is a field subject to $Q(I) = \Lambda/3$, and $V$ an independent field subject to $\ip{V}{I} = 0$, $Q(V) = -3/\Lambda$, of which Lemma \claimref{dark-energy.stelle-west-vector-frozen-point} exhibits the canonical solution; the identity $Q(I)\,Q(V) = -1$ holds for either sign of $\Lambda$.
\end{enumerate}
Two consequences, each at its level:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{(Proved.)} $S$ is polynomial in the connection, $I$ and $V$, with no inverse metric anywhere: its integrand is defined wherever the ambient bundle and its connection are regular. At the Schwarzschild wall the connection is not regular (Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.frozen-point-datum-kasner-wall} on the Kasner model, transported by Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-weighted-limit-schwarzschild-singularity-matching}: a simple pole with the Kasner residue), and there the integrand's topological term diverges while its variational principle and field equations reach the wall as conditions on the residue; what the polynomial form yields at $\Sigma$ is the equations, not the number. the section ``What the equations vacate, and what cannot evolve'' makes this precise.
\item \emph{(Proved; the limit at the level of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-macdowell-mansouri-theorem-stelle-west}.)} The prefactor $1/Q(I) = 3/\Lambda$ has a simple pole at $\Lambda = 0$ and the integrand $\epsilon(I, X, F, F)$ is polynomial in $\Lambda$: the curvature-squared presentation exists precisely when $\Lambda \neq 0$, that is, precisely when the frozen point is off the null cone. This is a boundary of the presentation, not of the action principle. By the expansion of Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-macdowell-mansouri-theorem-stelle-west},
\[
S \;\sim\; \frac{3}{\Lambda}\int \epsilon_{abcd}R^{ab}\wedge R^{cd} \;+\; 2\int \epsilon_{abcd}\,e^a\wedge e^b\wedge R^{cd} \;+\; \frac{\Lambda}{3}\int \epsilon_{abcd}\,e^a\wedge e^b\wedge e^c\wedge e^d ,
\]
the first term is topological with vanishing interior variation (a boundary term only), and after its subtraction the limit $\Lambda \to 0$ is the Einstein-Cartan functional $2\int\epsilon_{abcd}\,e^a\wedge e^b\wedge R^{cd}$: polynomial, defined at degenerate $e$, and no longer of curvature-squared form.
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.frozen-point-makes-stelle-west-vector, version 7 (T10 Thm 3.1).

**Fact (imported) 81 (Imported: the frozen point and its geometry).** Let $g$ be the derived metric of a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}). On the invertible region: the vacuum equations are exactly the covariant constancy of the null scale tractor $\nInf$; for the Einstein condition with $\Lambda \neq 0$ the parallel tractor has squared norm $Q(I) = \Lambda/3$, its stabiliser is the reductive de Sitter group $SO(1,4)$ (for $\Lambda > 0$; anti-de Sitter for $\Lambda < 0$), and the parabolic-to-reductive rotation of the stabiliser is proved with the sign fixed internally. The stabiliser of a non-null fibre vector of $\Cl(2,4)$ has dimension ten, with the orthogonal complement of the vector carrying the induced form of signature $(1,4)$ or $(2,3)$. In the scale $g$ the tractor connection is
\begin{equation}
\nabla_a(\sigma, \mu_b, \rho) \;=\; \bigl(\nabla_a\sigma - \mu_a,\;\; \nabla_a\mu_b + g_{ab}\,\rho - P_{ab}\,\sigma,\;\; \nabla_a\rho + P_a{}^{b}\mu_b\bigr),
\end{equation}
with $P_{ab}$ the Schouten tensor, $P_{ab} = (\Lambda/6)\,g_{ab}$ on the Einstein region, and it preserves $\ip{(\sigma,\mu,\rho)}{(\sigma',\mu',\rho')} \;=\; g(\mu,\mu') + \sigma\rho' + \rho\sigma'$. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[Fact~4.1, Thms.~4.5, 4.7, Props.~4.4, 4.10, Lem.~4.8]{ButlerT6}; the tractor connection and the almost-Einstein scale tractor are Gover's technology \cite{Gover2010}, as the geometry paper's Fact 4.1 records.

\emph{Remark (text only, not part of the Fact).} On flat spacetime this tractor connection is the gauge field $D^{T}_{a} = a \cdot \nabla + \Omega_{T}(a)\times$ of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gtg-tractor-gauge-field-reproduces-tractor-connection}. That theorem rests on this Fact, through Lemma \claimref{dark-energy.stelle-west-vector-frozen-point}, so the Fact does not use it, and a result citing the Fact cannot take the gauge-field form from it.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.imported-frozen-point-geometry, version 7 (T10 Fact 2.1).

**Fact (imported) 82 (The lift inherits the no-go, with its steps itemised).** The remaining open item, that the four-dimensional lift might introduce a coupling invisible to the s-wave sector, is addressed by covering the spacetime in two pieces, with one step upgraded. The steps, each with its provenance:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Partial waves (exact, kinematic).} Near the horizon the four-dimensional two-point function between the graviton mode and any probe decomposes over partial waves, and the angular integration is diagonal in $\ell$ by spherical-harmonic orthogonality.
\item \emph{Near-horizon reduction (assembly, at the level it carries in T5).} Each fixed-$\ell$ sector reduces, at the horizon where the potential barrier vanishes, to a two-dimensional copy of the setting of the current algebra, in which the $\ell$-th component of the graviton mode is the $\ell$-th $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-harmonic of the current bilinears. Which operator is the stress tensor in the $\ell$-th copy is fixed by step (4), not assumed.
\item \emph{Exterior exactness (imported theorem).} On any region where the gauge-theoretic metric is invertible, the horizon included, the description coincides exactly with classical general relativity, by the geometry paper's import of gauge-theory gravity from Lasenby, Doran and Gull \cite[Fact~2.1]{ButlerT6}, whose couplings to the metric are stress-energy alone. The two pieces overlap and cover the spacetime.
\item \emph{The sphere step (conditional on the entropy paper's Indifference premise; then proved by covariance).} By the sphere theorem (Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-walls-sphere-theorem}, \cite[Thm.~5.5]{ButlerT8}, conditional on \cite[Principle~5.4]{ButlerT8}) every angular operator of the lift is a boundary-mode bilinear of definite spin under the adjoint $\mathfrak{su}(2)$, of spin $\ell \geq 1$ for every non-trivial harmonic, while the s-wave stress tensor $T$ is an $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ singlet. In an $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-invariant state the symmetry orthogonality of Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.acoustic-no-go-one-way-grip} applies verbatim: nonzero spin, zero overlap. Orthogonality by dimension would \emph{not} do here, the bilinears having dimension two like $T$; it is the spin that separates them. The lift inherits the no-go because the sphere is built from the same correlations to which the no-go applies, and those correlations carry spin.
\end{enumerate}
With Propositions \claimref{dark-energy.acoustic-no-go-one-way-grip} and \claimref{dark-energy.point-infinity-has-no-physical-fluctuation} and these steps, the acoustic programme is closed at the mixed level of its inputs: the metric-forming correlations admit exactly one coupling sector, its weakness is dimensional, its leading nonlinearity is Gertsenshtein's, and its Goldstones are eaten. Step (2), the near-horizon reduction, is at assembly level, its promotion shared with T5 and T7; step (4) is conditional on the entropy paper's Indifference premise, and the closure of the acoustic programme inherits that condition.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.lift-inherits-no-go-steps-itemised, version 2 (T10 Asm 5.5).

**Fact (imported) 83 (Under the action as written, the norm is a parameter of the laws).** In the three-argument functional of Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.point-infinity-has-no-physical-fluctuation}, $\Lambda$ enters as the value of the Stelle-West constraints $Q(I) = \Lambda/3$, $Q(V) = -3/\Lambda$, a number fixed before variation: it is the index $\mu$ of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-energy.four-ways-number-enters-theory-shape}. Consequently:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] with $\Lambda$ fixed, the constrained fluctuation space of $(I, V)$ has dimension nine and the nine broken generators act onto it bijectively (Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.point-infinity-has-no-physical-fluctuation}): no fluctuation of the norm exists, every solution with parameter $\Lambda$ has $H^2 = Q(I) = \Lambda/3$ on its de Sitter region, and $N = M_P\sqrt{3/\Lambda}$ is constant on each $\mathcal{S}_\Lambda$;
\item[(ii)] $N$ varies with $\Lambda$ across the family, $d(N^2)/d\Lambda = -3M_P^2/\Lambda^2 \neq 0$, so it is not (D); and it is not (K), by the rescaling argument of Fact \claimref{standard-model.trichotomy-what-kind-number};
\item[(iii)] therefore $N$ is (P), a parameter of the laws, and not (I): clause (3) of the Standard-Model paper's classification, ``$N$ is initial data'', is false for the action as written.
\end{enumerate}
If instead $\Lambda$ is allowed to vary, the linearised constraints become three independent conditions on the thirteen components $(\delta I, \delta V, \delta\Lambda)$, the constrained space has dimension ten, the gauge orbit is still nine-dimensional (bivectors do not change a norm), and exactly one non-gauge direction appears: the norm mode
\[
(\delta I, \delta V, \delta\Lambda) \;\propto\; \Bigl(\frac{I}{2\Lambda},\ -\frac{V}{2\Lambda},\ 1\Bigr),
\]
which is the tangent at $s = 1$ of the rescaling family $(sI, V/s, s^2\Lambda)$ and which needs an equation of motion the action does not supply.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.under-action-written-norm-parameter-laws, version 4 (T10 Prop 5.8).

**Fact (imported) 84 ($w = -1$ on the vacuum region; the registration, and its condition).** Three clauses, at three levels. Gravity is a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime with derived metric $g$ (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}); the tractor connection is its $\Cl(2,4)$ gauge field (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.gtg-tractor-gauge-field-reproduces-tractor-connection}).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(Proved.)} On the Einstein region the frozen point is parallel (Fact \claimref{dark-energy.imported-frozen-point-geometry}) and the tractor connection preserves the fibre metric, so $\partial_aQ(I) = 2\ip{\nabla_a I}{I} = 0$: the norm $\Lambda/3$ cannot vary in space or time. A constant $\Lambda$ enters the Einstein equations as the stress-energy $T^{\Lambda}_{ab} = (\Lambda/8\pi G)\,g_{ab}$, that is $T^{\Lambda}(a) = (\Lambda/8\pi G)\,a$ (in the store's convention, where de Sitter space has $\Ric = \Lambda g$ in signature $(+,-,-,-)$, field strength $\mathcal{R}(a) = \Lambda a$, and the Einstein equation reads $\Ric + 8\pi G\,T = (\Lambda + 4\pi G\,g^{ab}T_{ab})\,g$), with energy density $\rho = \Lambda/8\pi G > 0$ for $\Lambda > 0$ and pressure $p = -\rho$ identically: $w = -1$.
\item[(ii)] \emph{(Proved.)} Off the Einstein region write $I_g$ again for the scale tractor of a general field's derived metric $g$ in its own scale, $I_g = (1, 0, J/4) = \nInf + \tfrac{J}{4}\nO$ (the object written $I$ above is its Einstein case, $J/4 = \Lambda/6$), with $J = P_a{}^a$ the trace of the Schouten tensor, the slot that makes $I_g$ parallel exactly when $g$ is Einstein (proved below); by $\ip{(\sigma,\mu,\rho)}{(\sigma',\mu',\rho')} \;=\; g(\mu,\mu') + \sigma\rho' + \rho\sigma'$, $Q(I_g) = J/2 = R/12$, which equals $\Lambda/3$ precisely when $R = 4\Lambda$. On a matter-dominated cosmology $R$ is not constant, so the naive extension of the identification to the epochs at which $w$ is measured does not give a constant.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(Registered, conditional, under Premise (FS).)} If dark energy is the norm of the frozen point (Theorem \claimref{dark-energy.frozen-point-makes-stelle-west-vector}), and if on matter-filled regions the frozen point is kept parallel by the gravitational connection, the $\Cl(2,4)$ connection that preserves $I$, in the physical scale, $\ip{X}{I} = 1$ (successor problem (3) of the section ``The engineering conclusion, and scope''; this is Premise \claimref{synthesis.field-space-premise-parallel-frozen-point}, which the store adopts), then $w = -1$ exactly, at every epoch, with no evolution and no quintessence. This is a maximally rigid stance in a field of flexible proposals: most dark-energy models add freedom to $\Lambda$CDM, this one removes it. \emph{The confrontation, recorded as of 2026:} the second data release of the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument \cite{DESI2025}, combining baryon-acoustic-oscillation, cosmic-microwave-background and supernova data, reports a preference for an evolving equation of state at roughly three to four standard deviations, with significance depending on the supernova compilation. The preference is contested on three grounds (it concentrates in the derivative of $w$ rather than any mean offset from $-1$; it is prior-sensitive; the driving dataset combinations exhibit internal tensions), so it is not a detection. The framework does not rely on the dispute and states its exposure plainly: a confirmed detection of $w \neq -1$ at any epoch refutes the frozen-point reading of dark energy, with no parameter available to absorb the result. The condition in (iii) is the one way the reading could avoid such a refutation, and the paper names it rather than uses it: a definition of the frozen point off the vacuum region that made the norm track $R/12$ would be refuted already.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Note (the wording of the condition, version 8).} The paper's wording of the condition, that the frozen point admits a definition on a matter-filled region that keeps it parallel, names neither the connection nor the scale. Read literally it is met for every field by the connection that preserves $I$, and without the scale clause by transport-parallel tractors whose own scale is not the physical one and whose norm is unrelated to the $\Lambda$ of the field equations (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.parallel-frozen-point-on-matter-regions-is-the-field-space-premise}(i), (iii)); for the series' transport, the tractor gauge field, it fails wherever matter is (part (ii) there). Worded with the connection and the physical scale, as above, it is Premise (FS): adopting (FS) implies it, and read as fixing which connection carries the frozen point it is (FS) restated (same theorem, Reading). On the reduced field space $\rho$ is constant, so $\Lambda$ is a constant parameter and $w = -1$ is the $\Lambda$CDM equation of state: a confirmed $w = -1$ supports $\Lambda$CDM with a frozen point and is not independent evidence for (FS), while a confirmed $w \neq -1$ would refute (FS), and $\Lambda$CDM with it.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result dark-energy.vacuum-region-registration-condition, version 8 (T10 Prop 4.2).

**Fact (imported) 85 (The alignment computed: the thirteenth refutation, democracy, and one heavy family).** Four statements, the first a refutation, the rest its redirection, each at the level marked.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{(The thirteenth refutation, by magnitude; a dimensional estimate.)} The source term of Proposition \claimref{families.source-term-linear-tilt-mass-functional} is $\Lambda$-suppressed, so the condensate it can induce is $\delta b \sim \Lambda\,\Omega_{\mathrm{vac}}$ with $\Omega_{\mathrm{vac}}$ a length; for any vacuum length between the Planck and Hubble scales, $\Lambda\Omega_{\mathrm{vac}}$ lies between $H^{2}/M_{P} \approx 10^{-94}$ eV and $H \approx 10^{-33}$ eV, that is between roughly $10^{-105}$ and $10^{-44}$ of the weak scale $10^{11}$ eV: the tilt cannot reach $10^{2}$ GeV, the natural conjecture that the vacuum $\omega$-area sets the weak scale is refuted, and the weak scale falls back to the weight question, where the hierarchy has remained since the mass paper's fifth refutation.
\item \emph{(The redirection.)} Alignment requires only a nonzero tilt, and directions are ratios, $\Lambda$-independent; so what the vacuum $\omega$-area does determine is its \emph{direction} in the three-dimensional face space of Conjecture \claimref{families.three-generations-faces-maximal-composite}, which carries the family ordering and the mixing. The magnitude was never the physical content; the direction is, and the direction is computable from symmetry where the magnitude was not.
\item \emph{(The direction, at structural level, under the Gram-square reading.)} The kinematic vacuum cannot break $S_{3}$ before its own source exists: a face-anisotropic vacuum would be uncaused structure, forbidden by the indifference instrument (Fact \claimref{families.imported-indifference-instrument}). The source therefore lies on the democratic line, spanned by $u = (1,1,1)/\sqrt{3}$. If the leading mass form is the Gram square of the source, $Y_{0} \propto u' u'^{\mathsf{T}}$ with $u' = (1,1,1)$, the democratic mass matrix of \cite{HHW1978} (the reading the section ``The Gaussian theorems'' realises at operator level and successor problem (2) asks to derive in general), then $Y_{0}$ has spectrum $(3, 0, 0)$ times the condensate scale: one heavy family and two light ones at leading order, the third generation's dominance as structure rather than fit; and Conjecture \claimref{families.three-generations-faces-maximal-composite} is refined rather than replaced: the physical families are the \emph{eigenvectors} of the face-space mass form, at leading order the democratic vector (heavy) and its orthogonal plane (light), the faces up to the rotation the vacuum performs.
\item \emph{(The residue, named.)} The second-order breaking $S_{3} \to S_{2} \to 1$, the anisotropic corrections that lift the two light families and convert the CKM shape into CKM numbers: the family programme's next computable target, confronted in the section ``The Koide geometry''-the section ``The Gaussian theorems''. Everything in this proposition past the magnitude refutation is at the structural and observation levels stated.
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result families.alignment-computed-thirteenth-refutation-democracy-one, version 4 (T13 Prop 4.3).

**Fact (imported) 86 (Greybody inheritance, under the interface premise).** \emph{Premise (I), the interface identification:} the horizon-sector state feeds the exterior modes in the standard way, i.e.\ the occupancy of each near-horizon channel is carried to the corresponding asymptotic channel by the exterior propagation. This is the same assumption every derivation of Hawking flux makes, from \cite{Hawking1975} onward, and it is this assembly's one non-theorem joint. Under (I), the emitted flux in each channel $(\omega, \ell, m, s)$ equals (with $\mathcal{N}$ the count of emitted quanta)
\[
\frac{d\mathcal{N}}{dt\,d\omega} \;=\; \frac{1}{2\pi}\,\Gamma_{s\ell m}(\omega)\; n_\pm(\omega), \qquad
n_\pm(\omega) = \frac{1}{e^{\omega/\TH} \pm 1}
\]
(the upper sign for the fermionic species, the lower for the bosonic sector), with $\Gamma_{s\ell m}$ the classical transmission coefficient through the Regge-Wheeler or Teukolsky barrier. The joints: (i) the barriers and their transmission coefficients are properties of the exterior metric, and by Fact \claimref{hawking.imported-exterior-exactly-general-relativity} the framework's exterior description \emph{is} classical general relativity on the invertible region, exactly and not asymptotically, so $\Gamma_{s\ell m}$ is inherited verbatim, mode by mode \cite{ReggeWheeler1957, Teukolsky1973}; (ii) the occupancies at the horizon are Theorem \claimref{hawking.i-state-continuum} for the fermionic species and Fact \claimref{hawking.imported-s-wave-bosonic-sector}'s (Q)-conditional half for the bosonic sector, both at exactly $\TH$ with the temperature's coefficient fixed geometrically (Fact \claimref{hawking.imported-modular-descent-premises-geometric-normalisation}); (iii) given (I), the displayed formula is the definition of filtered thermal flux: the framework's flux prediction equals Hawking's \cite{Hawking1975, Page1976}, greybody structure included.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result hawking.greybody-inheritance-under-interface-premise, version 6 (T07 Asm 5.4).

**Fact (imported) 87 (Non-null stabilisers, and the section they act on).** Let $I \in V$ with $Q(I) \neq 0$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the stabiliser subalgebra of $I$ is $\Lambda^2(I^\perp) \cong \mathfrak{so}(I^\perp)$, reductive, of dimension $10$, with trivial nilradical: $\mathfrak{so}(1,4)$ if $Q(I) > 0$ and $\mathfrak{so}(2,3)$ if $Q(I) < 0$;
\item the hyperplane section $M_I := \{X \in V : Q(X) = 0,\ \ip{X}{I} = 1\}$ is, under $Y := X - I/Q(I)$, the pseudosphere $\{Y \in I^\perp : Q(Y) = -1/Q(I)\}$, a complete constant-curvature Lorentzian $4$-manifold on which the stabiliser acts as the isometry group: de Sitter space for $Q(I) > 0$, anti-de Sitter space for $Q(I) < 0$;
\item the polar hypersurface $\{\ip{X}{I} = 0\}$ meets the quadric in the projectivised null cone of $I^\perp$: for $Q(I) > 0$ a spacelike conformal infinity ($S^3$ with its standard conformal structure), for $Q(I) < 0$ a timelike one.
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result interior.non-null-stabilisers-section-they-act, version 5 (T06 Prop 4.4).

**Fact (imported) 88 ($\Lambda \neq 0$: the scale tractor leaves the cone).** Let $g$ be the derived metric of a gravitational field $(\hb, \Omega)$ on flat spacetime (Definition \claimref{obj.synthesis.gtg-flat-spacetime-gauge-fields}) and let it satisfy the Einstein condition $\Ric_{ab} = \Lambda\, g_{ab}$ on the invertible region $M^\circ$, that is $\mathcal{R}(a) = \Lambda a$ for the field strength in the store's curvature sign, and set
\[
I_g \;:=\; \Bigl(1,\; 0,\; \tfrac{\Lambda}{6}\Bigr) \;=\; \nInf + \tfrac{\Lambda}{6}\,\nO
\]
in the scale $g$. Then $I_g$ is parallel, and every parallel section of the form $(1, 0, \rho)$ arises this way: $(1,0,\rho)$ is parallel if and only if $\rho$ is constant and $g$ is Einstein with $\Lambda = 6\rho$. The norm is
\[
Q(I_g) \;=\; \tfrac{\Lambda}{3} :
\]
the frozen point is null exactly in the $\Lambda = 0$ vacuum, and for $\Lambda \neq 0$ it leaves the cone, with the cosmological constant as its squared norm (times the positive constant $3$).

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result interior.scale-tractor-leaves-cone, version 8 (T06 Thm 4.7).

**Fact (imported) 89 (The stabiliser rotates from parabolic to reductive).** The stabiliser of the frozen point $I_g$ in the structure group of the completion is:
\begin{enumerate}
\item for $\Lambda = 0$: the stabiliser of a null vector, the Poincar\'e group $SO(1,3) \ltimes \R^4$ (Proposition \claimref{interior.stabilisers-flat-model}), a group with $4$-dimensional nilradical: the parabolic situation, whose transvections are the obstruction structure of \cite{ButlerT1};
\item for $\Lambda > 0$: the de Sitter group $SO(1,4)$, reductive, acting on the Einstein section as its isometry group, with conformal infinity the spacelike polar $3$-sphere $\ip{X}{I} = 0$;
\item for $\Lambda < 0$: the anti-de Sitter group $SO(2,3)$, reductive, with timelike polar infinity.
\end{enumerate}
In short: $\Lambda$ measures precisely the failure of infinity to be parabolic.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result interior.stabiliser-rotates-parabolic-reductive, version 2 (T06 Prop 4.10).

**Fact (imported) 90 (Mass as symplectic area: the two-spinor formula).** On the real slice, where $\varepsilon^{A'B'}$ is the conjugate of $\varepsilon^{AB}$ and $\bar\pi$ the conjugate of $\pi$ (the conjugation reverses the complex unit $I_{6}$): a single conformal spinor (twistor) carries null momentum $p_{AA'} = \pi_{A}\bar\pi_{A'}$ and is massless, and a system of two conformal spinors with momentum
\[
p_{AA'} = \pi^{1}_{A}\bar\pi^{1}_{A'} + \pi^{2}_{A}\bar\pi^{2}_{A'}
\]
has
\begin{equation}
m^{2} = p^{2} = 2\,\bigl|\br{\pi^{1}\pi^{2}}\bigr|^{2},
\end{equation}
the squared symplectic area between the two spinor parts. Aligned spinors (vanishing bracket) give a null composite, and mass grows with the misalignment.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result mass.mass-symplectic-area-two-twistor-formula, version 3 (T11 Prop 4.1).

**Fact (imported) 91 (Classification of the real Clifford algebras).** Let $d \equiv p - q \pmod 8$. Then $\Cl(p,q)$ is isomorphic, as a real associative algebra, to the entry of the table of the section ``The mod-8 classification'': $\Mat_k(\R)$ for $d \in \{0, 2\}$, $\Mat_k(\C)$ for $d \in \{3, 7\}$, $\Mat_k(\HH)$ for $d \in \{4, 6\}$, $\Mat_k(\R) \oplus \Mat_k(\R)$ for $d = 1$, and $\Mat_k(\HH) \oplus \Mat_k(\HH)$ for $d = 5$, with $k$ fixed in each case by $\dim_\R \Cl(p,q) = 2^n$. In particular $\Cl(p,q)$ is simple except when $p - q \equiv 1 \pmod 4$, where it is the direct sum of two isomorphic simple algebras.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result primer.classification-real-clifford-algebras, version 3 (T00 Thm 9.3).

**Fact (imported) 92 (The twisted adjoint action).** Let $g = u_1 \cdots u_k$ be a versor and define, on the whole algebra,
\begin{equation}
\tau_g(x) \;=\;
\begin{cases}
\,g\, x\, g^{-1} & g \text{ even},\\[2pt]
\,g\, \hat{x}\, g^{-1} & g \text{ odd}.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Then each $\tau_g$ is an algebra automorphism, and on vectors it restricts to the twisted adjoint $\Ad_g(x) = \hat{g} x g^{-1}$. For a single invertible vector $u$,
\[
\Ad_u(x) \;=\; -\,u x u^{-1} \;=\; x - 2\,\frac{x \cdot u}{Q(u)}\,u ,
\]
the reflection in the hyperplane orthogonal to $u$; consequently $g \mapsto \Ad_g$ maps $\Pin(p,q)$ onto $O(p,q)$ with kernel $\{\pm 1\}$.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result primer.twisted-adjoint-action, version 4 (T00 Lem 9.2).

**Fact (imported) 93 (The infinite wall sequence).** Let $\Sig$ be compact. For good couplings at every stage, there is an infinite sequence $\Sig = \Sig^{(0)}, \Sig^{(1)} = \Sig', \Sig^{(2)}, \dots$ of tame walls, each displaced from the last into the unphysical region, carrying Clifford sheaves of ranks $2^n, 2^{n+2}, 2^{n+4}, \dots$ whose physical-side fibres ascend the Witt tower $\Cl(p,q) \to \Cl(p{+}1,q{+}1) \to \Cl(p{+}2,q{+}2) \to \cdots$, one Rule-1 rung per wall. Degeneracy is conserved along the entire sequence: each completion resolves its wall and produces the next, displaced successively further from the physical region.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result sheaf.infinite-wall-sequence, version 4 (T04 Cor 5.5).

**Fact (imported) 94 (Chirality: the two halves of the spinor module, and conjugation as duality).** The conformal spinor space $\CS = \Lambda^{\mathrm{even}}W$ (twistor space) and its partner $\CSm{} = \Lambda^{\mathrm{odd}}W$ are the two chirality halves of the ascended Dirac module (Fact \claimref{standard-model.imported-pair-systems-two-chirality-halves}), and the sector of a single conformal spinor $\Lambda^{\bullet}W$ is their direct sum. Under $S(U(2) \times U(3))$ the two halves carry different content: $\CS$ carries $(1)_{0} \oplus (\bar{3})_{-2/3}$ and $\CSm{}$ carries $(3)_{-1/3} \oplus (1)_{-1}$ (Proposition \claimref{standard-model.quarks-leptons-two-level-fermion-content}(3)), so the two halves are inequivalent representations of the group: the chirality split is visible to the internal symmetry, not only to the grading. The singlet spectrum listed by a single handedness is Theorem \claimref{standard-model.hypercharge-global-form-pair-counting-consistency}(2)'s, and in particular the colour content $3 \oplus \bar{3}$ of the two halves becomes $\bar{3} \oplus \bar{3}$ under it, which is the conjugation of Remark \claimref{note.standard-model.weakness-examined-exotics-conjugation-hypercharge}.
