Moreover the conjugation is an operation inside the pair system: the dual of the grade-$k$ state is the grade-$(3-k)$ state with the full-system phase removed, $(\Lambda^{k}W)^{*} \cong \Lambda^{3-k}W \otimes (\Lambda^{3}W)^{*}$, of weight $+2k$; and the two doublets at the level of one conformal spinor of Proposition \claimref{standard-model.quarks-leptons-two-level-fermion-content}(2) are each other's duals, $(\C^{2} \otimes \Lambda^{0}W)^{*} \cong \C^{2} \otimes \Lambda^{3}W$, and under (S) have opposite parity. Under (S) they are therefore one doublet and its antiparticle, not two candidates, and Theorem \claimref{standard-model.hypercharge-global-form-pair-counting-consistency}(1)'s listing choice is the particle-antiparticle orientation of Remark \claimref{note.standard-model.weakness-examined-exotics-conjugation-hypercharge}.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result standard-model.chirality-two-halves-spinor-module-conjugation, version 6 (T12 Prop 5.6).

**Fact (imported) 95 (Four dimensions from the forces, and the converse).** (1) Let the spacetime dimension $n$ be even, and suppose that in dimension $n$, as in dimension four (Fact \claimref{mass.mass-symplectic-area-two-twistor-formula}), a single conformal spinor (twistor) is massless and mass first exists at two constituents. Then the pair count per conformal spinor is $(n+2)/2$ (Lemma \claimref{standard-model.pair-count-dimension}), the minimal massive hierarchy is $\C^{2} \otimes \C^{(n+2)/2}$, and its automorphism algebra is $\su(2) \oplus \su((n+2)/2) \oplus \uu(1)$ by the argument of Theorem \claimref{standard-model.gauge-algebra-minimal-massive-composite}, which for $m \neq k$ uses the Segre lemma and the tensor factorisation only, and which at $n = 2$, where $m = k = 2$, still applies because an automorphism is defined as a map of the form $A \otimes B$, so that the swap of factors is excluded by definition, as in (2). This equals the Standard Model algebra if and only if $n = 4$. If, therefore, the gauge algebra of matter is the automorphism algebra of its minimal massive compositeness, four dimensions is a consequence: four is the dimension in which the pair count is three. The identification premise is Theorem \claimref{standard-model.gauge-algebra-minimal-massive-composite}'s reading, the mass-begins-at-two hypothesis in dimension $n \neq 4$ is a hypothesis (the mass paper proves it in dimension four only), and both conditionalities are declared.

(2) Conversely, with no hypothesis on $n$: for every two-factor hierarchy $\C^{m} \otimes \C^{k}$ with $m, k \ge 1$ the automorphism algebra is $\su(m) \oplus \su(k) \oplus \uu(1)$, of dimension $m^{2} + k^{2} - 1$, and it is isomorphic to the Standard Model algebra if and only if $\{m, k\} = \{2, 3\}$ as an unordered pair. So the gauge algebra, once identified, forces the hierarchy to be a two-by-three, and one further input orders it: given that mass begins at two constituents ($m = 2$), the pair count is three, hence four dimensions by Lemma \claimref{standard-model.pair-count-dimension}; given four dimensions ($k = 3$), the constituents number two. Under Theorem \claimref{standard-model.gauge-algebra-minimal-massive-composite}'s reading, each of the two framework inputs is a consequence of the observed forces together with the other, not only consistent with them; the algebra alone does not distinguish two conformal spinors of three pairs from three of two.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result standard-model.four-dimensions-forces-converse, version 4 (T12 Prop 4.3).

**Fact (imported) 96 (Hypothesis (S): slot statistics, derived from the slot-module hypothesis).** \emph{Hypothesis (S), slot statistics.} Under the slot-module hypothesis (Premise \claimref{standard-model.slot-module-hypothesis}: the composite space $V = F \otimes \Lambda^{\bullet}W$, a graded $U(2)$ slot module tensored with one pair system, with hypercharge from the centre of $S(U(2) \times U(3))$, the slot module generated from a unique vacuum by one doublet of slot creation operators, the chirality a CPT halving that commutes with the Pati-Salam algebra of the chain once the slots are fermionic, and slot and pair operators one Clifford system):
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(Odd slots.)} If the slot quanta are tower elements of one tower parity $\pi$ whose tower grading $(-1)^{\pi n + k}$ is the chirality, then $\pi = 1$.
\item[(ii)] \emph{(Slot content.)} $F \cong \Lambda^{\bullet}\C^{2}$ as a graded $U(2)$-module, so the composite's state space is the Fock space $\Lambda^{\bullet}\C^{2} \otimes \Lambda^{\bullet}W = \Lambda^{\bullet}(\C^{2} \oplus W)$ of five modes, two slot modes and three pair modes, a state being a set of occupied slots and occupied pairs, on which $(A, B) \in U(2) \times U(3)$ acts as $\Lambda^{\bullet}(A \oplus B)$; its particle half is $(1,1)_{0} \oplus (1,\bar{3})_{-2/3} \oplus (2,3)_{1/6} \oplus (2,1)_{-1/2} \oplus (1,1)_{1} \oplus (1,\bar{3})_{1/3}$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(Chirality.)} As in Fact \claimref{standard-model.imported-pair-systems-two-chirality-halves} for the three-mode Fock space $\Lambda^{\bullet}W$, the chirality of a state is the parity of its total mode number.
\item[(iv)] \emph{(Exclusion statistics.)} Every $U(2)$-equivariant slot creation map $a^{\dagger} : \C^{2} \otimes F_{n} \to F_{n+1}$ satisfies $a^{\dagger}(u)a^{\dagger}(u') = -a^{\dagger}(u')a^{\dagger}(u)$ for all $u, u' \in \C^{2}$; in particular $a^{\dagger}(u)^{2} = 0$.
\item[(v)] \emph{(One Clifford system.)} The slots are fermionic modes on the same footing as the pairs: the two slot modes and the three pair modes satisfy the canonical anticommutation relations as one system of five modes, so that, by Fact \claimref{standard-model.imported-fock-representation-clifford-algebra-bogoliubov}, their Majorana combinations generate $\Cl(10)$ acting on $\Lambda^{\bullet}\C^{5}$, its spinor module, whose parity grading is that module's chirality.
\end{enumerate}
Clauses (ii) and (iv) are Theorem \claimref{synthesis.cpt-closure-forces-slot-module-and-chirality}(3) under the premise's generation clause and CPT halving, with no anomaly condition; (v) is the premise's clause (f), applied once (ii) has made the slot module fermionic; (iii) follows from anomaly freedom of the particle half, the consistency condition of Fact \claimref{standard-model.imported-anomaly-coefficients}, through Theorem \claimref{synthesis.cpt-closure-forces-slot-module-and-chirality}(4), together with the premise's requirement that the chirality commute with the Pati-Salam algebra, whose two-slot creation operator and its adjoint fix the neutral-singlet line; (i) compares (iii) with a tower grading. Anomaly freedom is used as that consistency condition, not as a clause of the premise.

\emph{Note (the slot operators of (v)).} In clause (f) of the premise, version 5, the five-mode system is formed with the Klein-twisted slot operators $\tilde a_{i} = a_{i}(-1)^{k}$ (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.joint-clifford-clause-is-a-klein-convention-on-the-composite-space}); the untwisted $a_{i}$ commute with the pair operators. The slot modes of (v) and their Majoranas are those of the twisted operators. Nothing else here depends on the choice: the Pati-Salam bivectors, the grading and the two-slot operator $a^{\dagger}_{1}a^{\dagger}_{2} = \tilde a^{\dagger}_{1}\tilde a^{\dagger}_{2}$ are the same in both conventions.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result standard-model.hyp-s-slot-statistics, version 10 (T12 Hypothesis (S)).

**Fact (imported) 97 (Imported: the Fock representation of a Clifford algebra, and its Bogoliubov group).** Let $E$ be a complex vector space of dimension $n$ with a Hermitian inner product, and let $\Lambda^{\bullet}E$ be the Fock space of $n$ fermionic modes, with creation operators the exterior multiplications and annihilation operators the contractions. The $2n$ Majorana combinations of these operators generate an action of the complex Clifford algebra of a $2n$-dimensional orthogonal space on $\Lambda^{\bullet}E$, which is its irreducible spinor module; the parity grading $\Lambda^{\mathrm{even}} \oplus \Lambda^{\mathrm{odd}}$ is the chirality decomposition into the two half-spinor modules. The orthogonal transformations of the Majorana generators preserve the canonical anticommutation relations and are implemented on $\Lambda^{\bullet}E$ by the group $\mathrm{Pin}(2n)$, the connected component $SO(2n)$ by $\mathrm{Spin}(2n)$, which acts on each half-spinor module irreducibly for $n \ge 2$; the transformations preserving the complex structure of $E$, equivalently the mode number, form $U(n) \subset SO(2n)$, acting on $\Lambda^{\bullet}E$ as $\Lambda^{\bullet}(g)$ up to a determinant phase. For $n = 3$ this is the structure of Fact \claimref{standard-model.imported-pair-systems-two-chirality-halves}; for $n = 5$ it is $\Lambda^{\bullet}\C^{5}$ with $\mathrm{Spin}(10)$. \emph{Provenance:} \cite[Lecture~20, \S 20.1]{FultonHarris1991} for the spinor module as an exterior algebra and its half-spin decomposition; \cite[Ch.~I, \S\S 2, 5]{LawsonMichelsohn1989} for $\mathrm{Spin}(2n)$ as the double cover of $SO(2n)$ and its spin representation; the Fock-space form is the standard second-quantised reading of the same construction.

*Provenance:* W. Fulton and J. Harris, Representation Theory: A First Course, Graduate Texts in Mathematics 129, Springer (1991)., Lecture~20, \S 20.1; H. B. Lawson and M.-L. Michelsohn, Spin Geometry, Princeton University Press (1989)., Ch.~I, \S\S 2, 5.

**Fact (imported) 98 (The trichotomy: what kind of number $N$ is).** The question the paper ends by pointing at can itself be classified. The classification has four parts, of which the first contains a proved statement and a reading, the second is an import, the third is conditional on the first two, on Observation \claimref{standard-model.dating-descent} and on a decision the fourth names, and the fourth is imported from the action paper; the levels are marked clause by clause.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{(Kinematic.)} Proved: under $\Lambda \to s^{2}\Lambda$ at fixed $M_{P}$, that is $I \to sI$ on the frozen point with the conformal class unchanged, $H \to sH$ and $N \to N/s$, while every discrete invariant this paper derives (the algebra and its dimension twelve, the global form and its $\mathbb{Z}_{6}$, the dimension four, the content and the hypercharges, and under (S) the ratio $3/8$) is unchanged, since each is computed from the conformal class, the pair structure and the group alone, none of which the rescaling affects. Reading, at observation level: since the conformal class and the pair structure carry no scale, no construction from them alone can fix $N$, which lies in the ratio of the frozen point's norm to the Planck scale; on this reading the kinematic layer determines all discrete invariants and no continuous modulus, and $N$ and the weight spectrum are its only continuous moduli.
\item \emph{(Non-dynamical, imported.)} $\Lambda$ is the norm of the frozen point, and the norm is not a field: it is fixed by a constraint, the fluctuations of the frozen point are pure gauge, and the action carries no equation of motion for it (Fact \claimref{standard-model.imported-norm-frozen-point-not-field}).
\item \emph{(Initial data, conditional.)} If the alternatives kinematic, dynamical and initial-data are exhaustive, then by (1) and (2) $N$ is initial data, and Observation \claimref{standard-model.dating-descent}(ii) locates the boundary that carries it at the origin. The exhaustiveness is not proved here, and the location is an Observation; the clause is conditional on both, and clause (4) says what the exhaustiveness costs.
\item \emph{(Parameter of the laws, imported.)} The three alternatives are not exhaustive: a conserved quantity that is constant on every solution of a theory but varies across a family of theories indexed by a parameter is a parameter of the laws, and the action paper proves that under the action as written, with $\Lambda$ the value of the Stelle-West constraints fixed before variation, $N$ is exactly that: constant on each solution space $\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}$ by the rigidity of (2), varying across $\Lambda$, so neither dynamically fixed nor initial data \cite[Prop.~5.8]{ButlerT10}. Clause (3) therefore holds only if the norm is promoted from a fixed number to the value of a Lagrange-multiplier field whose constancy is an equation of motion, the Henneaux-Teitelboim mechanism, which makes $\Lambda$ a label of the solution rather than of the theory; that promotion is a decision for the series, recorded open with both branches stated \cite[Rem.~5.9]{ButlerT10}, which the store adopts (\claimref{programme.n-parameter-of-the-laws}), and the entropic and null-endpoint effects named below act on a measure over theories or on a measure within one theory according to it.
\end{enumerate}
At observation level, two competing effects are then named without being balanced: an entropic weighting of the freezing by $\exp(S_{\Lambda}) = \exp(\pi N^{2})$, with $S_{\Lambda} = \pi N^{2}$ the cosmological horizon's area-law entropy (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.de-sitter-entropy-pi-n-squared}), favours large $N$ overwhelmingly (the well-known entropic argument for small $\Lambda$, inherited with every one of its known objections, listed as inherited and not solved), while at the endpoint $\Lambda = 0$ the frozen point is null and the curvature-squared presentation of the action, whose prefactor is $3/\Lambda$, does not exist; the action paper is explicit that this is a boundary of the presentation and not of the action, the Einstein-Cartan functional remaining \cite[Thm.~3.1, Rem.~3.2]{ButlerT10}, so what is lost at $\Lambda = 0$ is the reductive structure and the frozen point's length, not gravity. Large because large boundary mode spaces dominate the measure; nonzero because the descent selects a frozen point of nonzero length; the value is the balance point, and the balance point awaits the descent measure, the one object this framework does not possess. Last, a statement of units only, flagged as such: in the boundary modes' own units, qubits, one per rung of the Fock module (Theorem \claimref{tower.fock-module-one-qubit-per-rung}), the horizon's capacity $S_{\Lambda} = \pi N^{2}$ is $\pi N^{2}/\ln 2$, about $4.8 \times 10^{122}$ qubits (the mode count of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.de-sitter-mode-count} under the black hole's counting rule), whose base-two logarithm is about $407$. The qubit is here a unit of capacity, $\ln 2$: the horizon count is a count of registered units of that capacity, not of rungs or of ladder pairs, and no integrality of $\pi N^{2}/\ln 2$ is claimed. The paper's $R = \log_{2} N^{2} \approx 405$ counts the rungs of a module of dimension $N^{2}$, whose capacity is only $\ln N^{2} = 2\ln N$ nats, not the horizon's. Neither number is a count of turns of the mod-8 clock, which no rung moves: each rung preserves the residue $p - q \pmod 8$ (Theorem \claimref{transport.rule-1-morita-trivial}). This is the question in the framework's own coordinates, not an answer to it.

\emph{Note (the Hubble unit).} The numbers for $N = M_{P}/H$ here use $H^{2} = \Lambda/3$, that is $H_{0}\sqrt{\Omega_{\Lambda}}$ (with $H_{0} = 67.4$ km/s/Mpc and $\Omega_{\Lambda} = 0.685$), the $H$ in which the register states the rung unit $v/H$; some texts quote $v/H$ with $H_{0}$, which differs by the factor $\sqrt{\Omega_{\Lambda}}$, of order one ($\ln(v/H)$ changes by about $0.2$). No value of $v/H$ is quoted here.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result standard-model.trichotomy-what-kind-number, version 12 (T12 Prop 7.6).

**Fact (imported) 99 (Isometries extend to algebra homomorphisms).** An isometry $f : (V, Q) \to (W, Q')$ extends to a unique unital algebra homomorphism $\Cl(f) : \Cl(V, Q) \to \Cl(W, Q')$ with $\Cl(f)(v) = f(v)$. It is injective when $f$ is, and $\Cl(g \circ f) = \Cl(g) \circ \Cl(f)$. In particular the orthogonal group $\mathrm{O}(V, Q)$ acts on $\Cl(V, Q)$ by automorphisms, and the inclusion of a subspace $U \subseteq V$ gives an embedding $\Cl(U, Q|_U) \hookrightarrow \Cl(V, Q)$.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result std-algebra.functoriality, version 1.

**Fact (imported) 100 (How the pseudoscalar commutes).** In $\Cl(p, q, r)$, $n = p + q + r$, with unit pseudoscalar $I$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $I X_k = (-1)^{k(n-1)} X_k I$ for $X_k \in \Cl^{k}$; so $I$ commutes with $\Cl^{+}$, and with $\Cl^{-}$ when $n$ is odd, while it anticommutes with $\Cl^{-}$ when $n$ is even. Equivalently $I X = X I$ for $n$ odd and $I X = \hat{X} I$ for $n$ even.
\item[(ii)] $I \tilde{I} = \prod_{i} Q(e_i)$, which is $(-1)^{q}$ when $r = 0$ and $0$ when $r > 0$.
\item[(iii)] $I^{2} = (-1)^{n(n-1)/2} (-1)^{q}$ when $r = 0$ (\claimref{primer.sign}), and $I^{2} = 0$ when $r > 0$: in a degenerate algebra the pseudoscalar is nilpotent.
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result std-algebra.pseudoscalar-commutation, version 1.

**Fact (imported) 101 (Null pairs satisfy the canonical anticommutation relations).** Let $v_1, \dots, v_k, w_1, \dots, w_k \in V$ be a \emph{Witt basis} of their span: $B(v_i, v_j) = B(w_i, w_j) = 0$ and $B(v_i, w_j) = \tfrac12 \delta_{ij}$. Then in $\Cl(V, Q)$
\begin{equation}
v_i v_j + v_j v_i = 0, \qquad w_i w_j + w_j w_i = 0, \qquad v_i w_j + w_j v_i = \delta_{ij},
\end{equation}
the canonical anticommutation relations for $k$ modes (read $v_i$ as annihilators, $w_i$ as creators). The elements $N_i = w_i v_i$ are mutually commuting idempotents with $N_i + v_i w_i = 1$. In particular, for orthogonal hyperbolic planes $\operatorname{span}(e_i, e_{k+i})$ of $\R^{k,k}$, $v_i = \tfrac12(e_i + e_{k+i})$ and $w_i = \tfrac12(e_i - e_{k+i})$ form such a basis. The form need not be nondegenerate outside the span.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result std-algebra.witt-basis-car, version 1.

**Fact (imported) 102 (Witt's extension theorem).** Let $(V, Q)$ be a nondegenerate real quadratic space, $U \subseteq V$ any subspace and $f : U \to V$ an injective isometry. Then $f$ extends to an isometry of $V$, that is an element of $\mathrm{O}(V, Q)$. Equivalently (Witt cancellation): if $A \perp B$ and $A' \perp B'$ are nondegenerate with $A \cong A'$ and $A \perp B \cong A' \perp B'$, then $B \cong B'$. Every such extension is a product of reflections $x \mapsto x - 2\frac{B(x, a)}{Q(a)}\, a$, since $V$ is nondegenerate (\claimref{std-algebra.cartan-dieudonne}). The hypothesis that $V$ is nondegenerate cannot be dropped: an isometry of $V$ maps $\operatorname{rad} V$ to itself.

*Provenance:* E. Witt, Theorie der quadratischen Formen in beliebigen Koerpern, J. Reine Angew. Math. 176 (1937)., Satz 4; T. Y. Lam, Introduction to Quadratic Forms over Fields, AMS (2005)., Ch. I (Witt's extension and cancellation theorems).

**Fact (imported) 103 (Completion recovers the real Clifford algebras; Sylvester's law of inertia in GA).** Let $F$ be an ordered field. A quadratic space over $F$ is a finite-dimensional $F$-vector space $V$ with $Q(\lambda v) = \lambda^{2}Q(v)$ and polar form $B(u, v) = \tfrac12(Q(u + v) - Q(u) - Q(v))$ bilinear (as in \claimref{obj.std-algebra.quadratic-space}, with $F$ for $\R$); its Clifford algebra is defined by the universal property of \claimref{obj.std-algebra.clifford-algebra} over $F$, in which $uv + vu = 2B(u,v)$. $F$ is \emph{Euclidean} if every positive element is a square. $\Cl_{F}(p,q,r)$ is the Clifford algebra of $F^{n}$, $n = p + q + r$, with an orthogonal basis of $p$, $q$, $r$ vectors of squares $1$, $-1$, $0$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(Completeness gives square roots.)} If $F$ is complete (every nonempty subset bounded above has a least upper bound), then $F$ is Euclidean.
\item[(ii)] \emph{(Orthogonal bases by rejection.)} Every quadratic space over $F$ has a basis $u_{1}, \dots, u_{n}$ of mutually anticommuting vectors, $u_{i}u_{j} = -u_{j}u_{i}$ for $i \ne j$, obtained by repeated rejection $x \mapsto x - B(x, u)\,u^{-1}$ from vectors $u$ with $Q(u) \ne 0$. If $F$ is Euclidean, the basis can be chosen with $Q(e_{i}) \in \{1, -1, 0\}$, by $e_{i} = u_{i}/\sqrt{|Q(u_{i})|}$ where $Q(u_{i}) \ne 0$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(Sylvester's law of inertia.)} For any ordered $F$, the numbers $p$, $q$, $r$ of vectors with $Q > 0$, $Q < 0$, $Q = 0$ in an anticommuting basis do not depend on the basis: $r = \dim \operatorname{rad} V$, and $p$ (respectively $q$) is the largest dimension of a subspace on which $Q$ is positive (negative) definite. If $F$ is Euclidean, two quadratic spaces are isometric if and only if they have the same $(p, q, r)$, and then $\Cl(V, Q) \cong \Cl_{F}(p,q,r)$.
\item[(iv)] \emph{(The completion recovers the store's algebra.)} Let $\Cl_{\Q}(p,q,r)$ be the $\Q$-span of the blades with the blade product of Proposition \claimref{std-algebra.orthogonal-anticommute} (structure constants $0$, $\pm 1$). If $\R$ is a complete ordered field, as premise (F4) takes it, then $e_{S} \mapsto e_{S}$ extends to an isomorphism $\Cl_{\Q}(p,q,r) \otimes_{\Q} \R \cong \Cl(p,q,r)$, and for every real quadratic space $\Cl(V, Q) \cong \Cl_{\Q}(p,q,r) \otimes_{\Q} \R$ with $(p, q, r)$ its inertia; through $r$ added hyperbolic planes it embeds in the nondegenerate $\Cl_{\Q}(p{+}r,q{+}r) \otimes_{\Q} \R$, the radical generators going to null vectors $e'_{k} + f'_{k}$ (Theorem \claimref{conformal.minimal-nondegenerate-completion}). So every real Clifford algebra of the store is, up to isomorphism, a rational Clifford algebra of a $\pm 1$ form, or a subalgebra of one, extended by scalars.
\item[(v)] \emph{(Over $\Q$ square classes remain.)} Over $\Q$, (ii) holds without the normalisation and the invariance of $(p, q, r)$ in (iii) holds, but the isometry criterion fails: $Q(\lambda u) = \lambda^{2}Q(u)$ fixes $Q(u)$ only up to a nonzero rational square. The line with $e^{2} = 2$ is not isometric to the line with $e^{2} = \pm 1$; its Clifford algebra, with $x\bar{x} = a^{2} - 2b^{2}$ for $x = a + be$, has no zero divisors, while $\Cl_{\Q}(1,0)$ has $(1 + e_{1})(1 - e_{1}) = 0$. The plane with $e_{1}^{2} = 2$, $e_{2}^{2} = 3$ has $x\bar{x} = a^{2} - 2b^{2} - 3c^{2} + 6d^{2}$ for $x = a + be_{1} + ce_{2} + de_{1}e_{2}$, which has no nontrivial rational zero, so its Clifford algebra is a division ring, while $\Cl_{\Q}(2,0)$ has zero divisors. Over any Euclidean field they become $\Cl(1,0)$ and $\Cl(2,0)$.
\item[(vi)] \emph{(What is used.)} Clauses (ii) to (iv) use of the coefficient field only that it is ordered and, for the normalisation, Euclidean. Completeness enters (iv) only through (i).
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result synthesis.completion-recovers-the-real-clifford-algebras, version 2.

**Fact (imported) 104 (CPT conjugation as the pseudoscalar rotor times complex conjugation).** Let $\R^{2,4}$ carry the conformal ambient metric, $\Cl(2,4)$ its Clifford algebra with $e_0^2 = e_1^2 = 1$ and $e_2^2 = \dots = e_5^2 = -1$, $I_6 = e_0 e_1 \cdots e_5$ its unit pseudoscalar, and $\Cl(2,4) \otimes \C = \Cl(3,4) = \Cl(2,4) \oplus \Cl(2,4)I_7$ its complexification, whose complex unit is the central pseudoscalar $I_7 = I_6e$ of the odd extension ($e$ orthogonal to $\R^{2,4}$, $Q(e) = +1$; Theorem \claimref{std-algebra.complexification-is-the-odd-extension}); $\kappa$ is complex conjugation, the automorphism induced by $e \mapsto -e$, which fixes $\Cl(2,4)$ and sends $I_7$ to $-I_7$. $I_7$ is the complex unit throughout; $I_6$ is the real pseudoscalar of $\Cl(2,4)$, which acts on pure spinors with eigenvalues $\pm I_7$, and the two are not identified. Let $W \subset \C^6 = \R^{2,4} \oplus I_7\R^{2,4}$ be a maximal isotropic subspace with $W \cap \overline W = 0$ (the generic Witt flag of Definition \claimref{obj.dark-matter.witt-flag}), and $\psi_W$ its pure spinor.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The composite of grade involution and reversal is an anti-automorphism of $\Cl(2,4)$, not an automorphism, and acts on vectors as $-1$. Grade involution equals $\mathrm{Ad}(I_6)$, and $I_6 = (e_0e_1)(e_2e_3)(e_4e_5)$ is a rotor of the identity component: each factor squares to $-1$ and is $\exp(\pi B_k/2)$, and $I_6 \widetilde{I_6} = 1$. So the vector action $-1$ lies in $SO_0(2,4)$. In $\Cl(1,3)$, by contrast, $I_4\widetilde{I_4} = -1$.
\item $W$ is the $+I_7$ eigenspace of an orthogonal complex structure $J$ on $\R^{2,4}$, $W \oplus \overline W = \C^6$, and $I_6\psi_{W} = \pm I_7\,\psi_{W}$, $I_6 \psi_{\overline W} = \mp I_7\,\psi_{\overline W}$. So $\overline W$ lies in the other family, and complex conjugation is a flag conjugate. In $\Cl(3,3)$, $I_6^2 = +1$ and conjugation preserves the family.
\item Every real-linear isometry $g$ of $\R^{2,4}$ with $g(W) = \overline W$ has $\det g = -1$, so that $\epsilon(gI, gV, gF, gF) = -\epsilon(I, V, F, F)$ for vectors $I, V$ and bivectors $F$, $g$ acting on bivectors by its outermorphism. For a basis $w_k$ of $W$ and the dual basis $v_k$ of $\overline W$ ($B(w_j, v_k) = \delta_{jk}$, $B$ the symmetric bilinear form of $Q$ extended complex-bilinearly), the complex-linear Witt exchange $v_k \leftrightarrow w_k$ preserves $\R^{2,4}$ exactly when the Gram matrix $c_{jk} = B(w_j, \overline{w_k})$ of the $w_k$ against their conjugates satisfies $c\,\overline{c} = 1$, in particular when $v_k = \pm\overline{w_k}$; it is then a real isometry of determinant $-1$, a product of three reflections, an instance of the first sentence. For other normalisations, such as $w_k = e_{2k-2} - I_7e_{2k-1}$ with $v_k = \overline{w_k}/B(w_k, \overline{w_k})$, it does not preserve $\R^{2,4}$.
\item $C := \mathrm{Ad}(I_6) \circ \kappa$ is an antilinear involutive automorphism of $\Cl(2,4) \otimes \C$ that is $-1$ on $\R^{2,4}$, fixes the metric, $\epsilon$ and $I_6$, and carries $\psi_W$ to a multiple of $\psi_{\overline W}$. With the flag conjugate realised by $\kappa$, the stored composite $\phi$ (grade involution, reversal, flag conjugate) is reversal composed with $C$: an antilinear anti-automorphism. No realisation of the flag conjugate on $\R^{2,4}$ is both real-linear and orientation-preserving.
\item \emph{Convention.} A (possibly anti-) automorphism $\phi$ acts on fields as a symmetry: on a vector field by its vector part, and on a connection $A$ through the Lie algebra automorphism it induces, $A \mapsto -\phi(A)$ when $\phi$ is an anti-automorphism, so that curvature transforms by the same automorphism; for the stored composite this is the action of $C$. In this convention every action functional of real tensor (multivector) fields on which $C$ acts through its vector part, invariant under constant $SO_0(2,4)$ transformations, in particular the frozen point's $Q(I)^{-1}\int \epsilon(I, V, F, F)$ (\claimref{dark-energy.frozen-point-makes-stelle-west-vector}), satisfies $S \circ \phi = S$, while with any real-linear flag conjugate in place of $\kappa$ the same action changes sign. Spinor fields are not covered. If instead $\phi$ is applied to the Clifford integrand $IVFF$ as a whole and the top-grade coefficient taken, both sign statements invert (with $\kappa$ the integrand changes sign, with a real-linear flag conjugate it is preserved), so the conclusion of (5) depends on the convention.
\end{enumerate}

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result synthesis.cpt-conjugation-pseudoscalar-rotor-times-complex-conjugation, version 5.

**Fact (imported) 105 (The dimmed final burst is a time-rescaled copy).** Let a black hole of the one metric evaporate in the regime where every populated tower's light species are lighter than its temperature, so that the emission coefficients are constant. Write $\Psi_{\mathrm{vis}}$ for this tower's coefficient in the mass-loss law, $\Psi_{\mathrm{grav}}$ for the gravitational sector's, $\Psi_{\mathrm{odd}}$ for the odd towers', $\Psi_{\mathrm{tot}}$ for the total, $g = \Psi_{\mathrm{grav}}/\Psi_{\mathrm{vis}}$, $o = \Psi_{\mathrm{odd}}/\Psi_{\mathrm{vis}}$, and let the standard expectation be the hole with this tower and gravitons only, total coefficient $\Psi_{0} = \Psi_{\mathrm{vis}} + \Psi_{\mathrm{grav}}$. Put $D = \Psi_{\mathrm{tot}}/\Psi_{0}$. Let $\tau$ be the hole's remaining lifetime and $N(E;\tau)$ the number of visible quanta per unit energy emitted in its last $\tau$, $N_{0}$ its standard value.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Every channel's flux per unit energy depends on $M$ and $E$ only through $ME$ (Theorem \claimref{hawking.greybody-inheritance-under-interface-premise}), so $dM/dt = -\Psi_{\mathrm{tot}}/M^{2}$ and $M(\tau)^{3} = 3\Psi_{\mathrm{tot}}\tau$; at fixed $M$ the visible power is the same as in the standard case (Proposition \claimref{dark-matter.parallel-boundary-mode-spaces-one-wall}(i)).
\item[(ii)] \emph{(Time rescaling.)} For any visible emission $h(E, M)$ per unit time, secondaries included, the same function in every tower configuration because the exterior metric does not record which tower the mass came from,
\[
N(E;\tau) \;=\; \frac{1}{D}\, N_{0}(E;D\tau):
\]
the visible burst of the last $\tau$ is the standard burst of the last $D\tau$, divided by $D$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(Three dimming laws.)} Consequently, at fixed remaining lifetime: the visible energy emitted in the last $\tau$ is $D^{-2/3}$ times its standard value; the hole's temperature is $D^{-1/3}$ times its standard value; and for primary emission, whose flux depends on $E$ and $M$ only through $ME$, the high-energy tail $N(E;\tau) \to C\,E^{-3}/(\Psi_{0}D)$ for $E\,M(\tau)$ large, which does not depend on $\tau$, is $1/D$ times its standard value. Only this primary tail, and the visible fraction of the power at fixed mass, are dimmed by the full ratio.
\item[(iv)] \emph{(Floor.)} The mirror's light content copies ours (Theorem \claimref{dark-matter.mirror-tower-copy-one}), so $D = (2 + g + o)/(1 + g) = 2 + (o - g)/(1 + g)$: $D \ge 2$ if and only if $o \ge g$, and with $o = 0$, $D = 2 - g/(1 + g)$. The factors of (iii) are $D$, $D^{2/3}$ and $D^{1/3}$.
\end{enumerate}
The odd towers enter only through $o$; the burst prediction needs their emission coefficients and no other property of them.

\emph{Scope note.} A populated five-dimensional tower never meets the hypothesis of constant coefficients at every mass: under the interface premise its Kaluza-Klein levels are emission channels of the shared exterior, the mass-loss law gains a term $B/M^{3}$ with $B$ proportional to the fifth direction's extent, and the time-rescaled copy (ii), with the laws (iii) drawn from it, holds for such a tower only for masses well above the crossover mass $M_{\times} = B/A$ of \claimref{synthesis.five-dimensional-tower-kaluza-klein-channels-steepen-burst}(ii); below it $M^{4} = 4B\tau$ and the primary high-energy tail steepens from $E^{-3}$ to $E^{-4}$ above a break.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result synthesis.dimmed-burst-time-rescaled-copy, version 2.

**Fact (imported) 106 (Gravitational pair production from the operator source at first order).** Units $\hbar = c = 1$, signature $(+,-,-,-)$ in $\Cl(1,3)$, $\bar{M}_{P}^{-2} = 8\pi G$. A \emph{source} at momentum $k$ is a symmetric linear map $\mathcal{T}$ of the vectors with $\mathcal{T}(k) = 0$; $\ip{\mathcal{T}_{1}}{\mathcal{T}_{2}} = \sum_{a} \mathcal{T}_{1}(e_{a}) \cdot \mathcal{T}_{2}(e^{a})$, $\tr\mathcal{T} = \sum_{a} e^{a} \cdot \mathcal{T}(e_{a})$, and
\[
B(\mathcal{T}_{1}, \mathcal{T}_{2}) \;=\; \ip{\mathcal{T}_{1}}{\mathcal{T}_{2}} - \tfrac12\,\tr\mathcal{T}_{1}\,\tr\mathcal{T}_{2} .
\]
\emph{Hypothesis (OS) (operator source at first order).} To first order in $G$ the gravitational gauge field is the solution of the stored frame equation (Fact \claimref{synthesis.imported-einstein-cartan-matter-coupling}(a), $\Lambda = 0$) linearised about the flat field, with the stress map of the matter fields, an element of the matter algebra, as its source, and matter responds to it through its stress map (clause (v) of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.weyl-flat-variations-carry-only-the-stress-trace-and-the-whole-stress-clause-is-the-trace-free-channel}); no state of the gauge field enters. (OS) is assumed here, not derived. In the static limit it is the stored coupling of Proposition \claimref{field-theory.operator-newtonian-coupling}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(The field at non-null momentum is fixed by the source; proved.)} With the stored first-order curvature $\mathcal{R}_{k}[h](B) = -\tfrac12\,k \wedge h(B \cdot k)$ and its Einstein map $\mathcal{G}_{k}$, every source at $k$ satisfies $\mathcal{G}_{k}[\mathcal{T} - \tfrac12\,\id\,\tr\mathcal{T}] = \tfrac12\,Q(k)\,\mathcal{T}$. For $Q(k) \neq 0$ the kernel of $\mathcal{G}_{k}$ on symmetric maps is the four-dimensional space of displacements, so the first-order field of a source is $h = \lambda\,(\mathcal{T} - \tfrac12\,\id\,\tr\mathcal{T})/Q(k)$ up to a displacement, with one constant $\lambda$: there is no solution without source, and nothing to choose between retarded, advanced, symmetric or time-ordered inverses. For $Q(k) = 0$ the kernel has dimension six: two directions beyond the displacements are not fixed by the source.
\item[(ii)] \emph{(The bilinear and its normalisation; proved given (OS).)} The first-order interaction of two sources at $k$, $Q(k) \neq 0$, is $8\pi G\,B(\mathcal{T}_{1}, \mathcal{T}_{2})/Q(k)$. For two bodies at rest, $\mathcal{T}_{i}(a) = m_{i}(a \cdot e_{0})e_{0}$, one has $B = \tfrac12 m_{1}m_{2}$ and $Q(k) = -|\mathbf{k}|^{2}$, and the interaction is $-4\pi G m_{1}m_{2}/|\mathbf{k}|^{2}$, the transform of $-G m_{1}m_{2}/r$: the stored Newtonian coupling fixes the constant. The transition amplitude between a pair of total momentum $k$, $Q(k) = s > 0$, and another pair is $|\mathcal{M}| = 8\pi G\,|B|/s$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{(Pair sources; computed.)} For incoming momenta $p_{1}, p_{2}$ and outgoing $p_{3}, p_{4}$, all null, with $t = Q(p_{1} - p_{3})$, $u = Q(p_{1} - p_{4})$, $s + t + u = 0$: a pair of quanta of a real scalar field with curvature coupling $\xi$ has the source $\mathcal{T}(a) = -(a \cdot p_{2})p_{1} - (a \cdot p_{1})p_{2} + (p_{1} \cdot p_{2})a + 2\xi\bigl((a \cdot k)k - Q(k)a\bigr)$, of trace $(1 - 6\xi)s$; a pair of quanta of a vector field with field bivectors $F_{i} = p_{i} \wedge \epsilon_{i}$ has $\mathcal{T}(a) = -\tfrac12(F_{1}aF_{2} + F_{2}aF_{1})$ (Fact \claimref{synthesis.imported-maxwell-stress-spacetime-algebra}), trace-free; a particle and antiparticle of one massless chiral fermion have $\mathcal{T}(a) = \tfrac14\bigl(j\,(a \cdot d) + d\,(a \cdot j)\bigr)$, $d = p_{1} - p_{2}$, with $j$ the pair's transition current, whose spin sum against real vectors $w$ is $2\br{p_{2}wp_{1}w}_{0}$ (half the sum of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.electron-positron-to-muon-pairs-in-the-spacetime-algebra-lsz-tree-cross-section-and-one-loop-unitarity}(1) at zero mass). Then, summed over polarisations, $B^{2} = t^{2}u^{2}$ for scalars to scalars at $\xi = 0$, $2t^{2}u^{2}$ for vectors to scalars and $\tfrac14(t - u)^{2}tu$ for the fermion pair to scalars; in the centre-of-mass frame the scalar bracket is $\tfrac{s^{2}}{4}(\cos^{2}\theta - \tfrac13) - \tfrac{s^{2}}{6}(1 - 6\xi_{1})(1 - 6\xi_{2})$, a spin-two part and a trace part.
\item[(iv)] \emph{(The channels factorise; computed.)} Let $W_{ij}$ be the integral over the sphere of $\sum B^{2}/s^{4}$ from a pair of species $i$ to a pair of species $j$, with a factor $\tfrac12$ for each pair of identical quanta. For real scalars, chiral fermions and real massless vectors, in all nine channels,
\[
W_{ij} \;=\; \frac{\pi}{180}\bigl(c_{i}c_{j} + 5\,\tau_{i}\tau_{j}\bigr), \qquad c = 1,\ 3,\ 12, \qquad \tau = (1 - 6\xi)^{2} \text{ for a scalar and } 0 \text{ otherwise.}
\]
\item[(v)] \emph{(The rate; proved given (OS) and the standard rate formula.)} In a bath at temperature $T$ of $N_{s}$ real scalars of trace weights $\tau_{i}$, $N_{w}$ chiral fermions and $N_{v}$ vectors, all free and massless at this order, with zero chemical potentials, pairs of a species $j$ absent from the bath are created at the rate per volume
\[
\Gamma_{j} \;=\; \alpha_{j}\,\frac{T^{8}}{\bar{M}_{P}^{4}}, \qquad
\alpha_{j} \;=\; \frac{\pi^{3}}{17280}\left[\frac{c_{j}(N_{s} + 12N_{v}) + 5\tau_{j}\sum_{i}\tau_{i}}{225} + \frac{49\,c_{j}\,N_{w}}{4800}\right],
\]
$\Gamma_{j}$ counting creation events, each of two quanta; tree level, no thermal masses, no occupation of the produced species. For the Standard Model's content $(N_{s}, N_{w}, N_{v}) = (4, 45, 12)$ with $\xi = 0$: $\alpha = 5789\pi^{3}/82944000 \approx 2.16 \times 10^{-3}$ for a real scalar, $16087\pi^{3}/82944000 \approx 6.01 \times 10^{-3}$ for a chiral fermion (twice that for a Dirac fermion), $16087\pi^{3}/20736000 \approx 2.41 \times 10^{-2}$ for a real massless vector, and $1519247\pi^{3}/82944000 \approx 0.568$ in total for a copy of that content. The value does not depend on the inverse chosen in (i): the integrand is bounded and $s = 0$ only where the incoming momenta are parallel, a set of measure zero.
\item[(vi)] \emph{(Against the published values.)} With each of the $45$ fermions given the weight $6$ of a Dirac fermion in place of $3$, the formula gives $3997\pi^{3}/41472000$ for a scalar and $11351\pi^{3}/20736000$ for a Dirac fermion, the values of arXiv:2112.15214 (its equations (86) and (90), with the coupling $-h_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}/\bar{M}_{P}$ of a canonically normalised field). The value stored in Fact \claimref{dark-matter.imported-massless-graviton-production-rate}, $3997\pi^{3}/663552000$ from \cite{Bernal2018}, is one sixteenth of the first: its source writes the coupling as $h_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}/(2\bar{M}_{P})$ with the propagator of a canonically normalised field, and its squared amplitudes are one sixteenth of the canonical ones: a factor two in the coupling is a factor sixteen in the rate. The normalisation of (ii), fixed by Newton's law, is the later one. So the stored scalar coefficient is low by $46312/3997 \approx 11.6$ against (v): sixteen from the coupling, $5789/7994$ from the fermion weight.
\item[(vii)] \emph{(The mean-field source gives no such rate; argued, standard.)} If instead the field is sourced by the expectation value of the bath's stress map, it is the homogeneous isotropic field of the expanding bath, Weyl-flat, and chiral fermions, vectors and conformally coupled scalars in their conformal vacuum stay in it (Fact \claimref{synthesis.imported-conformal-covariance-of-massless-fields-and-local-conformal-flatness}); a scalar with $\xi \neq \tfrac16$ is created only through the scalar curvature, which vanishes for a bath of trace-free stress. No pairs are created at order $G^{2}T^{8}$, up to terms suppressed by the trace of the bath's stress and by the expansion rate over the temperature.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Reading.} The rate the store imports from a calculation with a quantum of a spin-two field is, at this order, a process between matter and matter: it needs the operator source and uses no state of the gauge field, because the exchange is at $Q(k) = s > 0$, where by (i) the field has no freedom. It is the same whether or not the field has a free part at null momentum, which this rate does not probe; the standard calculation with a quantised field gives the same tree rate. Only the mean-field source changes it. So the correction of the stored coefficient in (vi) does not depend on (OS): it holds on the standard account as well. With (OS), the dark towers' gravitational heating stands with larger coefficients: the temperature floors, which go as $\alpha^{1/4}$, rise by a factor between $1.6$ and $1.8$, and the yields, which go as $\alpha$, by a factor between $7$ and $11$, on the stored brackets. With the mean-field source there is no floor at all. The scalar coefficients depend on the curvature couplings through $\tau$, the datum of the stored reading $\xi = 0$.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result synthesis.gravitational-pair-production-from-the-operator-source-at-first-order, version 2.

**Fact (imported) 107 (Commuting copies).** Inside $T_k$, let $B_k$ be the subalgebra generated by the $2k$ plane generators $e_1, f_1, \dots, e_k, f_k$, and let $A_k$ be the subalgebra generated by the twisted base generators $x^{(k)} := x\,\omega_1 \omega_2 \cdots \omega_k$ for $x$ among the ground generators. Then: $B_k \cong \Cl(k,k) \cong \Mat_{2^k}(\R)$; $A_k \cong \Cl(p,q)$ via $x \mapsto x^{(k)}$ (the twisted generators satisfy the ground Clifford relations); $A_k$ and $B_k$ commute elementwise; and multiplication $A_k \otimes B_k \to T_k$ is an isomorphism of algebras,
\[
\Cl(p{+}k, q{+}k) \;\cong\; \Cl(p,q) \otimes \Mat_{2^k}(\R) \;=\; \Mat_{2^k}(\Cl(p,q)).
\]
The isomorphism is canonical given the ordered planes, and compatible with the tower embeddings up to the twist tracked in the section ``The CAR limit''.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result tower.commuting-copies, version 5 (T03 Thm 3.2).

**Fact (imported) 108 (Fock module; one qubit per rung).** Let $W_k := \operatorname{span}\{v_1, \dots, v_k\}$, a maximal isotropic subspace of $H_1 \perp \cdots \perp H_k$. The Chevalley action ($v_j$ by wedge, $w_j$ by contraction against the pairing) makes the exterior algebra $\Lambda^\bullet W_k$, of dimension $2^k$, an irreducible module over $B_k \cong \Mat_{2^k}(\R)$, with the vacuum $1 \in \Lambda^0$ annihilated by every $w_j$ and cyclic for the $v_j$. Each rung doubles the module: $\Lambda^\bullet W_{k+1} = \Lambda^\bullet W_k \otimes \Lambda^\bullet \R v_{k+1}$, one qubit per rung.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result tower.fock-module-one-qubit-per-rung, version 6 (T03 Thm 4.2).

**Fact (imported) 109 (Rigidity; the clock is frozen).** Along the tower: the transport charge $d = p - q \bmod 8$ is constant; the division-ring type, simplicity class, and centre of every floor equal those of the ground floor; every floor is Morita equivalent to the ground floor; and the obstruction taxonomy of \cite{ButlerT1} is floor-independent: in particular a type-C obstruction (a central idempotent) never arises by transport from a simple ground algebra, at any height. The physical tower is $\Cl(1{+}k, 3{+}k) \cong \Mat_{2^{k+1}}(\HH)$, frozen at clock position $6$. Bott periodicity manifests as immediate freeze, not eventual return.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result tower.rigidity-clock-frozen, version 5 (T03 Thm 3.6).

**Fact (imported) 110 (Witt index is quantum capacity).** For any nondegenerate real quadratic space $(V, Q)$, the maximal number of pairwise-anticommuting fermionic mode pairs realisable by \emph{vectors} of $\Cl(V, Q)$ (families $(a_i, b_i)$ of vectors satisfying the displayed CAR of Proposition \claimref{tower.ladder-pairs-satisfy-car-vectors}) is exactly the Witt index $\min(p,q)$ of $Q$. Along the tower the Witt index is $\min(p,q) + k$: each rung raises the algebra's mode capacity by exactly one, and by no more.

*Provenance:* Physics Graph result tower.witt-index-quantum-capacity, version 5 (T03 Thm 4.3).

**Refutation 111 (A pre-descent $\theta$-term sources the matter asymmetry).** The refuted claim is that a $\theta$-term present before the descent sources the matter asymmetry through the topology of gauge bundles. A $\theta$-term integrates a characteristic class of even degree, so the odd-dimensional towers carry no continuous $\theta$. In this tower a non-relaxing angle of order one at the descent is excluded by the neutron electric dipole bound, which gives $|\theta| \lesssim 10^{-10}$ today; an angle that relaxes or rolls is not excluded. The framework instead inherits its asymmetry as initial data, the Witt flag being the one mechanism it supplies itself.

**Refutation 112 (The particle-antiparticle orientation is determined by the charge-zero condition).** The refuted expectation is that the charge-zero condition fixes the one absolute bit the Standard-Model derivation left open, the particle-antiparticle orientation. A $\Z_{2}$ covariance argument on three premises (CPT conjugation is an isomorphism of every kinematic structure and leaves every action invariant, Hypothesis (S), and the premise that the flag is a datum of each solution; no exhaustiveness of the trichotomy is used) shows that whatever the structures determine is conjugation-invariant, while the orientation is not. The charge-zero condition fixes all relative structure; the absolute bit is initial data, and it is the Witt flag, the same choice as the arrow of time.

**Refutation 113 (The vacuum omega-area sets the weak scale).** Refuted. The corrected statement: It is wrong by between forty-four and one hundred and five orders of magnitude; the weak scale falls back to the weight question, and the vacuum alignment instead orders the families: democracy, rank one, one heavy.

**Refutation 114 (Conformal spinors are the ladder operators).** Conformal spinors (twistors) are the ladder operators

**Prediction 115 (Dark energy cannot evolve: w = -1).** Dark energy cannot evolve: $w = -1$ exactly, at every epoch; registered with its one condition, that on matter-filled regions the gravitational connection, the $\Cl(2,4)$ connection that preserves the frozen point $I$, keeps it parallel in the physical scale, $\ip{X}{I} = 1$ (Proposition \claimref{dark-energy.vacuum-region-registration-condition}(iii)); in that form the condition is the field-space premise (FS) of the gravitational action (Premise \claimref{synthesis.field-space-premise-parallel-frozen-point}), which the store adopts.

\emph{Note (deflation).} On the reduced field space of (FS) the norm $Q(I) = \Lambda/3$ is constant, so $\Lambda$ is a constant parameter and $w = -1$ is the $\Lambda$CDM equation of state. A confirmed $w = -1$ therefore supports $\Lambda$CDM with a frozen point but is not evidence for (FS) independent of $\Lambda$CDM; a confirmed $w \neq -1$ would refute (FS), and $\Lambda$CDM with it (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.parallel-frozen-point-on-matter-regions-is-the-field-space-premise}, Reading).

\emph{Note (the registered region).} In the two-parameter form $w(a) = w_{0} + w_{a}(1 - a)$ the prediction is the single point $(w_{0}, w_{a}) = (-1, 0)$. The series counts it refuted when two independent surveys each exclude that point at five standard deviations or more. Two surveys are independent when their late-time data (galaxy, supernova and lensing samples) are disjoint; each may use the shared CMB prior. The significance is the collaboration's published frequentist distance of the point (the two-parameter $\Delta\chi^{2}$ converted to standard deviations); the Bayesian evidence is recorded beside it and does not decide. The wording ``at any epoch'' stays as the model-independent reading: an exclusion of constant dark-energy density at some epoch, at the same significance and by two independent surveys, in an analysis not tied to the two-parameter form, counts the same. The rule is conservative: under it the series counts $w = -1$ as standing through releases in which a combined analysis reports a preference for evolution at three or four standard deviations, and it says so here.

\emph{Note (no fallback; argued).} A search of the store, not a proof, finds no adopted structure that permits a dark-energy equation of state different from $-1$ on an accelerating solution, so the series has no fallback for $w \neq -1$. Of the two stored ways of making the norm a variable, the promoted norm keeps $w = -1$ on every solution, and the norm released locally gives, under hypothesis (Mat), for the bare functional on isotropic fields with flat slices, a cosmological term that tracks one fluid with deceleration, no solution with radiation and no prediction in vacuum (Theorem \claimref{synthesis.norm-released-locally-on-isotropic-fields-tracks-dust-and-never-accelerates}(iv)-(vii), within its scope). The same search finds one stored structure with a late-time equation of state different from $-1$: the branch of Theorem \claimref{synthesis.unit-ball-clauses-hold-at-one-epoch-unless-hidden-direction-co-expands}(ii) with R3 standing and a static hidden direction, in which a fraction between $0.316$ and $0.317$ of the dark matter dilutes with effective $w = -\tfrac14$. It is not a fallback: the branch is not adopted; it is a dark-matter component and not a variable norm, with no pressure derived; read with the constant as one dark energy it has $w > -1$ at every epoch and approaches $-1$ from above, so $w_{a} > 0$ and no crossing of $-1$, against the quadrant $w_{0} > -1$, $w_{a} < 0$ of the survey fits in the observations below; and its share grows by the factor between $189$ and $190$ from $1 + z = 1090$ stated there, so the dark-matter densities inferred at that epoch and today would disagree (not confronted with data here). The exponent of the hidden direction in that branch is therefore not a parameter available to absorb a detection, and adopting it after one would be a fit. A detection by the registered rule would refute (FS) and the reading of dark energy as the frozen point's norm, and the series would need a new premise.
